Proof of
Proposition

1

Let
b^{\prime}\in[b-1]
,
p^{\prime}=\sum_{i=b^{\prime}+1}^{b}d_{i}
and consider

\displaystyle\mathbf{B}_{b^{\prime}}^{\top}=[\mathbf{A}^{\top}_{b^{\prime}+1}/\rho_{b^{\prime}+1}^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\cdots\mathbf{A}^{\top}_{b}/\rho_{b}^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}]\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times p^{\prime}}\;,

\displaystyle\bar{\mathbf{B}}_{b^{\prime}}=\mathbf{B}_{b^{\prime}}^{\top}\mathbf{B}_{b^{\prime}}=\sum_{i=b^{\prime}+1}^{b}\{\mathbf{A}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{A}_{i}/\rho_{i}\}\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}\;.

(S2)

Note that under
1
,
\bar{\mathbf{B}}_{b^{\prime}}
is invertible. Indeed, it is a symmetric positive definite matrix since
for any
\boldsymbol{\theta}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,
\left\langle\bar{\mathbf{B}}_{b^{\prime}}\boldsymbol{\theta},\boldsymbol{\theta}\right\rangle\geq[\min_{i\in[b]}\rho_{i}^{-1}]\langle\sum^{b}_{i=b^{\prime}+1}\mathbf{A}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{A}_{i}\boldsymbol{\theta},\boldsymbol{\theta}\rangle>0
using that
\sum^{b}_{i=b^{\prime}+1}\mathbf{A}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{A}_{i}
is invertible.
Define the orthogonal projection onto the range of
\mathbf{B}_{b^{\prime}}
and the diagonal matrix:

\mathbf{P}_{b^{\prime}}=\mathbf{B}_{b^{\prime}}\bar{\mathbf{B}}_{b^{\prime}}^{-1}\mathbf{B}_{b^{\prime}}^{\top}\;,\qquad\mathbf{\tilde{D}}_{b^{\prime}}=\mathrm{diag}(\mathbf{I}_{d_{b^{\prime}+1}}/\rho_{b^{\prime}+1},\ldots,\mathbf{I}_{d_{b}}/\rho_{b})\;.

(S3)

S1.1
Technical lemma

Lemma S1
.

Assume
1
. For any
(\boldsymbol{\theta},\mathbf{z}_{b^{\prime}+1\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}b})\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{p^{\prime}}
, setting
\mathbf{z}=\mathbf{z}_{b^{\prime}+1\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}b}
, we have

\sum_{i=b^{\prime}+1}^{b}\left\{{\|\mathbf{z}_{i}-\mathbf{A}_{i}\boldsymbol{\theta}\|^{2}/\rho_{i}}\right\}=(\mathbf{\tilde{D}}_{b^{\prime}}^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\mathbf{z})^{\top}\{\mathbf{I}_{p^{\prime}}-\mathbf{P}_{b^{\prime}}\}(\mathbf{\tilde{D}}_{b^{\prime}}^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\mathbf{z})\\
+(\boldsymbol{\theta}-\bar{\mathbf{B}}_{b^{\prime}}^{-1}\mathbf{B}_{b^{\prime}}^{\top}\mathbf{\tilde{D}}_{b^{\prime}}^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\mathbf{z})^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{B}}_{b^{\prime}}(\boldsymbol{\theta}-\bar{\mathbf{B}}_{b^{\prime}}^{-1}\mathbf{B}_{b^{\prime}}^{\top}\mathbf{\tilde{D}}_{b^{\prime}}^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\mathbf{z})\;.

Proof.

Setting
\mathbf{b}=\mathbf{B}_{b^{\prime}}^{\top}\mathbf{\tilde{D}}_{b^{\prime}}^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\mathbf{z}
and using the fact that
\bar{\mathbf{B}}_{b^{\prime}}
is symmetric, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{i=b^{\prime}+1}^{b}\left\{{\|\mathbf{z}_{i}-\mathbf{A}_{i}\boldsymbol{\theta}\|^{2}/\rho_{i}}\right\}

\displaystyle=\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{B}}_{b^{\prime}}\boldsymbol{\theta}-2\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\top}\mathbf{b}+\sum_{i=b^{\prime}+1}^{b}\mathinner{\!\left\lVert\mathbf{z}_{i}\right\rVert}^{2}/\rho_{i}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=b^{\prime}+1}^{b}\mathinner{\!\left\lVert\mathbf{z}_{i}\right\rVert}^{2}/\rho_{i}-\mathbf{b}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{B}}_{b^{\prime}}^{-1}\mathbf{b}+(\boldsymbol{\theta}-\bar{\mathbf{B}}_{b^{\prime}}^{-1}\mathbf{b})^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{B}}_{b^{\prime}}(\boldsymbol{\theta}-\bar{\mathbf{B}}_{b^{\prime}}^{-1}\mathbf{b})\;.

Using that
\mathbf{b}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{B}}_{b^{\prime}}^{-1}\mathbf{b}=(\mathbf{\tilde{D}}_{b^{\prime}}^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\mathbf{z})^{\top}\mathbf{P}_{b^{\prime}}(\mathbf{\tilde{D}}_{b^{\prime}}^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\mathbf{z})
and
\mathbf{P}_{b^{\prime}}
is a projection,
\mathbf{P}_{b^{\prime}}^{2}=\mathbf{P}_{b^{\prime}}
completes the proof.
∎

S1.2
Proof of
Proposition

1

Proposition S2
.

Assume
1
. Then, the function
\psi\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}(\boldsymbol{\theta},\mathbf{z}_{1\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}b})\mapsto\prod_{i=1}^{b}\exp\{-U_{i}(\mathbf{z}_{i})-\|\mathbf{z}_{i}-\mathbf{A}_{i}\boldsymbol{\theta}\|^{2}/(2\rho_{i})\}
is integrable on
\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{p}
, where
p=\sum_{i=1}^{b}d_{i}
.

Proof.

Using
1
and the Fubini theorem, there exists
C_{1}>0
such that:

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left[{\prod_{i=1}^{b^{\prime}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{i}}}\mathrm{e}^{-U_{i}(\mathbf{z}_{i})}\mathrm{e}^{-\frac{\mathinner{\!\left\lVert\mathbf{z}_{i}-\mathbf{A}_{i}\boldsymbol{\theta}\right\rVert}^{2}}{2\rho_{i}}}\,\mathrm{d}\mathbf{z}_{i}\cdot\prod_{j=b^{\prime}+1}^{b}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{j}}}\mathrm{e}^{-U_{j}(\mathbf{z}_{j})}\mathrm{e}^{-\frac{\mathinner{\!\left\lVert\mathbf{z}_{j}-\mathbf{A}_{j}\boldsymbol{\theta}\right\rVert}^{2}}{2\rho_{j}}}\,\mathrm{d}\mathbf{z}_{j}}\right]\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{\theta}

\displaystyle\leq C_{1}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left[{\prod_{i=1}^{b^{\prime}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{i}}}\mathrm{e}^{-\frac{\mathinner{\!\left\lVert\mathbf{z}_{i}-\mathbf{A}_{i}\boldsymbol{\theta}\right\rVert}^{2}}{2\rho_{i}}}\,\mathrm{d}\mathbf{z}_{i}\cdot\prod_{j=b^{\prime}+1}^{b}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{j}}}\mathrm{e}^{-U_{j}(\mathbf{z}_{j})}\mathrm{e}^{-\frac{\mathinner{\!\left\lVert\mathbf{z}_{j}-\mathbf{A}_{j}\boldsymbol{\theta}\right\rVert}^{2}}{2\rho_{j}}}\,\mathrm{d}\mathbf{z}_{j}}\right]\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{\theta}

\displaystyle\leq C_{1}\prod_{i=1}^{b^{\prime}}(2\uppi\rho_{i})^{d_{i}/2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left[{\prod_{j=b^{\prime}+1}^{b}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{j}}}\mathrm{e}^{-U_{j}(\mathbf{z}_{j})}\exp\left({-\mathinner{\!\left\lVert\mathbf{z}_{j}-\mathbf{A}_{j}\boldsymbol{\theta}\right\rVert}^{2}/(2\rho_{j})}\right)\,\mathrm{d}\mathbf{z}_{j}}\right]\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{\theta}

\displaystyle=C_{1}\prod_{i=1}^{b^{\prime}}(2\uppi\rho_{i})^{d_{i}/2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{b^{\prime}+1}}}\cdots\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{b}}}\left[{\prod_{j=b^{\prime}+1}^{b}\mathrm{e}^{-U_{j}(\mathbf{z}_{j})}}\right]\left[{\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\prod_{j=b^{\prime}+1}^{b}\mathrm{e}^{-\frac{\mathinner{\!\left\lVert\mathbf{z}_{j}-\mathbf{A}_{j}\boldsymbol{\theta}\right\rVert}^{2}}{2\rho_{j}}}\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{\theta}}\right]\,\mathrm{d}\mathbf{z}_{b^{\prime}+1\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}b}\;.

(S4)

Using
Lemma

S1
and the fact that
\mathbf{I}_{p^{\prime}}-\mathbf{P}_{b^{\prime}}
is positive definite, we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\prod_{j=b^{\prime}+1}^{b}\exp\left({-\mathinner{\!\left\lVert\mathbf{z}_{j}-\mathbf{A}_{j}\boldsymbol{\theta}\right\rVert}^{2}/(2\rho_{j})}\right)\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{\theta}

\displaystyle=\exp\left({-(\mathbf{\tilde{D}}_{b^{\prime}}^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\mathbf{z})^{\top}\{\mathbf{I}_{p^{\prime}}-\mathbf{P}_{b^{\prime}}\}(\mathbf{\tilde{D}}_{b^{\prime}}^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\mathbf{z})/2}\right)

\displaystyle\times\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\exp\left({-(\boldsymbol{\theta}-\bar{\mathbf{B}}_{b^{\prime}}^{-1}\mathbf{B}_{b^{\prime}}^{\top}\mathbf{\tilde{D}}_{b^{\prime}}^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\mathbf{z})^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{B}}_{b^{\prime}}(\boldsymbol{\theta}-\bar{\mathbf{B}}_{b^{\prime}}^{-1}\mathbf{B}_{b^{\prime}}^{\top}\mathbf{\tilde{D}}_{b^{\prime}}^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\mathbf{z})/2}\right)\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{\theta}

\displaystyle\leq\mathrm{det}\left({\bar{\mathbf{B}}_{b^{\prime}}}\right)^{-1/2}(2\uppi)^{d/2}.

Then, the proof is completed by plugging this expression into (
S4
) and using from
1
that
\mathbf{z}_{b^{\prime}+1\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}b}\mapsto\prod_{j=b^{\prime}+1}^{b}\mathrm{e}^{-U_{j}(\mathbf{z}_{j})}
is integrable.
∎

Appendix S2
Proof of
Proposition

2

This section aims at proving
Proposition

2
in the main paper.
To ease the understanding, we dissociate the scenarios where
\max_{i\in[b]}N_{i}=1
and
\max_{i\in[b]}N_{i}>1
. In addition, in all this section
\boldsymbol{\rho}\in(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{*})^{b}
is assumed to be fixed.

S2.1
Single local LMC iteration

In this section, we assume that a single LMC step is performed locally on each worker, that is
\max_{i\in[b]}N_{i}=1
. For this, we introduce the conditional Markov transition kernel defined for any
\boldsymbol{\gamma}=(\gamma_{1},\ldots,\gamma_{b})
,
\boldsymbol{\theta}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,
\mathbf{z}=(\mathbf{z}_{1},\cdots,\mathbf{z}_{b})\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\cdots\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{b}}
, and for
i\in[b]
,
\mathsf{B}_{i}\in\mathcal{B}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{i}})
, by

\displaystyle Q_{\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{\gamma}}\left({\mathbf{z},\mathsf{B}_{1}\times\cdots\times\mathsf{B}_{b}|\boldsymbol{\theta}}\right)=\prod_{i=1}^{b}R_{\rho_{i},\gamma_{i}}(\mathbf{z}_{i},\mathsf{B}_{i}|\boldsymbol{\theta})\;,

(S5)

where

R_{\rho_{i},\gamma_{i}}(\mathbf{z}_{i},\mathsf{B}_{i}|\boldsymbol{\theta})=\int_{\mathsf{B}_{i}}\exp\left\{{-\frac{1}{4\gamma_{i}}\mathinner{\!\left\lVert\tilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i}-\left({1-\frac{\gamma_{i}}{\rho_{i}}}\right)\mathbf{z}_{i}-\frac{\gamma_{i}}{\rho_{i}}\mathbf{A}_{i}\boldsymbol{\theta}+\gamma_{i}\nabla U_{i}(\mathbf{z}_{i})\right\rVert}^{2}}\right\}\frac{\mathrm{d}\tilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i}}{(4\uppi\gamma_{i})^{d_{i}/2}}\;.

(S6)

Recall that
p=\sum_{i=1}^{b}d_{i}
.
The considered Gibbs sampler in
Algorithm

1
defines a homogeneous Markov chain
X_{n}^{\top}=(\theta_{n}^{\top},Z_{n}^{\top})_{n\geq 1}
where
Z_{n}^{\top}=([Z_{n}^{1}]^{\top},\cdots,[Z_{n}^{b}]^{\top})
. Indeed, it is easy to show that for any
n\in\mathbb{N}
and measurable bounded function
f\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}\mathbb{R}^{p}\to\mathbb{R}_{+}
,
\mathbb{E}[f(Z_{n+1})|X_{n}]=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{p}}f(\mathbf{z})Q_{\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{\gamma}}(Z_{n},\mathrm{d}\mathbf{z}|\theta_{n})
and therefore
(X_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
is associated with the Markov kernel defined, for any
\mathbf{x}^{\top}=(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\top},\mathbf{z}^{\top})\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{p}
and
\mathsf{A}\in\mathcal{B}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
,
\mathsf{B}\in\mathcal{B}(\mathbb{R}^{p})
, by

P_{\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{\gamma}}(\mathbf{x},\mathsf{A}\times\mathsf{B})=\int_{\mathsf{B}}Q_{\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{\gamma}}\left({\mathbf{z},\mathrm{d}{\tilde{\mathbf{z}}}|\boldsymbol{\theta}}\right)\int_{\mathsf{A}}\Pi_{\boldsymbol{\rho}}(\mathrm{d}\tilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}|\tilde{\mathbf{z}})\;,

(S7)

where
\Pi_{\boldsymbol{\rho}}(\cdot|\tilde{\mathbf{z}})
is defined in (
5
).
Let
(\xi_{n})_{n\geq 1}
be a sequence of i.i.d.
d
-dimensional standard Gaussian random variables independent of the family of independent random variables
\{(\eta_{n}^{i})_{n\geq 1}\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}i\in[b]\}
where for any
i\in[b]
and
n\geq 1
,
\eta_{n}^{i}
is a
d_{i}
-dimensional standard Gaussian random variable.
We define the stochastic processes
(X_{n},\tilde{X}_{n})_{n\geq 0}
on
\mathbb{R}^{p}\times\mathbb{R}^{p}
starting from
(X_{0},\tilde{X}_{0})=(\mathbf{x},\tilde{\mathbf{x}})=((\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\top},\mathbf{z}^{\top})^{\top},(\tilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{\top},\tilde{\mathbf{z}}^{\top})^{\top})
and following the recursion for
n\geq 0
,

X_{n+1}=(\theta_{n+1}^{\top},Z_{n+1}^{\top})^{\top}\;,\qquad\tilde{X}_{n+1}=(\tilde{\theta}_{n+1}^{\top},\tilde{Z}_{n+1}^{\top})^{\top}\;,

(S8)

where
Z_{n+1}=([Z_{n+1}^{1}]^{\top},\ldots,[Z_{n+1}^{b}]^{\top})^{\top},\tilde{Z}_{n+1}=([\tilde{Z}_{n+1}^{1}]^{\top},\ldots,[\tilde{Z}_{n+1}^{b}]^{\top})^{\top}
are defined, for any
i\in[b]
, by

\displaystyle Z_{n+1}^{i}

\displaystyle=\left({1-\gamma_{i}/\rho_{i}}\right)Z_{n}^{i}+\left({\gamma_{i}/\rho_{i}}\right)\mathbf{A}_{i}\theta_{n}-\gamma_{i}\nabla U_{i}(Z_{n}^{i})+\sqrt{2\gamma_{i}}\eta_{n+1}^{i}\;,

(S9)

\displaystyle\tilde{Z}_{n+1}^{i}

\displaystyle=\left({1-\gamma_{i}/\rho_{i}}\right)\tilde{Z}_{n}^{i}+\left({\gamma_{i}/\rho_{i}}\right)\mathbf{A}_{i}\tilde{\theta}_{n}-\gamma_{i}\nabla U_{i}(\tilde{Z}_{n}^{i})+\sqrt{2\gamma_{i}}\eta_{n+1}^{i}\;,

and
\theta_{n+1},\tilde{\theta}_{n+1}
by

\theta_{n+1}=\bar{\mathbf{B}}_{0}^{-1}\mathbf{B}_{0}^{\top}\mathbf{\tilde{D}}_{0}^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}Z_{n+1}+\bar{\mathbf{B}}_{0}^{-{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\xi_{n+1}\;,\qquad\tilde{\theta}_{n+1}=\bar{\mathbf{B}}_{0}^{-1}\mathbf{B}_{0}^{\top}\mathbf{\tilde{D}}_{0}^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\tilde{Z}_{n+1}+\bar{\mathbf{B}}_{0}^{-{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\xi_{n+1}\;,

(S10)

where
\bar{\mathbf{B}}_{0}
,
\mathbf{B}_{0}
and
\mathbf{\tilde{D}}_{0}
are given in (
S2
) and (
S3
), respectively.
Note that
X_{n}
and
\tilde{X}_{n}
are distributed according to
\updelta_{\mathbf{x}}P_{\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{n}
and
\updelta_{\tilde{\mathbf{x}}}P_{\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{n}
, respectively.
Hence, by definition of the Wasserstein distance of order 2, it follows that

W_{2}(\updelta_{\mathbf{x}}P_{\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{n},\updelta_{\tilde{\mathbf{x}}}P_{\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{n})\leq\mathbb{E}\left[{\|X_{n}-\tilde{X}_{n}\|^{2}}\right]^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}.

(S11)

Thus, in this section we focus on upper bounding the squared norm
\|X_{n}-\tilde{X}_{n}\|
from which we get an explicit bound on the Wasserstein distance thanks to the previous inequality.

S2.1.1
Supporting lemmata

Note that
1
implies the invertibility of the matrix
\mathbf{B}_{0}
defined in (
S2
) since we have the existence of
b^{\prime}\in[b-1]
, such that
\sum_{i=b^{\prime}+1}^{b}\lambda_{\min}(\mathbf{A}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{A}_{i})/\rho_{i}>0
and by the semi-positiveness of the symmetric matrices
\{\mathbf{A}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{A}_{i}\}_{i\in[b]}
, we get that
\lambda_{\min}({\mathbf{B}_{0}})=\sum_{i=1}^{b}\lambda_{\min}({\mathbf{A}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{A}_{i}})/\rho_{i}\geq\sum_{i=b^{\prime}+1}^{b}\lambda_{\min}(\mathbf{A}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{A}_{i})/\rho_{i}
.
To prove
Proposition

2
in the case
\max_{i\in[b]}N_{i}=1
, we first upper bound (
S83
) by building upon the following two technical lemmas.

Lemma S3
.

Assume
1
and consider
(X_{n},\tilde{X}_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
defined in (
S8
). Then, for any
n\in\mathbb{N}
, it holds almost surely that

\|X_{n+1}-\tilde{X}_{n+1}\|^{2}\leq(1+\|\bar{\mathbf{B}}_{0}^{-1}\mathbf{B}_{0}^{\top}\mathbf{\tilde{D}}_{0}^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\|^{2})\|Z_{n+1}-\tilde{Z}_{n+1}\|^{2}\;.

Proof.

Let
n\geq 0
. By (
S10
), we have
\theta_{n+1}-\tilde{\theta}_{n+1}=\bar{\mathbf{B}}_{0}^{-1}\mathbf{B}_{0}^{\top}\mathbf{\tilde{D}}_{0}^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}(Z_{n+1}-\tilde{Z}_{n+1})
which implies that

\|X_{n+1}-\tilde{X}_{n+1}\|^{2}=\|\theta_{n+1}-\tilde{\theta}_{n+1}\|^{2}+\|Z_{n+1}-\tilde{Z}_{n+1}\|^{2}\leq(1+\|\bar{\mathbf{B}}_{0}^{-1}\mathbf{B}_{0}^{\top}\mathbf{\tilde{D}}_{0}^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\|^{2})\|Z_{n+1}-\tilde{Z}_{n+1}\|^{2}\;.

∎

Define the contraction factor

\kappa_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}\textstyle=\max_{i\in[b]}\left\{{|1-\gamma_{i}m_{i}|\vee|1-\gamma_{i}(M_{i}+1/\rho_{i})|}\right\}\;.

(S12)

Then, the following result holds.

Lemma S4
.

Assume
1
-
2
and let
\boldsymbol{\gamma}\in(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{*})^{b}
.
Then for any
\mathbf{x}=(\mathbf{z}^{\top},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\top})^{\top},\tilde{\mathbf{x}}=(\tilde{\mathbf{z}}^{\top},\tilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{\top})^{\top}
, with
(\boldsymbol{\theta},\tilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}})\in(\mathbb{R}^{d})^{2}
and
(\mathbf{z},\tilde{\mathbf{z}})\in(\mathbb{R}^{p})^{2}
, for any
n\geq 1
, we have

W_{2}(\updelta_{\mathbf{x}}P_{\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{n},\updelta_{\tilde{\mathbf{x}}}P_{\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{n})\leq\kappa_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{n-1}\cdot\bigg({(1+\|\bar{\mathbf{B}}_{0}^{-1}\mathbf{B}_{0}^{\top}\mathbf{\tilde{D}}_{0}^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\|^{2})\cdot\frac{\max_{i\in[b]}\{\gamma_{i}\}}{\min_{i\in[b]}\{\gamma_{i}\}}}\bigg)^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\\
\times\left[{\kappa_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}\|\mathbf{z}-\tilde{\mathbf{z}}\|+\|\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}/\sqrt{\boldsymbol{\rho}}}\mathbf{B}_{0}\|\|\boldsymbol{\theta}-\tilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}\|}\right]\;,

where
\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}/\sqrt{\boldsymbol{\rho}}}
is defined as in (
S1
) with
\boldsymbol{\gamma}/\sqrt{\boldsymbol{\rho}}=(\gamma_{1}/\rho_{1}^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}},\ldots,\gamma_{b}/\rho_{b}^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}})
,
\bar{\mathbf{B}}_{0}
,
\mathbf{B}_{0}
,
P_{\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{\gamma}}
and
\kappa_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}
are given in (
S2
), (
S7
), (
S12
), respectively.

Proof.

Consider
(X_{k},\tilde{X}_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
defined in (
S8
). By (
S83
) and
Lemma

S3
, we need to bound
(\|Z_{k}-\tilde{Z}_{k}\|)_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
.
Let
n\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
.
For any
i\in[b]
, we have by (
S9
), that

\displaystyle Z_{n+1}^{i}-\tilde{Z}_{n+1}^{i}

\displaystyle=\Big({1-\frac{\gamma_{i}}{\rho_{i}}}\Big)(Z_{n}^{i}-\tilde{Z}_{n}^{i})+\frac{\gamma_{i}}{\rho_{i}}\mathbf{A}_{i}(\theta_{n}-\tilde{\theta}_{n})-\gamma_{i}\left({\nabla U_{i}(Z_{n}^{i})-\nabla U_{i}(\tilde{Z}_{n}^{i})}\right)\;.

(S13)

Since
U_{i}
is twice differentiable, we have

\nabla U_{i}(Z_{n}^{i})-\nabla U_{i}(\tilde{Z}_{n}^{i})=\int_{0}^{1}\nabla^{2}U_{i}(\tilde{Z}_{n}^{i}+t(Z_{n}^{i}-\tilde{Z}_{n}^{i}))\,\mathrm{d}t\cdot(Z_{n}^{i}-\tilde{Z}_{n}^{i})\;.

Using
\theta_{n}-\tilde{\theta}_{n}=\bar{\mathbf{B}}_{0}^{-1}\mathbf{B}_{0}^{\top}\mathbf{\tilde{D}}_{0}^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}(Z_{n}-\tilde{Z}_{n})
, it follows that

Z_{n+1}^{i}-\tilde{Z}_{n+1}^{i}=\left({\Big[1-\frac{\gamma_{i}}{\rho_{i}}\Big]\mathbf{I}_{d_{i}}-\gamma_{i}\int_{0}^{1}\nabla^{2}U_{i}(\tilde{Z}_{n}^{i}+t(Z_{n}^{i}-\tilde{Z}_{n}^{i}))\,\mathrm{d}t}\right)(Z_{n}^{i}-\tilde{Z}_{n}^{i})\\
+\frac{\gamma_{i}}{\rho_{i}}\mathbf{A}_{i}\bar{\mathbf{B}}_{0}^{-1}\mathbf{B}_{0}^{\top}\mathbf{\tilde{D}}_{0}^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}(Z_{n}-\tilde{Z}_{n})\;.

Consider the
p\times p
block diagonal matrix defined by

\displaystyle\mathbf{D}_{U,n}=\mathrm{diag}\left({\gamma_{1}\int_{0}^{1}\nabla^{2}U_{1}(\tilde{Z}_{n}^{1}+t(Z_{n}^{1}-\tilde{Z}_{n}^{1}))\,\mathrm{d}t,\cdots,\gamma_{b}\int_{0}^{1}\nabla^{2}U_{b}(\tilde{Z}_{n}^{b}+t(Z_{n}^{b}-\tilde{Z}_{n}^{b}))\,\mathrm{d}t}\right)\;.

With the projection matrix
\mathbf{P}_{0}
defined in (
S3
), the difference
Z_{n+1}-\tilde{Z}_{n+1}
can be rewritten as

\displaystyle Z_{n+1}-\tilde{Z}_{n+1}

\displaystyle=\left({\mathbf{I}_{p}-\mathbf{D}_{U,n}-\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}/\boldsymbol{\rho}}^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}(\mathbf{I}_{p}-\mathbf{P}_{0})\mathbf{\tilde{D}}_{0}^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}}\right)(Z_{n}-\tilde{Z}_{n})\;,

where
\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}/\boldsymbol{\rho}}
is defined as in (
S1
) with
\boldsymbol{\gamma}/\boldsymbol{\rho}=(\gamma_{1}/\rho_{1},\ldots,\gamma_{b}/\rho_{b})
.
Since
\mathbf{D}_{U,n}
commutes with
\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}
and
\mathbf{P}_{0}
is an orthogonal projection matrix, using
2
-
(i)
-
(ii)
, we get

\displaystyle\|Z_{n+1}-\tilde{Z}_{n+1}\|_{\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{-1}}

\displaystyle=\|\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{-{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}(\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{-{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}-\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\mathbf{D}_{U,n}\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{-{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}-\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}/\boldsymbol{\rho}}^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}(\mathbf{I}_{p}-\mathbf{P}_{0})\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}/\boldsymbol{\rho}}^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{-{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}})(Z_{n}-\tilde{Z}_{n})\|

\displaystyle\leq\|\mathbf{I}_{p}-\mathbf{D}_{U,n}-\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}/\boldsymbol{\rho}}^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\left({\mathbf{I}_{p}-\mathbf{P}_{0}}\right)\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}/\boldsymbol{\rho}}^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\|\|Z_{n}-\tilde{Z}_{n}\|_{\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{-1}}\;.

Note that
1
and
2
and the fact that
\mathbf{P}_{0}
is an orthogonal projector, so
\mathbf{0}_{p}\preccurlyeq\mathbf{I}_{p}-\mathbf{P}_{0}
, imply that

\mathrm{diag}(\{1-\gamma_{1}(M_{1}+1/\rho_{1})\}\mathbf{I}_{d_{1}},\cdots,\{1-\gamma_{b}(M_{b}+1/\rho_{b})\}\mathbf{I}_{d_{b}})\preccurlyeq\mathbf{I}_{p}-\mathbf{D}_{U,n}-\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}/\boldsymbol{\rho}}^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\left({\mathbf{I}_{p}-\mathbf{P}_{0}}\right)\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}/\boldsymbol{\rho}}^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\\
\preccurlyeq\mathrm{diag}\left({\left\{1-\gamma_{1}m_{1}\right\}\mathbf{I}_{d_{1}},\ldots,\left\{1-\gamma_{b}m_{b}\right\}\mathbf{I}_{d_{b}}}\right)\;.

Therefore, we get

\displaystyle\|Z_{n+1}-\tilde{Z}_{n+1}\|_{\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{-1}}

\displaystyle\leq\max_{i\in[b]}\left\{{\max(|1-\gamma_{i}m_{i}|,|1-\gamma_{i}(M_{i}+1/\rho_{i})|)}\right\}\|Z_{n}-\tilde{Z}_{n}\|_{\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{-1}}

\displaystyle=\kappa_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}\|Z_{n}-\tilde{Z}_{n}\|_{\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{-1}}\;.

(S14)

An immediate induction shows, for any
n\geq 1
,

\|Z_{n}-\tilde{Z}_{n}\|_{\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{-1}}\leq\kappa_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{n-1}\|Z_{1}-\tilde{Z}_{1}\|_{\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{-1}}\;.

(S15)

In addition, by (
S13
), we have for any
i\in[b]
,

\displaystyle Z_{1}^{i}-\tilde{Z}_{1}^{i}

\displaystyle=\Big({1-\frac{\gamma_{i}}{\rho_{i}}}\Big)(\mathbf{z}_{i}-\tilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i})+\frac{\gamma_{i}}{\rho_{i}}\mathbf{A}_{i}(\boldsymbol{\theta}-\tilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}})-\gamma_{i}(\nabla U_{i}(\mathbf{z}_{i})-\nabla U_{i}(\tilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i}))\;.

It follows that
Z_{1}-\tilde{Z}_{1}=(\mathbf{I}_{p}-\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}/\boldsymbol{\rho}}-\mathbf{D}_{U,0})(\mathbf{z}-\tilde{\mathbf{z}})+\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}/\boldsymbol{\rho}}\mathbf{\tilde{D}}_{0}^{-{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\mathbf{B}_{0}(\boldsymbol{\theta}-\tilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}})
.
Using the triangle inequality and
2
gives

\displaystyle\|Z_{1}-\tilde{Z}_{1}\|_{\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{-1}}

\displaystyle\textstyle\leq(\min_{i\in[b]}\{\gamma_{i}\})^{-{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\|(\mathbf{I}_{p}-\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}/\boldsymbol{\rho}}-\mathbf{D}_{U,0})(\mathbf{z}-\tilde{\mathbf{z}})+(\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}/\sqrt{\boldsymbol{\rho}}}\mathbf{B}_{0}(\boldsymbol{\theta}-\tilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}})\|

\displaystyle\textstyle\leq(\min_{i\in[b]}\{\gamma_{i}\})^{-{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\Big[\|\mathbf{I}_{p}-\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}/\boldsymbol{\rho}}-\mathbf{D}_{U,0}\|\|\mathbf{z}-\tilde{\mathbf{z}}\|+\|\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}/\sqrt{\boldsymbol{\rho}}}\mathbf{B}_{0}\|\|\boldsymbol{\theta}-\tilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}\|\Big]

\displaystyle\textstyle\leq(\min_{i\in[b]}\{\gamma_{i}\})^{-{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\Big[\max_{i\in[b]}\{\textstyle|{1-\gamma_{i}(m_{i}+1/\rho_{i})}|,\textstyle|{1-\gamma_{i}(M_{i}+1/\rho_{i})}|\}\|\mathbf{z}-\tilde{\mathbf{z}}\|

\displaystyle\qquad+\|\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}/\sqrt{\boldsymbol{\rho}}}\mathbf{B}_{0}\|\|\boldsymbol{\theta}-\tilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}\|\Big]

\displaystyle\textstyle\leq(\min_{i\in[b]}\{\gamma_{i}\})^{-{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\Big[{\kappa_{\gamma}\|\mathbf{z}-\tilde{\mathbf{z}}\|+\|\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}/\sqrt{\boldsymbol{\rho}}}\mathbf{B}_{0}\|\|\boldsymbol{\theta}-\tilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}\|}\Big]\;.

Combining (
S15
) and the previous inequality and using
Lemma

S3
, we get for
n\geq 1
,

\|X_{n}-\tilde{X}_{n}\|^{2}\leq\kappa_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{2(n-1)}\left({1+\|\bar{\mathbf{B}}_{0}^{-1}\mathbf{B}_{0}^{\top}\mathbf{\tilde{D}}_{0}^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\|^{2}}\right)\frac{\max_{i\in[b]}\{\gamma_{i}\}}{\min_{i\in[b]}\{\gamma_{i}\}}\\
\times\Big[{\kappa_{\gamma}\|\mathbf{z}-\tilde{\mathbf{z}}\|+\|\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}/\sqrt{\boldsymbol{\rho}}}\mathbf{B}_{0}\|\|\boldsymbol{\theta}-\tilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}\|}\Big]^{2}\;.

The proof is concluded by (
S83
).
∎

S2.1.2
Specific case of

Based on the previous lemmata, we provide in what follows a specific instance of

in the scenario where

.

Proposition S5
.

Assume
1
-
2
and let

such that, for any
i\in[b]
,
\gamma_{i}\leq 2(m_{i}+M_{i}+1/\rho_{i})^{-1}
.
Then,

defined in (

) admits a unique stationary distribution
\Pi_{\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{\gamma}}
and for any

with

,
\mathbf{z}\in\mathbb{R}^{p}
and any

, we have

\displaystyle W_{2}^{2}(\updelta_{\mathbf{x}}P_{\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{n},\Pi_{\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{\gamma}})

\displaystyle\leq\big({1-\min_{i\in[b]}\{{\gamma_{i}m_{i}}\}}\big)^{2(n-1)}\bigg({(1+\|\bar{\mathbf{B}}_{0}^{-1}\mathbf{B}_{0}^{\top}\mathbf{\tilde{D}}_{0}^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\|^{2})\cdot\frac{\max_{i\in[b]}\{\gamma_{i}\}}{\min_{i\in[b]}\{\gamma_{i}\}}}\bigg)

\displaystyle\times\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{p}}\Big[({1-\min_{i\in[b]}\{{\gamma_{i}m_{i}}\}})\|\mathbf{z}-\tilde{\mathbf{z}}\|+\|\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}/\sqrt{\boldsymbol{\rho}}}\mathbf{B}_{0}\|\|\boldsymbol{\theta}-\tilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}\|\Big]^{2}\mathrm{d}\Pi_{\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{\gamma}}(\tilde{\mathbf{x}})\;,

where
\bar{\mathbf{B}}_{0},\mathbf{B}_{0},\mathbf{\tilde{D}}_{0}
,

are defined in (

) and (

).

Proof.

For any

, note that the condition

ensures that

and the proof follows from

combined with
Douc
et al. 2018
.
∎

S2.2
Multiple local LMC iterations

In this section, we consider the general case
\max_{i\in[b]}N_{i}\geq 1
. For this, we introduce the conditional Markov transition kernel defined for any
\boldsymbol{\gamma}=(\gamma_{1},\ldots,\gamma_{b})
,
\boldsymbol{N}=(N_{1},\ldots,N_{b})
,
\boldsymbol{\theta}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,
\mathbf{z}=(\mathbf{z}_{1},\cdots,\mathbf{z}_{b})\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\cdots\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{b}}
, for
i\in[b]
and
\mathsf{B}_{i}\in\mathcal{B}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{i}})
, by

Q_{\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{\gamma},\boldsymbol{N}}\left({\mathbf{z},\mathsf{B}_{1}\times\cdots\times\mathsf{B}_{b}|\boldsymbol{\theta}}\right)=\prod_{i=1}^{b}R_{\rho_{i},\gamma_{i}}^{N_{i}}(\mathbf{z}_{i},\mathsf{B}_{i}|\boldsymbol{\theta})\;,

(S16)

where
R_{\rho_{i},\gamma_{i}}
is defined by (
S6
).
Then, as in the case
\max_{i\in[b]}N_{i}=1
, the Gibbs sampler presented in
Algorithm

1
defines a homogeneous Markov chain
X_{n}^{\top}=(\theta_{n}^{\top},Z_{n}^{\top})_{n\geq 1}
where
Z_{n}^{\top}=([Z_{n}^{1}]^{\top},\cdots,[Z_{n}^{b}]^{\top})
. Indeed, it is easy to show that for any
n\in\mathbb{N}
and measurable function
f\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}\mathbb{R}^{p}\to\mathbb{R}_{+}
,
\mathbb{E}[f(Z_{n+1})|X_{n}]=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{p}}f(\mathbf{z})Q_{\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{\gamma},\boldsymbol{N}}(Z_{n},\mathrm{d}\mathbf{z}|\theta_{n})
.
Therefore,
(X_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
is associated with the Markov kernel defined, for any
\mathbf{x}^{\top}=(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\top},\mathbf{z}^{\top})
and
\mathsf{A}\in\mathcal{B}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
,
\mathsf{B}\in\mathcal{B}(\mathbb{R}^{p})
, by

P_{\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{\gamma},\boldsymbol{N}}(\mathbf{x},\mathsf{A}\times\mathsf{B})=\int_{\mathsf{B}}Q_{\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{\gamma},\boldsymbol{N}}\left({\mathbf{z},\mathrm{d}{\tilde{\mathbf{z}}}|\boldsymbol{\theta}}\right)\int_{\mathsf{A}}\Pi_{\boldsymbol{\rho}}(\mathrm{d}\tilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}|\tilde{\mathbf{z}})\;,

(S17)

where
\Pi_{\boldsymbol{\rho}}(\cdot|\tilde{\mathbf{z}})
is defined in (
5
).
We now define a coupling between
\updelta_{\mathbf{x}}P^{n}_{\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{\gamma},\boldsymbol{N}}
and
\updelta_{\mathbf{\tilde{x}}}P^{n}_{\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{\gamma},\boldsymbol{N}}
for any
n\geq 1
and
\mathbf{x},\mathbf{\tilde{x}}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{p}
.
Let
(\xi_{n})_{n\geq 1}
be a sequence of i.i.d.
d
-dimensional standard Gaussian random variables independent of the family of independent random variables
\{(\eta_{n}^{i})_{n\geq 1}\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}i\in[b]\}
where for any
i\in[b]
and
n\geq 1
,
\eta_{n}^{i}
is a
d_{i}
-dimensional standard Gaussian random variable.
Define by induction the synchronous coupling
(\theta_{n},Z_{n})_{n\geq 0},(\tilde{\theta}_{n},\tilde{Z}_{n})_{n\geq 0}
, for any
i\in[b]
starting from
(\theta_{0},Z_{0})=\mathbf{x}=(\boldsymbol{\theta},\mathbf{z})
,
(\tilde{\theta}_{0},\tilde{Z}_{0})=\tilde{\mathbf{x}}=(\tilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}},\tilde{\mathbf{z}})
and for any
n\geq 0
by

\displaystyle\tilde{Z}_{n+1}^{i}=\tilde{Y}_{N_{i}}^{(i,n)}\;,\qquad\tilde{\theta}_{n+1}=\bar{\mathbf{B}}_{0}^{-1}\mathbf{B}_{0}^{\top}\mathbf{\tilde{D}}_{0}^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\tilde{Z}_{n+1}+\bar{\mathbf{B}}_{0}^{-{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\xi_{n+1}\;,

(S18)

\displaystyle Z_{n+1}^{i}=Y_{N_{i}}^{(i,n)}\;,\qquad\theta_{n+1}=\bar{\mathbf{B}}_{0}^{-1}\mathbf{B}_{0}^{\top}\mathbf{\tilde{D}}_{0}^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}Z_{n+1}+\bar{\mathbf{B}}_{0}^{-{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\xi_{n+1}\;,

where
\bar{\mathbf{B}}_{0},\mathbf{B}_{0},\mathbf{\tilde{D}}_{0}
are given by (
S2
)-(
S3
) and
\tilde{Y}_{0}^{(i,n)}=\tilde{Z}_{n}^{i}
,
Y_{0}^{(i,n)}=Z_{n}^{i}
, and for any
k\in\mathbb{N}

\displaystyle\tilde{Y}_{k+1}^{(i,n)}=\tilde{Y}_{k}^{(i,n)}-\gamma_{i}\nabla V_{i}(\tilde{Y}_{k}^{(i,n)})+(\gamma_{i}/\rho_{i})\mathbf{A}_{i}\tilde{\theta}_{n}+\sqrt{2\gamma_{i}}\eta_{k+1}^{(i,n)}\;,

(S19)

\displaystyle Y_{k+1}^{(i,n)}=Y_{k}^{(i,n)}-\gamma_{i}\nabla V_{i}(Y_{k}^{(i,n)})+(\gamma_{i}/\rho_{i})\mathbf{A}_{i}\theta_{n}+\sqrt{2\gamma_{i}}\eta_{k+1}^{(i,n)}\;,

where, for any
\mathbf{z}_{i}\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{i}}
,
V_{i}
is defined by

V_{i}(\mathbf{z}_{i})=U_{i}(\mathbf{z}_{i})+(2\rho_{i})^{-1}\mathinner{\!\left\lVert\mathbf{z}_{i}\right\rVert}^{2}\;.

(S20)

For any
n,k\in\mathbb{N}
consider the
p\times p
matrices defined by

\displaystyle\mathbf{H}_{U,k}^{(n)}=\mathrm{diag}\bigg(\gamma_{1}\int_{0}^{1}\nabla^{2}U_{1}((1-s)Y_{k}^{(1,n)}+s\tilde{Y}_{k}^{(1,n)})\,\mathrm{d}s,

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\ldots,\gamma_{b}\int_{0}^{1}\nabla^{2}U_{b}((1-s)Y_{k}^{(b,n)}+s\tilde{Y}_{k}^{(b,n)})\,\mathrm{d}s\bigg)\;,

\displaystyle\mathbf{J}(k)=\mathrm{diag}\left({\mathds{1}_{[N_{1}]}(k+1)\cdot\mathbf{I}_{d_{1}},\cdots,\mathds{1}_{[N_{b}]}(k+1)\cdot\mathbf{I}_{d_{b}}}\right)\;,

(S21)

\displaystyle\mathbf{C}_{k}^{(n)}=\mathbf{J}(k)(\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}/\boldsymbol{\rho}}+\mathbf{H}_{U,k}^{(n)})\;,

(S22)

\displaystyle\mathbf{M}_{k+1}^{(n)}=(\mathbf{I}_{p}-\mathbf{C}_{0}^{(n)})^{-1}\ldots(\mathbf{I}_{p}-\mathbf{C}_{k}^{(n)})^{-1}\;,\qquad\text{ with }\mathbf{M}_{0}^{(n)}=\mathbf{I}_{p}\;.

(S23)

Under
2
, we have
\|\mathbf{C}_{k}^{(n)}\|\leq\max_{i\in[b]}\{{\gamma_{i}(M_{i}+1/\rho_{i})}\}
, thus if we suppose that for any
i\in[b],0<\gamma_{i}<(M_{i}+1/\rho_{i})^{-1}
, the matrix
(\mathbf{I}_{p}-\mathbf{C}_{k}^{(n)})
is invertible.
In addition, for any
n\in\mathbb{N},k\geq\max_{i\in[b]}\{N_{i}\},\mathbf{C}_{k}^{(n)}=\mathbf{0}_{p\times p}
, hence the sequence
(\mathbf{M}_{k}^{(n)})_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
is stationary and we denote its limit by
\mathbf{M}_{\infty}^{(n)}
which is equal to
\mathbf{M}_{\max_{i\in[b]}\{N_{i}\}}^{(n)}
.

S2.2.1
Technical lemmata

Similarly to
Lemma

S3
, the following result shows that it is enough to consider the marginal process
(Z_{n},\tilde{Z}_{n})_{n\geq 0}
to control

W_{2}(\updelta_{\mathbf{x}}P_{\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{\gamma},\boldsymbol{N}}^{n},\updelta_{\tilde{\mathbf{x}}}P_{\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{\gamma},\boldsymbol{N}}^{n})\leq\mathbb{E}\left[{\|X_{n}-\tilde{X}_{n}\|^{2}}\right]^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\;.

(S24)

Lemma S6
.

Assume
1
and let
\boldsymbol{N}\in(\mathbb{N}^{*})^{b},\boldsymbol{\gamma}\in(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{*})^{b}
.
Then, for any
n\in\mathbb{N}
, the random variables
X_{n}=(\theta_{n}^{\top},Z_{n}^{\top})^{\top},\tilde{X}_{n}=(\tilde{\theta}_{n}^{\top},\tilde{Z}_{n}^{\top})^{\top}
defined in (
S18
) satisfy

\|\tilde{X}_{n+1}-X_{n+1}\|^{2}\leq(1+\|\bar{\mathbf{B}}_{0}^{-1}\mathbf{B}_{0}^{\top}\mathbf{\tilde{D}}_{0}^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\|^{2})\|\tilde{Z}_{n+1}-Z_{n+1}\|^{2}\;,

where
\bar{\mathbf{B}}_{0},\mathbf{B}_{0},\mathbf{\tilde{D}}_{0}
are defined in (
S2
)-(
S3
).

Proof.

The proof is similar to the proof of
Lemma

S3
and is omitted.
∎

To ease notation, for any
i\in[b]
, we consider all along this section the quantities

\displaystyle\tilde{m}_{i}=m_{i}+1/\rho_{i}\;,

\displaystyle\tilde{M}_{i}=M_{i}+1/\rho_{i}\;.

(S25)

The following lemma provides an explicit expression for
\|\tilde{Z}_{n+1}-Z_{n+1}\|
with respect to
\|\tilde{Z}_{n}-Z_{n}\|
.

Lemma S7
.

Assume
1
-
2
and let
\boldsymbol{N}\in(\mathbb{N}^{*})^{b},\boldsymbol{\gamma}\in(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{*})^{b}
such that, for any
i\in[b],\gamma_{i}<\tilde{M}_{i}^{-1}
.
Then, for any
n\geq 1
, we have

\|\tilde{Z}_{n+1}-Z_{n+1}\|_{\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{N}\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{-1}}\leq\Big\|{[\mathbf{M}_{\infty}^{(n)}]^{-1}+\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}[\mathbf{M}_{\infty}^{(n)}]^{-1}\mathbf{M}_{k+1}^{(n)}\mathbf{J}(k)\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{N}}^{-{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}/\boldsymbol{\rho}}^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\mathbf{P}_{0}\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}/\boldsymbol{\rho}}^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{N}}^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}}\Big\|\\
\times\|\tilde{Z}_{n}-Z_{n}\|_{\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{N}\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{-1}}\;,

(S26)

where
(\mathbf{M}_{k}^{(n)})_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
is defined in (
S23
),
(\tilde{Z}_{k},Z_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
in (
S18
),
\boldsymbol{N}\boldsymbol{\gamma}=(\gamma_{1}N_{1},\ldots,\gamma_{b}N_{b})
and
\boldsymbol{\gamma}/\boldsymbol{\rho}=(\gamma_{1}/\rho_{1},\ldots,\gamma_{b}/\rho_{b})
.

Proof.

Let
n\geq 1
.
By (
S19
), for any
i\in[b],k\in\mathbb{N}
, we obtain

\tilde{Y}_{k+1}^{(i,n)}-Y_{k+1}^{(i,n)}=\bigg({\mathbf{I}_{d_{i}}-\gamma_{i}\int_{0}^{1}\nabla^{2}V_{i}((1-s)Y_{k}^{(i,n)}+s\tilde{Y}_{k}^{(i,n)})\,\mathrm{d}s}\bigg)(\tilde{Y}_{k}^{(i,n)}-Y_{k}^{(i,n)})\\
+(\gamma_{i}/\rho_{i})\mathbf{A}_{i}(\tilde{\theta}_{n}-\theta_{n})\;.

Consider the process
((\tilde{\mathrm{Y}}_{k}^{(n)},\mathrm{Y}_{k}^{(n)})=\{\tilde{\mathrm{Y}}_{k}^{(i,n)},\mathrm{Y}_{k}^{(i,n)}\}_{i=1}^{b})_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
with values in
\mathbb{R}^{p}\times\mathbb{R}^{p}
defined for any
i\in[b]
,
k\geq 0
, by

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathrm{Y}}_{k}^{(i,n)}=\tilde{Y}_{\min(k,N_{i})}^{(i,n)}\;,

\displaystyle\mathrm{Y}_{k}^{(i,n)}=Y_{\min(k,N_{i})}^{(i,n)}\;.

(S27)

By (
S18
), we have
\mathbf{A}_{i}(\tilde{\theta}_{n}-\theta_{n})=\mathbf{A}_{i}\bar{\mathbf{B}}_{0}^{-1}\mathbf{B}_{0}^{\top}\mathbf{\tilde{D}}_{0}^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}(\tilde{Z}_{n}-Z_{n})
. Since
\mathbf{B}_{0}^{\top}=[\mathbf{A}^{\top}_{1}/\rho_{1}^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\cdots\mathbf{A}^{\top}_{b}/\rho_{b}^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}]
and
\mathbf{P}_{0}=\mathbf{B}_{0}\bar{\mathbf{B}}_{0}^{-1}\mathbf{B}_{0}^{\top}
is the orthogonal projection matrix defined in (
S3
), it follows that

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathrm{Y}}_{k+1}^{(n)}-\mathrm{Y}_{k+1}^{(n)}=

\displaystyle\big({\mathbf{I}_{p}-\mathbf{C}_{k}^{(n)}}\big)(\tilde{\mathrm{Y}}_{k}^{(n)}-\mathrm{Y}_{k}^{(n)})+\mathbf{J}(k)\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}/\sqrt{\boldsymbol{\rho}}}\mathbf{P}_{0}\mathbf{\tilde{D}}_{0}^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}(\tilde{\mathrm{Y}}_{0}^{(n)}-\mathrm{Y}_{0}^{(n)})\;.

(S28)

Since
\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{N}\boldsymbol{\gamma}}
commutes with
\mathbf{C}_{k}^{(n)}
and
\mathbf{J}(k)
, multiplying (
S28
) by
\mathbf{M}_{k+1}^{(n)}\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{N}\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{-{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}
, yields

\mathbf{M}_{k+1}^{(n)}\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{N}\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{-{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}(\tilde{\mathrm{Y}}_{k+1}^{(n)}-\mathrm{Y}_{k+1}^{(n)})=\mathbf{M}_{k}^{(n)}\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{N}\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{-{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}(\tilde{\mathrm{Y}}_{k}^{(n)}-\mathrm{Y}_{k}^{(n)})\\
+\mathbf{M}_{k+1}^{(n)}\mathbf{J}(k)\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{N}}^{-{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}/\boldsymbol{\rho}}^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\mathbf{P}_{0}\mathbf{\tilde{D}}_{0}^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}(\tilde{\mathrm{Y}}_{0}^{(n)}-\mathrm{Y}_{0}^{(n)})\;.

(S29)

By definition of the processes in (
S18
)-(
S19
) and (
S27
), we have for
k\geq\max_{i\in[b]}\{N_{i}\}
,
(\tilde{\mathrm{Y}}_{k}^{(n)},\mathrm{Y}_{k}^{(n)})=(\tilde{Z}_{n+1},Z_{n+1})
and
\mathbf{J}(k)=\mathbf{0}_{p\times p}
.
Therefore summing the previous equality (
S29
) yields

\textstyle\mathbf{M}_{\infty}^{(n)}\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{N}\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{-{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}(\tilde{Z}_{n+1}-Z_{n+1})=\big[{\mathbf{M}_{0}^{(n)}+\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\mathbf{M}_{k+1}^{(n)}\mathbf{J}(k)\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{N}}^{-{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}/\boldsymbol{\rho}}^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\mathbf{P}_{0}\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}/\boldsymbol{\rho}}^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{N}}^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}}\big]\\
\times\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{N}\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{-{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}(\tilde{\mathrm{Y}}_{0}^{(n)}-\mathrm{Y}_{0}^{(n)})\;.

Multiplying this last equality by
[\mathbf{M}_{\infty}^{(n)}]^{-1}
and applying the norm
\|\cdot\|_{\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{N}\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{-1}}
concludes the proof.
∎

The three following lemmata aim at providing an explicit upper bound on (
S26
).
To this end, for
n,k\in\mathbb{N}
and
i\in[b]
, consider
\mathbf{C}_{k}^{(i,n)}
corresponding to the
i
-th diagonal block of
\mathbf{C}_{k}^{(n)}
defined in (
S22
),
i.e.

\mathbf{C}_{k}^{(i,n)}=\mathds{1}_{[N_{i}]}(k+1)\gamma_{i}\left\{{\rho_{i}^{-1}\mathbf{I}_{d_{i}}+\int_{0}^{1}\nabla^{2}U_{i}((1-s)Y_{k}^{(i,n)}+s\tilde{Y}_{k}^{(i,n)})\,\mathrm{d}s}\right\}\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{i}\times d_{i}}\;,

(S30)

where, for any
n\in\mathbb{N}
and
i\in[b]
,
(Y_{k}^{(i,n)},\tilde{Y}_{k}^{(i,n)})_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
is defined in (
S19
).
Thus, using the definition (
S23
) of
\mathbf{M}_{k}^{(n)}
, we can write
[\mathbf{M}_{\infty}^{(n)}]^{-1}\mathbf{M}_{k}^{(n)}
as a block-diagonal matrix
\mathrm{diag}(([\mathbf{M}_{\infty}^{(n)}]^{-1}\mathbf{M}_{k}^{(n)})^{1},\ldots,([\mathbf{M}_{\infty}^{(n)}]^{-1}\mathbf{M}_{k}^{(n)})^{b})
where for any
i\in[b]
,
([\mathbf{M}_{\infty}^{(n)}]^{-1}\mathbf{M}_{k}^{(n)})^{i}=\prod_{l=k}^{N_{i}-1}(\mathbf{I}_{d_{i}}-\mathbf{C}_{l}^{(i,n)})\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{i}\times d_{i}}
.

Lemma S8
.

Assume
1
-
2
and let
\boldsymbol{N}\in(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{*})^{b}
,
\boldsymbol{\gamma}\in(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{*})^{b}
such that, for any
i\in[b]
,
\gamma_{i}<\tilde{M}_{i}^{-1}
. Then, for any
i\in[b]
,
n\in\mathbb{N}
and
k\in[N_{i}]
, we have

\|{([\mathbf{M}_{\infty}^{(n)}]^{-1}\mathbf{M}_{k}^{(n)})^{i}-\mathbf{I}_{d_{i}}-\textstyle\sum_{l=k}^{\infty}\mathbf{C}_{l}^{(i,n)}}\|\leq\exp\{(N_{i}-k)\gamma_{i}\tilde{M}_{i}\}-1-(N_{i}-k)\gamma_{i}\tilde{M}_{i}\;,

where
\mathbf{M}_{k}^{(n)},\tilde{M}_{i}
are defined in (
S23
), (
S25
) respectively, and
\mathbf{M}_{\infty}^{(n)}
is the limit of the stationnary sequence
(\mathbf{M}_{k}^{(n)})_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
.

Proof.

Let
n\in\mathbb{N}
,
i\in[b]
and
k\in[N_{i}]
. The approximation error between
\prod_{l=k}^{\infty}(\mathbf{I}_{d_{i}}-\mathbf{C}_{l}^{(i,n)})
and its linear approximation can be upper bounded as

\displaystyle\bigg\|{\prod_{l=k}^{\infty}(\mathbf{I}_{d_{i}}-\mathbf{C}_{l}^{(i,n)})-\mathbf{I}_{d_{i}}-\sum_{l=k}^{\infty}\mathbf{C}_{l}^{(i,n)}}\bigg\|=\bigg\|{\sum_{m=2}^{\infty}(-1)^{m}\sum_{k\leq l_{1}<\cdots<l_{m}}\mathbf{C}_{l_{1}}^{(i,n)}\cdots\mathbf{C}_{l_{m}}^{(i,n)}}\bigg\|

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\leq\sum_{m=2}^{\infty}\sum_{k\leq l_{1}<\cdots<l_{m}}\|\mathbf{C}_{l_{1}}^{(i,n)}\|\cdots\|\mathbf{C}_{l_{m}}^{(i,n)}\|=\prod_{l=k}^{\infty}(1+\|\mathbf{C}_{l}^{(i,n)}\|)-1-\sum_{l\geq k}\|\mathbf{C}_{l}^{(i,n)}\|

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\leq\exp\bigg({\sum_{l=k}^{\infty}\|\mathbf{C}_{l}^{(i,n)}\|}\bigg)-1-\sum_{l=k}^{\infty}\|\mathbf{C}_{l}^{(i,n)}\|\;,

where the products and the sums are well defined since for any
l\geq N_{i}
, we have
\mathbf{C}_{l}^{(i,n)}=\mathbf{0}_{d_{i}}
.
Finally, the proof is concluded using that
x\mapsto\exp(x)-1-x
is increasing on
\mathbb{R}
and for
l\in\mathbb{N}
,
\|\mathbf{C}_{l}^{(i,n)}\|\leq\gamma_{i}\tilde{M}_{i}\mathds{1}_{[N_{i}]}(l+1)
from
2
-
(i)
.
∎

For any
\boldsymbol{N}=(N_{1},\ldots,N_{b})\in(\mathbb{N}^{*})^{b},\boldsymbol{\gamma}=(\gamma_{1},\ldots,\gamma_{b})\in(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{*})^{b}
, define the
p\times p
block matrices

\displaystyle\mathbf{S}_{1}=\mathrm{diag}(\{1-N_{1}\gamma_{1}\tilde{M}_{1}\}\mathbf{I}_{d_{1}},\cdots,\{1-N_{b}\gamma_{b}\tilde{M}_{b}\}\mathbf{I}_{d_{b}})\;,

\displaystyle\mathbf{S}_{2}=\mathbf{I}_{p}-\sum_{l=0}^{\infty}\mathbf{J}(l)\mathbf{H}_{U,l}^{(n)}-(\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{N}}\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}/\boldsymbol{\rho}})^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}(\mathbf{I}_{p}-\mathbf{P}_{0})(\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{N}}\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}/\boldsymbol{\rho}})^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\;,

(S31)

\displaystyle\mathbf{S}_{3}=\mathrm{diag}\left({\left\{1-N_{1}\gamma_{1}m_{1}\right\}\mathbf{I}_{d_{1}},\ldots,\left\{1-N_{b}\gamma_{b}m_{b}\right\}\mathbf{I}_{d_{b}}}\right)\;,

where for any
i\in[b]
,
\tilde{M}_{i}
is defined in (
S25
) and
\mathbf{P}_{0},\mathbf{J}(l),\mathbf{H}_{U,l}^{(n)}
are defined in (
S3
), (
S86
), (
S87
), respectively.

Lemma S9
.

Assume
1
-
2
. Then, for any
\boldsymbol{N}\in(\mathbb{N}^{*})^{b},\boldsymbol{\gamma}\in(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{*})^{b}
, we have

\mathbf{S}_{1}\preccurlyeq\mathbf{S}_{2}\preccurlyeq\mathbf{S}_{3}\;.

As a result, under the additional assumption, for any
i\in[b]
,
\gamma_{i}N_{i}\leq 2/(m_{i}+M_{i}+1/\rho_{i})
, we get

\mathinner{\!\left\lVert\mathbf{S}_{2}\right\rVert}\leq 1-\min_{i\in[b]}\{N_{i}\gamma_{i}m_{i}\}\;.

(S32)

Proof.

Since
\mathbf{P}_{0}
is an orthogonal projection defined in (
S3
), we have
\mathbf{P}_{0}\preccurlyeq\mathbf{I}_{p}
, therefore we easily get

\mathbf{0}_{p\times p}\preccurlyeq(\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{N}}\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}/\boldsymbol{\rho}})^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}(\mathbf{I}_{p}-\mathbf{P}_{0})(\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{N}}\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}/\boldsymbol{\rho}})^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\preccurlyeq\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{N}}\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}/\boldsymbol{\rho}}

and
2
-
(i)
-
(ii)
imply

\mathrm{diag}(N_{1}\gamma_{1}m_{1}\mathbf{I}_{d_{1}},\cdots,N_{b}\gamma_{b}m_{b}\mathbf{I}_{d_{b}})\preccurlyeq\sum_{l=0}^{\infty}\mathbf{J}(l)\mathbf{H}_{U,l}^{(n)}\preccurlyeq\mathrm{diag}(N_{1}\gamma_{1}M_{1}\mathbf{I}_{d_{1}},\cdots,N_{b}\gamma_{b}M_{b}\mathbf{I}_{d_{b}})\;.

Substracting these previous inequalities and adding
\mathbf{I}_{p}
complete the first part of the proof.
The additional condition, for any
i\in[b]
,
\gamma_{i}N_{i}\leq 2/(m_{i}+M_{i}+1/\rho_{i})
, ensures that
\mathbf{S}_{1}
is definite-positive.
Since
\mathbf{S}_{1}\preceq\mathbf{S}_{2}
, we deduce that
\mathbf{S}_{2}
is symmetric positive-definite as well.
Then,
\mathinner{\!\left\lVert\mathbf{S}_{2}\right\rVert}
is equal to the largest eigenvalue of
\mathbf{S}_{2}
.
The inequality
\mathbf{S}_{2}\preceq\mathbf{S}_{3}
concludes the second part of the proof.
∎

For any
\boldsymbol{N}=(N_{1},\ldots,N_{b})\in(\mathbb{N}^{*})^{b},\boldsymbol{\gamma}=(\gamma_{1},\ldots,\gamma_{b})\in(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{*})^{b}
, define

r_{\boldsymbol{\gamma},\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{N}}=\max_{i\in[b]}\{N_{i}\gamma_{i}/\rho_{i}\}\max_{i\in[b]}\{N_{i}\gamma_{i}\tilde{M}_{i}\}\Big(1/2+\max_{i\in[b]}\{N_{i}\gamma_{i}\tilde{M}_{i}\}\Big)+4\max_{i\in[b]}\{N_{i}\gamma_{i}\tilde{M}_{i}\}^{2}\;,

(S33)

where
\tilde{M}_{i}
is defined in (
S25
).

Lemma S10
.

Assume
1
-
2
. Let
\boldsymbol{N}\in(\mathbb{N}^{*})^{b},\boldsymbol{\gamma}\in(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{*})^{b}
such that, for any
i\in[b]
,
N_{i}\gamma_{i}\leq 2/(m_{i}+\tilde{M}_{i})
and
\gamma_{i}<\tilde{M}_{i}^{-1}
. Then, for any
n\in\mathbb{N}
, we have

\displaystyle\|[\mathbf{M}_{\infty}^{(n)}]^{-1}+\textstyle\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}[\mathbf{M}_{\infty}^{(n)}]^{-1}\mathbf{M}_{k+1}^{(n)}\mathbf{J}(k)\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{N}}^{-{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}/\boldsymbol{\rho}}^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\mathbf{P}_{0}\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}/\boldsymbol{\rho}}^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{N}}^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\|

\displaystyle\leq 1-\min_{i\in[b]}\{N_{i}\gamma_{i}m_{i}\}+r_{\boldsymbol{\gamma},\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{N}}\;,

where
\mathbf{P}_{0}
,
\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}/\boldsymbol{\rho}}
,
\mathbf{J}(k),\mathbf{M}_{k}^{(n)}
and
r_{\boldsymbol{\gamma},\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{N}}
are defined in (
S3
), (
S21
), (
S23
) and (
S33
), respectively.

Proof.

Let
n\in\mathbb{N}
. For any
k\in\mathbb{N}
, define

\mathbf{R}_{k}^{(n)}=\prod_{l=k}^{\infty}(\mathbf{I}_{p}-\mathbf{C}_{l}^{(n)})-\mathbf{I}_{p}+\sum_{l=k}^{\infty}\mathbf{C}_{l}^{(n)}\;,\qquad\mathbf{R}_{k}^{(i,n)}=\prod_{l=k}^{\infty}(\mathbf{I}_{d_{i}}-\mathbf{C}_{l}^{(i,n)})-\mathbf{I}_{d_{i}}+\sum_{l=k}^{\infty}\mathbf{C}_{l}^{(i,n)}\;,\quad i\in[b]\;,

(S34)

where
(\mathbf{C}_{l}^{(i,n)})_{l\in\mathbb{N}}
is defined in (
S30
) and remark that the products and the sums are well defined since for any
l\geq N_{i}
, we have
\mathbf{C}_{l}^{(i,n)}=\mathbf{0}_{d_{i}}
.
By noting, for any
k\in[\max_{i\in[b]}N_{i}]
, that

[\mathbf{M}_{\infty}^{(n)}]^{-1}\mathbf{M}_{k}^{(n)}=\prod_{l=k}^{\infty}(\mathbf{I}_{p}-\mathbf{C}_{l}^{(n)})
,
it follows that

[\mathbf{M}_{\infty}^{(n)}]^{-1}\mathbf{M}_{k}^{(n)}=\mathbf{I}_{p}-\sum_{l=k}^{\infty}\mathbf{C}_{l}^{(n)}+\mathbf{R}_{k}^{(n)}
.
Since for any
i\in[b],l\geq N_{i}
,
\mathbf{R}_{k}^{(i,n)}=\mathbf{0}_{d_{i}}
, thus we have
\mathbf{J}(k)\mathbf{R}_{k+1}^{(n)}=\mathbf{R}_{k+1}^{(n)}
. In addition, using that
\mathbf{M}_{0}^{(n)}=\mathbf{I}_{p}
,
\mathbf{C}_{l}^{(n)}=\mathbf{J}(l)(\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}/\boldsymbol{\rho}}+\mathbf{H}_{U,l}^{(n)})
,
\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{N}}=\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\mathbf{J}(k)
,
\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{N}}\mathbf{C}_{l}^{(n)}=\mathbf{C}_{l}^{(n)}\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{N}}
, we get

\displaystyle[\mathbf{M}_{\infty}^{(n)}]^{-1}+\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}[\mathbf{M}_{\infty}^{(n)}]^{-1}\mathbf{M}_{k+1}^{(n)}\mathbf{J}(k)\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{N}}^{-{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}/\boldsymbol{\rho}}^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\mathbf{P}_{0}\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}/\boldsymbol{\rho}}^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{N}}^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ \mathbf{I}_{p}-\sum_{l=0}^{\infty}\mathbf{C}_{l}^{(n)}+\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\mathbf{J}(k)\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{N}}^{-{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}/\boldsymbol{\rho}}^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\mathbf{P}_{0}\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}/\boldsymbol{\rho}}^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{N}}^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}-\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\sum_{l=k+1}^{\infty}\mathbf{J}(k)\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{N}}^{-{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\mathbf{C}_{l}^{(n)}\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}/\boldsymbol{\rho}}^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\mathbf{P}_{0}\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}/\boldsymbol{\rho}}^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{N}}^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}

\displaystyle+\mathbf{R}_{0}^{(n)}+\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\mathbf{R}_{k+1}^{(n)}\mathbf{J}(k)\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{N}}^{-{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}/\boldsymbol{\rho}}^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\mathbf{P}_{0}\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}/\boldsymbol{\rho}}^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{N}}^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ \mathbf{I}_{p}-\sum_{l=0}^{\infty}\mathbf{J}(l)\mathbf{H}_{U,l}^{(n)}-\Big(\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\mathbf{J}(k)\Big)\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{N}}^{-{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}/\boldsymbol{\rho}}^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}(\mathbf{I}_{p}-\mathbf{P}_{0})\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}/\boldsymbol{\rho}}^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{N}}^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}

\displaystyle-\sum_{l=1}^{\infty}\Big(\sum_{k=0}^{l-1}\mathbf{J}(k)\Big)\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{N}}^{-{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\mathbf{C}_{l}^{(n)}\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}/\boldsymbol{\rho}}^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\mathbf{P}_{0}\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}/\boldsymbol{\rho}}^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{N}}^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}+\mathbf{R}_{0}^{(n)}+\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\mathbf{J}(k)\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{N}}^{-{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\mathbf{R}_{k+1}^{(n)}\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}/\boldsymbol{\rho}}^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\mathbf{P}_{0}\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}/\boldsymbol{\rho}}^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{N}}^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ \mathbf{S}_{2}-\sum_{l=1}^{\infty}\Big(\sum_{k=0}^{l-1}\mathbf{J}(k)\Big)\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{N}}^{-1}\mathbf{C}_{l}^{(n)}(\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{N}}\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}/\boldsymbol{\rho}})^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\mathbf{P}_{0}(\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}/\boldsymbol{\rho}}\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{N}})^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}

\displaystyle+\mathbf{R}_{0}^{(n)}+\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{N}}^{-1}\mathbf{R}_{k}^{(n)}(\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{N}}\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}/\boldsymbol{\rho}})^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\mathbf{P}_{0}(\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}/\boldsymbol{\rho}}\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{N}})^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\;,

(S35)

where
\mathbf{S}_{2}
is defined in (
S31
).
We now bound the different terms of (
S35
) separately.
First, using (
S32
), we have

\mathinner{\!\left\lVert\mathbf{S}_{2}\right\rVert}\leq 1-\min_{i\in[b]}\{N_{i}\gamma_{i}m_{i}\}\;.

(S36)

By recalling
\mathbf{R}_{0}^{(n)}
defined in (
S34
),
Lemma

S8
shows that

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{R}_{0}^{(n)}\|\leq\max_{i\in[b]}\|\mathbf{R}_{0}^{(i,n)}\|

\displaystyle=\max_{i\in[b]}\bigg\{\Big\|\prod_{l=0}^{\infty}\big(\mathbf{I}_{d_{i}}-\mathbf{C}_{l}^{(i,n)}\big)-\mathbf{I}_{d_{i}}-\sum_{l=0}^{\infty}\mathbf{C}_{l}^{(i,n)}\Big\|\bigg\}

(S37)

\displaystyle\leq\max_{i\in[b]}\bigg\{\exp\Big(\sum_{l=0}^{\infty}\|\mathbf{C}_{l}^{(i,n)}\|\Big)-1-\sum_{l=0}^{\infty}\|\mathbf{C}_{l}^{(i,n)}\|\bigg\}

(S38)

\displaystyle\leq\max_{i\in[b]}\big\{\exp\{(N_{i}-1)\gamma_{i}\tilde{M}_{i}\}-1-(N_{i}-1)\gamma_{i}\tilde{M}_{i}\big\}

(S39)

\displaystyle\leq\max_{i\in[b]}\{((N_{i}-1)\gamma_{i}\tilde{M}_{i})^{2}\mathrm{e}^{(N_{i}-1)\gamma_{i}\tilde{M}_{i}}\}/2

(S40)

\displaystyle\leq 4\max_{i\in[b]}\{(N_{i}-1)\gamma_{i}\tilde{M}_{i}\}^{2}\;,

(S41)

where, in the penultimate line, we used for any
t\geq 0
, that
\exp(t)-1-t\leq t^{2}\exp(t)/2
.
Regarding the second term of (
S35
), using that
\mathbf{P}_{0}
is an orthogonal projector, we get

\mathinner{\!\left\lVert\sum_{l=1}^{\infty}\Big(\sum_{k=0}^{l-1}\mathbf{J}(k)\Big)\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{N}}^{-1}\mathbf{C}_{l}^{(n)}(\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{N}}\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}/\boldsymbol{\rho}})^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\mathbf{P}_{0}(\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{N}}\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}/\boldsymbol{\rho}})^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\right\rVert}\\
\leq\max_{i\in[b]}\left(\frac{N_{i}\gamma_{i}}{\rho_{i}}\right)\mathinner{\!\left\lVert\sum_{l=1}^{\infty}\Big(\sum_{k=0}^{l-1}\mathbf{J}(k)\Big)\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{N}}^{-1}\mathbf{C}_{l}^{(n)}\right\rVert}\;.

Combining the following upper bound

\mathinner{\!\left\lVert\sum_{l=1}^{\infty}\Big(\sum_{k=0}^{l-1}\mathbf{J}(k)\Big)\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{N}}^{-1}\mathbf{C}_{l}^{(n)}\right\rVert}\leq\max_{i\in[b]}\bigg\{{\frac{1}{N_{i}}\sum_{l=1}^{\infty}l\|\mathbf{C}_{l}^{(i,n)}\|}\bigg\}

with the fact, for any
i\in[b]
, that
\|\mathbf{C}_{l}^{(i,n)}\|\leq\gamma_{i}\tilde{M}_{i}\mathds{1}_{[N_{i}]}(l+1)
, we get that

\mathinner{\!\left\lVert\sum_{l=1}^{\infty}\Big(\sum_{k=0}^{l-1}\mathbf{J}(k)\Big)\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{N}}^{-1}\mathbf{C}_{l}^{(n)}(\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{N}}\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}/\boldsymbol{\rho}})^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\mathbf{P}_{0}(\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{N}}\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}/\boldsymbol{\rho}})^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\right\rVert}\\
\leq\max_{i\in[b]}\bigg(\frac{N_{i}\gamma_{i}}{\rho_{i}}\bigg)\max_{i\in[b]}\bigg\{{\frac{N_{i}\gamma_{i}\tilde{M}_{i}}{2}}\bigg\}\;.

(S42)

To upper bound the last term of (
S35
), we start from the following inequality

\mathinner{\!\left\lVert\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{N}}^{-1}\mathbf{R}_{k}^{(n)}\right\rVert}\leq\max_{i\in[b]}\bigg\{{\frac{1}{N_{i}}\sum_{k=1}^{N_{i}-1}\|\mathbf{R}_{k}^{(i,n)}\|}\bigg\}\;.

Lemma

S8
shows that for any
k\in[N_{i}-1]
and
i\in[b]
,

\textstyle\|\mathbf{R}_{k}^{(i,n)}\|\leq\exp\{(N_{i}-k)\gamma_{i}\tilde{M}_{i}\}-1-(N_{i}-k)\gamma_{i}\tilde{M}_{i}
. Then, for any
i\in[b]
, we have

\frac{1}{N_{i}}\sum_{k=1}^{N_{i}-1}\|\mathbf{R}_{k}^{(i,n)}\|\leq\frac{1}{N_{i}}\sum_{k=1}^{N_{i}-1}[\exp\{(N_{i}-k)\gamma_{i}\tilde{M}_{i}\}-1-(N_{i}-k)\gamma_{i}\tilde{M}_{i}]\\
\leq(N_{i}\gamma_{i}\tilde{M}_{i})^{-1}\int_{0}^{N_{i}\gamma_{i}\tilde{M}_{i}}(\mathrm{e}^{t}-1-t)\,\mathrm{d}t\leq\frac{(N_{i}\gamma_{i}\tilde{M}_{i})^{2}}{12}\big(\mathrm{e}^{N_{i}\gamma_{i}\tilde{M}_{i}}+1\big)\\
\leq\max_{i\in[b]}\{(N_{i}\gamma_{i}\tilde{M}_{i})^{2}\}\;,

(S43)

where we have used
\mathrm{e}^{2}+1\leq 12
. Plugging (
S43
), (
S42
), (
S41
) into (
S32
), we get

\displaystyle\Big\|{[\mathbf{M}_{\infty}^{(n)}]^{-1}+\sum_{k\in\mathbb{N}}[\mathbf{M}_{\infty}^{(n)}]^{-1}\mathbf{M}_{k+1}^{(n)}\mathbf{J}(k)\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{N}}^{-{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}/\boldsymbol{\rho}}^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\mathbf{P}_{0}\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}/\boldsymbol{\rho}}^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{N}}^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}}\Big\|\leq 1-\min_{i\in[b]}\{N_{i}\gamma_{i}m_{i}\}+r_{\boldsymbol{\gamma},\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{N}}\;,

where
r_{\boldsymbol{\gamma},\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{N}}
is defined in (
S33
).
∎

Lemma S11
.

Assume
1
-
2
. Let
\boldsymbol{N}\in(\mathbb{N}^{*})^{b},\boldsymbol{\gamma}\in(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{*})^{b}
such that, for any
i\in[b]
,
N_{i}\gamma_{i}\leq 2/(m_{i}+\tilde{M}_{i})
and
\gamma_{i}<\tilde{M}_{i}^{-1}
.
Then, for any
\mathbf{x}=(\mathbf{z}^{\top},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\top})^{\top},\tilde{\mathbf{x}}=(\tilde{\mathbf{z}}^{\top},\tilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{\top})^{\top}\in\mathbb{R}^{p+d}
, with
(\boldsymbol{\theta},\tilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}})\in(\mathbb{R}^{d})^{2},(\mathbf{z},\tilde{\mathbf{z}})\in(\mathbb{R}^{p})^{2}
and any
n\geq 1
we have

W_{2}^{2}(\updelta_{\tilde{\mathbf{x}}}P_{\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{\gamma},\boldsymbol{N}}^{n},\updelta_{\mathbf{x}}P_{\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{\gamma},\boldsymbol{N}}^{n})\leq(1-\min_{i\in[b]}\{N_{i}\gamma_{i}m_{i}\}+r_{\boldsymbol{\gamma},\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{N}})^{2n-2}(1+\|\bar{\mathbf{B}}_{0}^{-1}\mathbf{B}_{0}^{\top}\mathbf{\tilde{D}}_{0}^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\|^{2})\\
\times\frac{\max_{i\in[b]}\{N_{i}\gamma_{i}\}}{\min_{i\in[b]}\{N_{i}\gamma_{i}\}}\textstyle\left[\|[\mathbf{M}_{\infty}^{(0)}]^{-1}\|\|\tilde{\mathbf{z}}-\mathbf{z}\|+(\sum_{i\in[b]}\|\mathbf{A}_{i}\|/\rho_{i})\|\tilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}-\boldsymbol{\theta}\|\right]^{2}\;,

where
\mathbf{B}_{0},\bar{\mathbf{B}}_{0},\mathbf{\tilde{D}}_{0},P_{\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{\gamma},\boldsymbol{N}},\mathbf{M}_{\infty}^{(0)},r_{\boldsymbol{\gamma},\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{N}}
are defined in (
S2
), (
S3
), (
S17
), (
S23
), (
S33
), respectively.

Proof.

Combining
Lemma

S7
and
Lemma

S10
, we have for
n\geq 1
,

\displaystyle\|\tilde{Z}_{n+1}-Z_{n+1}\|_{\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{N}\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{-1}}

\displaystyle\leq(1-\min_{i\in[b]}\{N_{i}\gamma_{i}m_{i}\}+r_{\boldsymbol{\gamma},\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{N}})\|\tilde{Z}_{n}-Z_{n}\|_{\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{N}\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{-1}}\;.

Thereby, for any
n\geq 1
, we obtain by induction

\|\tilde{Z}_{n}-Z_{n}\|_{\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{N}\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{-1}}\leq(1-\min_{i\in[b]}\{N_{i}\gamma_{i}m_{i}\}+r_{\boldsymbol{\gamma},\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{N}})^{n-1}\|\tilde{Z}_{1}-Z_{1}\|_{\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{N}\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{-1}}\;.

(S44)

Define the process
((\tilde{\mathrm{Y}}_{k}^{(0)},\mathrm{Y}_{k}^{(0)})=\{\tilde{\mathrm{Y}}_{k}^{(i,0)},\mathrm{Y}_{k}^{(i,0)}\}_{i=1}^{b})_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
with values in
\mathbb{R}^{p}\times\mathbb{R}^{p}
defined for any
i\in[b]
,
k\geq 0
by

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathrm{Y}}_{k}^{(i,0)}=\tilde{Y}_{\min(k,N_{i})}^{(i,0)}\;,

\displaystyle\mathrm{Y}_{k}^{(i,0)}=Y_{\min(k,N_{i})}^{(i,0)}\;.

By (
S18
), it follows that for any
i\in[b]
,
(\tilde{Z}_{1}^{i},Z_{1}^{i})=(\tilde{Y}_{N_{i}}^{(i,0)},Y_{N_{i}}^{(i,0)})
where
(\tilde{Y}_{0}^{(i,0)},Y_{0}^{(i,0)})=(\tilde{Z}^{i}_{0},Z^{i}_{0})
. We get by (
S19
) for
k\geq 0
,

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathrm{Y}}_{k+1}^{(0)}-\mathrm{Y}_{k+1}^{(0)}=

\displaystyle(\mathbf{I}_{p}-\mathbf{C}_{k}^{(0)})(\tilde{\mathrm{Y}}_{k}^{(0)}-\mathrm{Y}_{k}^{(0)})+\mathbf{J}(k)\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}/\sqrt{\boldsymbol{\rho}}}\mathbf{B}_{0}(\tilde{\theta}_{0}-\theta_{0})\;.

Hence, for
k\geq 0
, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbf{M}_{k+1}^{(0)}\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{N}\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{-{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}(\tilde{\mathrm{Y}}_{k+1}^{(0)}-\mathrm{Y}_{k+1}^{(0)})=\mathbf{M}_{k}^{(0)}\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{N}\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{-{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}(\tilde{\mathrm{Y}}_{k}^{(0)}-\mathrm{Y}_{k}^{(0)})+\mathbf{M}_{k+1}^{(0)}\mathbf{J}(k)\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{N}}^{-{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}/\boldsymbol{\rho}}^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\mathbf{B}_{0}(\tilde{\theta}_{0}-\theta_{0})\;.

Summing the previous equality gives

\displaystyle\mathbf{M}_{\infty}^{(0)}\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{N}\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{-{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}(\tilde{\mathrm{Y}}_{\boldsymbol{N}}^{(0)}-\mathrm{Y}_{\boldsymbol{N}}^{(0)})

\displaystyle=\mathbf{M}_{0}^{(0)}\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{N}\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{-{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}(\tilde{\mathrm{Y}}_{0}^{(0)}-\mathrm{Y}_{0}^{(0)})+\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\mathbf{M}_{k+1}^{(0)}\mathbf{J}(k)\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{N}}^{-{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}/\boldsymbol{\rho}}^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\mathbf{B}_{0}(\tilde{\theta}_{0}-\theta_{0})\;.

Multiplying by
[\mathbf{M}_{\infty}^{(0)}]^{-1}
and using the fact that
(\theta_{0},\mathrm{Y}_{0}^{(0)})=(\boldsymbol{\theta},\mathbf{z})
,
(\tilde{\theta}_{0},\tilde{\mathrm{Y}}_{0}^{(0)})=(\tilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}},\tilde{\mathbf{z}})
, we get

\displaystyle\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{N}\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{-{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}(\tilde{Z}_{1}-Z_{1})=

\displaystyle[\mathbf{M}_{\infty}^{(0)}]^{-1}\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{N}\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{-{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}(\tilde{\mathbf{z}}-\mathbf{z})+\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}[\mathbf{M}_{\infty}^{(0)}]^{-1}\mathbf{M}_{k+1}^{(0)}\mathbf{J}(k)\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{N}}^{-{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}/\boldsymbol{\rho}}^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\mathbf{B}_{0}(\tilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}-\boldsymbol{\theta})\;.

Plugging the result in (
S52
) implies for any
n\geq 1
,

\textstyle\|\tilde{Z}_{n}-Z_{n}\|_{\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{N}\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{-1}}\leq\textstyle(1-\min_{i\in[b]}\{N_{i}\gamma_{i}m_{i}\}+r_{\boldsymbol{\gamma},\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{N}})^{n-1}\left[\|[\mathbf{M}_{\infty}^{(0)}]^{-1}\|\|\tilde{\mathbf{z}}-\mathbf{z}\|_{\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{N}\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{-1}}\right.\\
+\|\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}[\mathbf{M}_{\infty}^{(0)}]^{-1}\mathbf{M}_{k+1}^{(0)}\mathbf{J}(k)\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{N}}^{-{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}/\boldsymbol{\rho}}^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\mathbf{B}_{0}\|\|\tilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}-\boldsymbol{\theta}\|\bigg]\;.

(S45)

By
2
-
(ii)
and the definitions of
\mathbf{C}_{l}^{(0)},\mathbf{M}_{k}^{(0)}
given in (
S22
), (
S23
), we have
\|\mathbf{I}_{d_{i}}-\mathbf{C}_{l}^{(i,0)}\|\leq 1-\gamma_{i}\tilde{m}_{i}
. As a result and since
([\mathbf{M}_{\infty}^{(0)}]^{-1}\mathbf{M}_{k}^{(0)})^{i}=\prod_{l=0}^{k-1}(\mathbf{I}_{d_{i}}-\mathbf{C}_{l}^{(i,0)})
, the triangle inequality implies

\displaystyle\bigg\|{\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}[\mathbf{M}_{\infty}^{(0)}]^{-1}\mathbf{M}_{k+1}^{(0)}\mathbf{J}(k)\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{N}}^{-{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}/\boldsymbol{\rho}}^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\mathbf{B}_{0}}\bigg\|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i\in[b]}\sqrt{\gamma_{i}/N_{i}}(\|\mathbf{A}_{i}\|/\rho_{i})\sum_{k=1}^{N_{i}}\|([\mathbf{M}_{\infty}^{(0)}]^{-1}\mathbf{M}_{k}^{(0)})^{i}\|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i\in[b]}\sqrt{\gamma_{i}/N_{i}}(\|\mathbf{A}_{i}\|/\rho_{i})\sum_{k=0}^{N_{i}-1}(1-\gamma_{i}\tilde{m}_{i})^{k}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i\in[b]}\|\mathbf{A}_{i}\|\sqrt{N_{i}\gamma_{i}}/\rho_{i}\;.

Plugging this result in (
S45
), we get

\|\tilde{Z}_{n}-Z_{n}\|_{\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{N}\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{-1}}\leq(1-\min_{i\in[b]}\{N_{i}\gamma_{i}m_{i}\}+r_{\boldsymbol{\gamma},\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{N}})^{n-1}\Big[\|[\mathbf{M}_{\infty}^{(0)}]^{-1}\|\|\tilde{\mathbf{z}}-\mathbf{z}\|_{\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{N}\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{-1}}\\
+\Big({\sum_{i\in[b]}\|\mathbf{A}_{i}\|\sqrt{N_{i}\gamma_{i}}/\rho_{i}}\Big)\|\tilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}-\boldsymbol{\theta}\|\bigg]\;.

Finally,
Lemma

S6
gives

\|\tilde{X}_{n}-X_{n}\|^{2}\leq(1-\min_{i\in[b]}\{N_{i}\gamma_{i}m_{i}\}+r_{\boldsymbol{\gamma},\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{N}})^{2n-2}\cdot(1+\|\bar{\mathbf{B}}_{0}^{-1}\mathbf{B}_{0}^{\top}\mathbf{\tilde{D}}_{0}^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\|^{2})\frac{\max_{i\in[b]}\{N_{i}\gamma_{i}\}}{\min_{i\in[b]}\{N_{i}\gamma_{i}\}}\\
\times\bigg[{\|[\mathbf{M}_{\infty}^{(0)}]^{-1}\|\|\tilde{\mathbf{z}}-\mathbf{z}\|+\Big({\sum_{i\in[b]}\|\mathbf{A}_{i}\|/\rho_{i}}\Big)\|\tilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}-\boldsymbol{\theta}\|}\bigg]^{2}\;.

Plugging this result into (
S24
) concludes the proof.
∎

The following result gives a condition on
\max_{i\in[b]}\{N_{i}\gamma_{i}\}
to simplify the contrating term in
Lemma

S11
to
1-\min_{i\in[b]}\{N_{i}\gamma_{i}m_{i}\}/2
.
To this end, define

\displaystyle A_{0}

\displaystyle=\max_{i\in[b]}\{\tilde{M}_{i}\}\max_{i\in[b]}\{1/\rho_{i}\}/2+4\max_{i\in[b]}\{\tilde{M}_{i}\}^{2}\;,

\displaystyle A_{1}

\displaystyle=\max_{i\in[b]}\{\tilde{M}_{i}\}^{2}\max_{i\in[b]}\{1/\rho_{i}\}\;.

Lemma S12
.

Assume
1
-
2
and let
c\in\mathbb{R}_{+}^{*},\boldsymbol{N}\in(\mathbb{N}^{*})^{b},\boldsymbol{\gamma}\in(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{*})^{b}
such that

\displaystyle\min_{i\in[b]}\{N_{i}\gamma_{i}\}/\max_{i\in[b]}\{N_{i}\gamma_{i}\}\geq c\;,

(S46)

\displaystyle\max_{i\in[b]}\{N_{i}\gamma_{i}\}\leq\frac{c\min_{i\in[b]}\{m_{i}\}}{2A_{0}+\sqrt{2A_{1}c\min_{i\in[b]}\{m_{i}\}}}\wedge\frac{2}{\max_{i\in[b]}\{m_{i}+M_{i}+1/\rho_{i}\}}\;.

Then,
1-\min_{i\in[b]}\{N_{i}\gamma_{i}m_{i}\}+r_{\boldsymbol{\gamma},\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{N}}<1-\min_{i\in[b]}\{N_{i}\gamma_{i}m_{i}\}/2<1
, where
r_{\boldsymbol{\gamma},\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{N}}
is defined in (
S33
).

Proof.

The proof is straightforward solving a second order polynomial inequality and using for any
a,{b}\in\mathbb{R}_{+}^{*}
,
{a}+\frac{b^{2}}{2{a}+{b}}\leq\sqrt{{a}^{2}+b^{2}}
.
∎

S2.2.2
Proof of
Proposition

2

The next proposition quantifies the convergence of
\updelta_{\mathbf{x}}P_{\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{\gamma},\boldsymbol{N}}^{n}
towards
\Pi_{\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{\gamma}}
in
(\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),W_{2})
, where
\Pi_{\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{\gamma}}
is the stationnary distribution derived in
Proposition

S5
. In addition, it generalises and gives a more formal statement than
Proposition

2
.

Proposition S13
.

Assume
1
-
2
and let
c>0
and
\boldsymbol{\gamma}=\{\gamma_{i}\}_{i=1}^{b}
,
\boldsymbol{N}\in(\mathbb{N}^{*})^{b}
such that (
S46
) is satisfied, for any
i\in[b]
,
N_{i}\gamma_{i}<2/\textstyle\max_{i\in[b]}\{m_{i}+\tilde{M}_{i}\}
and
\gamma_{i}<\tilde{M}_{i}^{-1}
. Then,
P_{\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{\gamma},\boldsymbol{N}}
defined in (
S17
) admits a unique invariant probability measure
\Pi_{\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{\gamma},\boldsymbol{N}}
. In addition, for any
\mathbf{x}=(\mathbf{z}^{\top},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\top})^{\top}
whith
(\boldsymbol{\theta},\mathbf{z})\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{p}
, any integer
n\geq 1
, we have

W_{2}^{2}(\updelta_{\mathbf{x}}P_{\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{\gamma},\boldsymbol{N}}^{n},\Pi_{\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{\gamma}})\leq(1-\min_{i\in[b]}\{N_{i}\gamma_{i}m_{i}\}/2)^{2n-2}\cdot(1+\|\bar{\mathbf{B}}_{0}^{-1}\mathbf{B}_{0}^{\top}\mathbf{\tilde{D}}_{0}^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\|^{2})\frac{\max_{i\in[b]}\{N_{i}\gamma_{i}\}}{\min_{i\in[b]}\{N_{i}\gamma_{i}\}}\\
\times\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{p}}\bigg[{\|[\mathbf{M}_{\infty}^{(0)}]^{-1}\|\|\tilde{\mathbf{z}}-\mathbf{z}\|+\Big({\sum_{i\in[b]}\|\mathbf{A}_{i}\|/\rho_{i}}\Big)\|\tilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}-\boldsymbol{\theta}\|}\bigg]^{2}\mathrm{d}\Pi_{\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{\gamma}}(\tilde{\mathbf{x}})\;,

where
\mathbf{B}_{0},\bar{\mathbf{B}}_{0},\mathbf{M}_{\infty}^{(0)}
are defined in (
S2
), (
S23
), respectively.

Finally, if
\boldsymbol{N}=N(1,\ldots,1)=N\boldsymbol{1}_{b}
for
N\geq 1
, then
\Pi_{\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{\gamma},\boldsymbol{N}}=\Pi_{\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{\gamma},\boldsymbol{1}_{b}}
.

Proof.

Note that under the conditions on
\boldsymbol{\gamma}
and
\boldsymbol{N}
stated in
Proposition

S13
,
Lemma

S12
ensures that
1-\min_{i\in[b]}\{N_{i}\gamma_{i}m_{i}\}/2<1
.
Then, from
Lemma

S11
and
Douc
et al. 2018
, we deduce the existence and unicity of a stationary distribution
\Pi_{\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{\gamma},\boldsymbol{N}}
for
P_{\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{\gamma},\boldsymbol{N}}
.
The proof is concluded by using the upper bound given in
Lemma

S11
.

We now show the last statement and assume that
\boldsymbol{N}=N\boldsymbol{1}_{b}
, for
N\geq 1
. By
Proposition

S5
, we have the existence and unicity of a stationary distribution
\Pi_{\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{\gamma},\boldsymbol{1}_{b}}
which is invariant for
P_{\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{\gamma}}
defined in (
S7
). For ease of notation, we simply denote
\Pi_{\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{\gamma},\boldsymbol{1}_{b}}
by
\Pi_{\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{\gamma}}

We now show that
\Pi_{\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{\gamma}}
is also invariant for
P_{\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{\gamma},\boldsymbol{N}}
defined in (
S17
).
Using the fact that
P_{\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{\gamma}}
defined in (
S7
) leaves
\Pi_{\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{\gamma}}
invariant from
Proposition

S5
and Fubini’s theorem, we get for any
\mathsf{A}\in\mathcal{B}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
and
\mathsf{B}\in\mathcal{B}(\mathbb{R}^{p})
,

\displaystyle\Pi_{\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{\gamma}}P_{\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{\gamma},\boldsymbol{N}}(\mathsf{A}\times\mathsf{B})

(S47)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathsf{A}\times\mathsf{B}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{p}}\Pi_{\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{\gamma}}(\mathrm{d}\tilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}},\mathrm{d}\tilde{\mathbf{z}})P_{\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{\gamma},\boldsymbol{N}}((\tilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}},\tilde{\mathbf{z}}),(\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{\theta},\mathrm{d}\mathbf{z}))

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathsf{A}\times\mathsf{B}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{p}}\Pi_{\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{\gamma}}(\mathrm{d}\tilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}},\mathrm{d}\tilde{\mathbf{z}})Q_{\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{\gamma},\boldsymbol{N}}(\tilde{\mathbf{z}},\mathrm{d}\mathbf{z}|\tilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}})\Pi_{\boldsymbol{\rho}}(\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{\theta}|\mathbf{z})

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathsf{A}\times\mathsf{B}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{p}}\Pi_{\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{\gamma}}(\mathrm{d}\tilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}},\mathrm{d}\tilde{\mathbf{z}})\left[{\prod_{i=1}^{b}R_{\rho_{i},\gamma_{i}}^{N_{i}}(\tilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i},\mathrm{d}\mathbf{z}_{i}|\tilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}})}\right]\Pi_{\boldsymbol{\rho}}(\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{\theta}|\mathbf{z})

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathsf{A}\times\mathsf{B}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{p}}\Pi_{\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{\gamma}}(\mathrm{d}\tilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}},\mathrm{d}\tilde{\mathbf{z}})\int_{\mathbb{R}^{p}}\left[{\prod_{i=1}^{b}R_{\rho_{i},\gamma_{i}}(\tilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i},\mathrm{d}\tilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i}^{(1)}|\tilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}})}\right]\left[{\prod_{i=1}^{b}R_{\rho_{i},\gamma_{i}}^{N_{i}-1}(\tilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i}^{(1)},\mathrm{d}\mathbf{z}_{i}|\tilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}})}\right]\Pi_{\boldsymbol{\rho}}(\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{\theta}|\mathbf{z})

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathsf{A}\times\mathsf{B}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{p}}\left[{\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{p}}\Pi_{\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{\gamma}}(\mathrm{d}\tilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}},\mathrm{d}\tilde{\mathbf{z}})\left[{\prod_{i=1}^{b}R_{\rho_{i},\gamma_{i}}(\tilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i},\mathrm{d}\tilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i}^{(1)}|\tilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}})}\right]\Pi_{\boldsymbol{\rho}}(\mathrm{d}\tilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{(1)}|\tilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i}^{(1)})}\right]

\displaystyle\times\left[{\prod_{i=1}^{b}R_{\rho_{i},\gamma_{i}}^{N_{i}-1}(\tilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i}^{(1)},\mathrm{d}\mathbf{z}_{i}|\tilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}})}\right]\Pi_{\boldsymbol{\rho}}(\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{\theta}|\mathbf{z})

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathsf{A}\times\mathsf{B}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{p}}\Pi_{\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{\gamma}}(\mathrm{d}\tilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{(1)},\mathrm{d}\tilde{\mathbf{z}}^{(1)})\left[{\prod_{i=1}^{b}R_{\rho_{i},\gamma_{i}}^{N_{i}-1}(\tilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i}^{(1)},\mathrm{d}\mathbf{z}_{i}|\tilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{(1)})}\right]\Pi_{\boldsymbol{\rho}}(\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{\theta}|\mathbf{z})\;.

Using a straightforward induction, we finally get

\displaystyle\int_{\mathsf{A}\times\mathsf{B}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{p}}\Pi_{\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{\gamma}}(\mathrm{d}\tilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}},\mathrm{d}\tilde{\mathbf{z}})P_{\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{\gamma},\boldsymbol{N}}((\tilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}},\tilde{\mathbf{z}}),(\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{\theta},\mathrm{d}\mathbf{z}))=\int_{\mathsf{A}\times\mathsf{B}}\Pi_{\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{\gamma}}(\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{\theta},\mathrm{d}\mathbf{z})\;,

which shows that
P_{\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{\gamma},\boldsymbol{N}}
leaves
\Pi_{\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{\gamma}}
invariant.
Since this stationary distribution is unique, we conclude that
\Pi_{\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{\gamma},\boldsymbol{N}}=\Pi_{\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{\gamma}}
.
∎

We specify our result to the case where we take a specific initial distribution. To define it, consider

\text{
$\mathbf{x}^{\star}=([\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\star}]^{\top},[\mathbf{z}^{\star}]^{\top})^{\top}$, where $\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\star}=\operatorname*{arg\,min}\{-\log\pi\}$ and $\mathbf{z}^{\star}=([\mathbf{A}_{1}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\star}]^{\top},\cdots,[\mathbf{A}_{b}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\star}]^{\top})^{\top}$}\;.

(S48)

We define the probability measure

\mu_{\boldsymbol{\rho}}^{\star}=\updelta_{\mathbf{z}^{\star}}\otimes\Pi_{\boldsymbol{\rho}}(\cdot|\mathbf{z}^{\star})\;.

(S49)

Note that sampling from
\mu_{\boldsymbol{\rho}}^{\star}
is straightforward and simply consists in setting
\mathbf{z}_{0}=\mathbf{z}^{\star}
and
\boldsymbol{\theta}_{0}=\bar{\mathbf{B}}_{0}^{-1}\mathbf{B}_{0}^{\top}\mathbf{\tilde{D}}_{0}^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\mathbf{z}_{0}+\bar{\mathbf{B}}_{0}^{-{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\xi
, where
\xi
is a
d
-dimensional standard Gaussian random variable.
We now specify our result when using
\mu_{\boldsymbol{\rho}}^{\star}
as an initial distribution.
Define the
\mathbf{z}
-marginal under
\Pi_{\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{\gamma}}
by

\pi^{\mathbf{z}}_{\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{\gamma}}=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\Pi_{\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{\gamma}}(\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{\theta},\mathbf{z})\;,

(S50)

and the transition kernel of the Markov chain
\{Z_{n}\}_{n\geq 0}
, for all
\mathbf{z}\in\mathbb{R}^{p}
and
\mathsf{B}\in\mathcal{B}(\mathbb{R}^{p})
, by

P_{\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{\gamma},\boldsymbol{N}}^{\mathbf{z}}(\mathbf{z},\mathsf{B})=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}Q_{\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{\gamma},\boldsymbol{N}}(\mathbf{z},\mathsf{B}|\boldsymbol{\theta})\Pi_{\rho}(\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{\theta}|\mathbf{z})\;,

(S51)

where
\Pi_{\rho}(\cdot|\cdot)
and
Q_{\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{\gamma},\boldsymbol{N}}
are defined in (
5
) and (
S16
), respectively.

Proposition S14
.

Assume
1
-
2
and let
c>0
and
\boldsymbol{\gamma}=\{\gamma_{i}\}_{i=1}^{b}
,
\boldsymbol{N}\in(\mathbb{N}^{*})^{b}
such that (
S46
) is satisfied, for any
i\in[b]
,
N_{i}\gamma_{i}<2/\textstyle\max_{i\in[b]}\{m_{i}+\tilde{M}_{i}\}
and
\gamma_{i}<\tilde{M}_{i}^{-1}
.
Then, for any integer
n\geq 1
, we have

W_{2}(\mu_{\boldsymbol{\rho}}^{\star}P_{\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{\gamma},\boldsymbol{N}}^{n},\Pi_{\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{\gamma}})\leq 2^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}(1-\min_{i\in[b]}\{N_{i}\gamma_{i}m_{i}\}/2)^{n-1}\cdot(1+\|\bar{\mathbf{B}}_{0}^{-1}\mathbf{B}_{0}^{\top}\mathbf{\tilde{D}}_{0}^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\|^{2})^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\max_{i\in[b]}\{N_{i}\gamma_{i}\}^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\\
\times\left\{\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\|\mathbf{z}_{1}-\mathbf{z}^{\star}\|_{\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{N}\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{-1}}^{2}\pi^{\mathbf{z}}_{\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{\gamma}}(\mathrm{d}\mathbf{z}_{1})+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\|\mathbf{z}_{1}-\mathbf{z}^{\star}\|_{\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{N}\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{-1}}^{2}P_{\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{\gamma},\boldsymbol{N}}^{\mathbf{z}}(\mathbf{z}^{\star},\mathrm{d}\mathbf{z}_{1})\right\}^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\;,

where
\bar{\mathbf{B}}_{0},\mathbf{B}_{0},\mathbf{\tilde{D}}_{0}
are defined in (
S2
)-(
S3
).

Proof.

Consider for
n\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
,
X_{n}=(\theta_{n}^{\top},Z_{n}^{\top})^{\top},\tilde{X}_{n}=(\tilde{\theta}_{n}^{\top},\tilde{Z}_{n}^{\top})^{\top}
defined in (
S18
) with
X_{0}
distributed according to
\mu_{\boldsymbol{\rho}}^{\star}
and
\tilde{X}_{0}
distributed according to
\Pi_{\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{\gamma}}
.
Combining
Lemma

S7
,
Lemma

S10
and
Lemma

S12
, we have for
n\geq 1
,

\displaystyle\|\tilde{Z}_{n+1}-Z_{n+1}\|_{\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{N}\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{-1}}

\displaystyle\leq(1-\min_{i\in[b]}\{N_{i}\gamma_{i}m_{i}\}/2)\|\tilde{Z}_{n}-Z_{n}\|_{\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{N}\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{-1}}\;.

Thereby, for any
n\geq 1
, we obtain by induction

\|\tilde{Z}_{n}-Z_{n}\|_{\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{N}\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{-1}}\leq(1-\min_{i\in[b]}\{N_{i}\gamma_{i}m_{i}\}/2)^{n-1}\|\tilde{Z}_{1}-Z_{1}\|_{\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{N}\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{-1}}\;.

(S52)

Using
\|{\tilde{Z}_{1}-Z_{1}}\|^{2}_{\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{N}\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{-1}}\leq 2\|{\tilde{Z}_{1}-\mathbf{z}^{\star}}\|_{\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{N}\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{-{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}}^{2}+2\|{Z_{1}-\mathbf{z}^{\star}}\|_{\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{N}\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{-{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}}^{2}
combined with the definition of the Wasserstein distance and
Lemma

S6
give

\displaystyle W_{2}(\mu_{\boldsymbol{\rho}}^{\star}P_{\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{\gamma},\boldsymbol{N}}^{n},\Pi_{\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{\gamma}})

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left[{\|{\tilde{X}_{n}-X_{n}}\|^{2}}\right]^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}

\displaystyle\leq(1+\|\bar{\mathbf{B}}_{0}^{-1}\mathbf{B}_{0}^{\top}\mathbf{\tilde{D}}_{0}^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\|^{2})^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\max_{i\in[b]}\{N_{i}\gamma_{i}\}^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\mathbb{E}\bigg[{\|{\tilde{Z}_{n}-Z_{n}}\|_{\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{N}\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{-{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}}^{2}}\bigg]^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}

\displaystyle\leq 2^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}(1-\min_{i\in[b]}\{N_{i}\gamma_{i}m_{i}\}/2)^{n-1}(1+\|\bar{\mathbf{B}}_{0}^{-1}\mathbf{B}_{0}^{\top}\mathbf{\tilde{D}}_{0}^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\|^{2})^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\max_{i\in[b]}\{N_{i}\gamma_{i}\}^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}

\displaystyle\times\mathbb{E}\bigg[{\|{\tilde{Z}_{1}-\mathbf{z}^{\star}}\|_{\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{N}\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{-{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}}^{2}+\|{Z_{1}-\mathbf{z}^{\star}}\|_{\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{N}\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{-{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}}^{2}}\bigg]^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\;.

(S53)

Since
\tilde{X}_{0}
is distributed according to the stationnary distribution
\Pi_{\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{\gamma}}
,
\tilde{X}_{1}
also and therefore
\tilde{Z}_{1}
is distributed according to
\pi_{\boldsymbol{\rho},\bar{\gamma}}^{\mathbf{z}}
. Finally, by definition
Z_{1}
has distribution
P_{\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{\gamma},\boldsymbol{N}}^{\mathbf{z}}(\mathbf{z}^{\star},\cdot)
, therefore (
S53
) completes the proof.
∎

Appendix S3
Proof of
Proposition

3

The proof of Proposition 3 stands for a generalization of
Vono
et al. 2019
which only considered the specific case
\rho_{i}=\rho^{2}
for
i\in[b]
.
This section is divided into two parts, the first gathers lemmas which allow us to upper bound the
\xi^{2}
-divergence between
\pi_{\boldsymbol{\rho}}
and
\pi
. Then, in the second subsection, we combine these results to control the Wasserstein distance
W_{2}(\pi_{\boldsymbol{\rho}},\pi)
by showing that it is smaller than
\chi^{2}(\pi_{\boldsymbol{\rho}}|\pi)
.
For any
\boldsymbol{\theta}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
and
\boldsymbol{\rho}\in(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{*})^{b}
, define

\displaystyle U_{i}^{\rho_{i}}(\mathbf{A}_{i}\boldsymbol{\theta})=-\log\bigg({\int_{\mathbf{z}_{i}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\exp\{{-U_{i}(\mathbf{z}_{i})-\|\mathbf{z}_{i}-\mathbf{A}_{i}\boldsymbol{\theta}\|^{2}/(2\rho_{i})}\}\,\mathrm{d}\mathbf{z}_{i}/(2\pi\rho_{i})^{d_{i}/2}}\bigg)\;,

(S54)

\displaystyle\overline{B}(\boldsymbol{\theta})=\sum_{i=1}^{b}\rho_{i}\|\nabla U_{i}(\mathbf{A}_{i}\boldsymbol{\theta})\|^{2}/2\;,

(S55)

\displaystyle\underline{B}(\boldsymbol{\theta})=\sum_{i=1}^{b}\left\{{\rho_{i}\|\nabla U_{i}(\mathbf{A}_{i}\boldsymbol{\theta})\|^{2}/[2(1+\rho_{i}M_{i})]-d_{i}\log(1+\rho_{i}M_{i})/2}\right\}

(S56)

and consider

\displaystyle U(\boldsymbol{\theta})=\sum_{i\in[b]}U_{i}(\mathbf{A}_{i}\boldsymbol{\theta})\;,

\displaystyle U^{\boldsymbol{\rho}}(\boldsymbol{\theta})=\sum_{i\in[b]}U_{i}^{\rho_{i}}(\mathbf{A}_{i}\boldsymbol{\theta})\;.

S3.1
Technical lemmata

We start this subsection by
Lemma

S15
which allow us to bound the ratio between the integrals defined by
\textstyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\exp\{{-\sum_{i\in[b]}U_{i}^{\rho_{i}}(\mathbf{A}_{i}\boldsymbol{\theta})}\}
and
\textstyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\exp\{{-\sum_{i\in[b]}U_{i}(\mathbf{A}_{i}\boldsymbol{\theta})}\}\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{\theta}
.

Lemma S15
.

Assume
1
-
2
-
(i)
and let
\boldsymbol{\rho}\in(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{*})^{b}
. Then, we have
\underline{B}(\boldsymbol{\theta})\leq U(\boldsymbol{\theta})-U^{\boldsymbol{\rho}}(\boldsymbol{\theta})
, for any
\boldsymbol{\theta}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
.
If we assume in addition that for any
i\in[b]
,
U_{i}
is convex, we have
U(\boldsymbol{\theta})-U^{\boldsymbol{\rho}}(\boldsymbol{\theta})\leq\overline{B}(\boldsymbol{\theta})
, for any
\boldsymbol{\theta}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
.

Proof.

The proof follows from the same lines as in
Vono
et al. 2019
. In what follows, we give it for the sake of completeness.
First, note for any
\boldsymbol{\theta}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
and
i\in[b]
,

\exp\left\{{U_{i}(\mathbf{A}_{i}\boldsymbol{\theta})-U_{i}^{\rho_{i}}(\mathbf{A}_{i}\boldsymbol{\theta})}\right\}=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{i}}}\exp\left({U_{i}(\mathbf{A}_{i}\boldsymbol{\theta})-U_{i}(\mathbf{z}_{i})-\|\mathbf{z}_{i}-\mathbf{A}_{i}\boldsymbol{\theta}\|^{2}/(2\rho_{i})}\right)\frac{\mathrm{d}\mathbf{z}_{i}}{(2\uppi\rho_{i})^{d_{i}/2}}\;.

(S57)

Using
2
-
(i)
, and a second order Taylor expansion, for any
\boldsymbol{\theta}\in\mathbb{R}^{d},i\in[b],\mathbf{z}_{i}\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{i}}
, we have

\displaystyle U_{i}(\mathbf{A}_{i}\boldsymbol{\theta})-U_{i}(\mathbf{z}_{i})

\displaystyle\geq\nabla U_{i}(\mathbf{A}_{i}\boldsymbol{\theta})^{\top}(\mathbf{A}_{i}\boldsymbol{\theta}-\mathbf{z}_{i})-M_{i}\|\mathbf{A}_{i}\boldsymbol{\theta}-\mathbf{z}_{i}\|^{2}/2\;.

Hence, using (
S57
), we have for any
\boldsymbol{\theta}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
and
i\in[b]
,

\displaystyle\exp\bigg({\sum_{i=1}^{b}U_{i}(\mathbf{A}_{i}\boldsymbol{\theta})-U_{i}^{\rho_{i}}(\mathbf{A}_{i}\boldsymbol{\theta})}\bigg)

\displaystyle\geq\prod_{i=1}^{b}\exp\Big({\dfrac{\rho_{i}}{2(1+\rho_{i}M_{i})}\mathinner{\!\left\lVert\nabla U_{i}(\mathbf{A}_{i}\boldsymbol{\theta})\right\rVert}^{2}}\Big)\left({1+\rho_{i}M_{i}}\right)^{-d_{i}/2}

\displaystyle=\exp(\underline{B}(\boldsymbol{\theta}))\;.

Similarly, under the assumption that for any
i\in[b]
,
U_{i}
is convex, the proof for the upper bound follows from the same lines using, for any
i\in[b]
,
\boldsymbol{\theta}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
and
\mathbf{z}_{i}\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{i}}
, that
