\displaystyle U_{i}(\mathbf{A}_{i}\boldsymbol{\theta})-U_{i}(\mathbf{z}_{i})\leq\nabla U_{i}(\mathbf{A}_{i}\boldsymbol{\theta})^{\top}(\mathbf{A}_{i}\boldsymbol{\theta}-\mathbf{z}_{i})\;.

∎

Lemma S16
.

Assume
1
-
2
.
Then,
U
is
m_{U}
-strongly convex with
m_{U}=\lambda_{\min}(\sum_{i=1}^{b}m_{i}\mathbf{A}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{A}_{i})
.

Proof.

Using by
2
-
(i)
that for any
i\in[b]
,
U_{i}
is twice differentiable and by
2
-
(ii)
the fact that for any
i\in[b]
,
U_{i}
is
m_{i}
-strongly convex, we have for any
\boldsymbol{\theta}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}

\nabla^{2}U(\boldsymbol{\theta})=\sum_{i=1}^{b}\mathbf{A}_{i}^{\top}\nabla^{2}U_{i}(\mathbf{A}_{i}\boldsymbol{\theta})\mathbf{A}_{i}\succeq\sum_{i=1}^{b}m_{i}\mathbf{A}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{A}_{i}\succeq\lambda_{\min}\bigg({\sum_{i=1}^{b}m_{i}\mathbf{A}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{A}_{i}}\bigg)\mathbf{I}_{d}=m_{U}\mathbf{I}_{d}\;.

∎

For any
\boldsymbol{\theta}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, define

\beta(\boldsymbol{\theta})=\bigg({\sum_{i=1}^{b}\rho_{i}\bigg\|{\nabla U_{i}(\mathbf{A}_{i}\boldsymbol{\theta})}\bigg\|^{2}}\bigg)^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\;.

(S58)

Lemma S17
.

Assume
2
-
(i)
and let
\boldsymbol{\rho}\in(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{*})^{b}
.
Then
\beta
is a Lipschitz function w.r.t.
\mathinner{\!\left\lVert\cdot\right\rVert}
, with Lipschitz constant

L_{\beta}=\lambda_{\max}\bigg({\sum_{i=1}^{b}\rho_{i}M_{i}^{2}\mathbf{A}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{A}_{i}}\bigg)^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\;.

(S59)

Proof.

For any
\boldsymbol{\theta}_{1},\boldsymbol{\theta}_{2}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, we have using

|(\sum_{i=1}^{b}a_{i}^{2})^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}-(\sum_{i=1}^{b}b_{i}^{2})^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}|\leq(\sum_{i=1}^{b}(a_{i}-b_{i})^{2})^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}},

that

\displaystyle|\beta(\boldsymbol{\theta}_{1})-\beta(\boldsymbol{\theta}_{2})|\leq\bigg({\sum_{i=1}^{b}\rho_{i}\|\nabla U_{i}(\mathbf{A}_{i}\boldsymbol{\theta}_{1})-\nabla U_{i}(\mathbf{A}_{i}\boldsymbol{\theta}_{2})\|^{2}}\bigg)^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\leq\bigg({\sum_{i=1}^{b}\rho_{i}M_{i}^{2}\|\mathbf{A}_{i}(\boldsymbol{\theta}_{1}-\boldsymbol{\theta}_{2})\|^{2}}\bigg)^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}},

which completes the proof.
∎

Suppose
2
-
(ii)
and for any
i\in[b]
, denote
\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}^{\star}
a minimiser of
\boldsymbol{\theta}\mapsto U_{i}(\mathbf{A}_{i}\boldsymbol{\theta})
.

Lemma S18
.

Assume
1
-
2
and let
\boldsymbol{\rho}\in(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{*})^{b}
.
Then for any
s<m_{U}/(12L_{\beta}^{2})
, where
L_{\beta}
is defined in (
S59
), we have

\log\pi\big[{\mathrm{e}^{s\{\beta^{2}-\pi[\beta^{2}]\}}}\big]\leq 8s^{2}L_{\beta}^{4}/m_{U}^{2}+4s^{2}\{\pi[\beta]\}^{2}L_{\beta}^{2}/m_{U}\;.

(S60)

In addition,

\pi(\beta^{2})\leq 2dL_{\beta}^{2}/m_{U}+2\sum_{i=1}^{b}\rho_{i}M_{i}^{2}\|\mathbf{A}_{i}(\boldsymbol{\theta^{\star}}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\star}_{i})\|^{2}\;.

(S61)

Proof.

Using the decomposition

\beta^{2}(\boldsymbol{\theta})-\{\pi[\beta]\}^{2}=(\beta(\boldsymbol{\theta})-\pi[\beta])^{2}+2\pi[\beta](\beta(\boldsymbol{\theta})-\pi[\beta])

and the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality imply, for any
s>0
,

\pi\big[{\mathrm{e}^{s\{\beta^{2}-\{\pi[\beta]\}^{2}\}}}\big]\leq\big\{{\pi[\mathrm{e}^{2s\{\beta-\pi[\beta]\}^{2}}]}\big\}^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\cdot\big\{{{\pi}[\mathrm{e}^{4s\pi[\beta]\{\beta-\pi[\beta]\}}]}\big\}^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\;.

(S62)

The proof consists in bounding the two terms in the right-hand sided.
Since
\beta\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}
is
L_{\beta}
-Lipschitz by
Lemma

S17
, for any
0\leq s\leq m_{U}/(12L_{\beta}^{2})
, using
Vono
et al. 2019
and
Lemma

S16
gives setting
\bar{\beta}=\beta-\pi[\beta]
, that

\pi\big[{\exp\{2s(\bar{\beta}^{2}-\pi[\bar{\beta}^{2}])\}}\big]\leq\exp(16s^{2}L_{\beta}^{4}/m_{U}^{2})\;.

(S63)

In addition, using
Bakry
et al. 2013
,
Lemma

S17
and
Lemma

S16
, we get for any
s\geq 0
,

\pi\big[{\mathrm{e}^{4s\pi[\beta](\beta-\pi[\beta])}}\big]\leq\mathrm{e}^{8s^{2}\{\pi[\beta]\}^{2}L_{\beta}^{2}/m_{U}}\;.

Plugging this result and (
S63
) into (
S62
), we get

\pi\big[{\mathrm{e}^{s\{\beta^{2}-\{\pi[\beta]\}^{2}\}}}\big]\leq\exp(s\pi(\bar{\beta}^{2})+8s^{2}L_{\beta}^{4}/m_{U}^{2}+4s^{2}\{\pi[\beta]\}^{2}L_{\beta}^{2}/m_{U})\;.

The proof of (
S60
) follows using
\pi(\bar{\beta}^{2})=\pi(\beta^{2})-[\pi(\beta)]^{2}
and rearranging terms.

Using the Young inequality,
2
-
(i)
,
\nabla U_{i}(\mathbf{A}_{i}\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}^{\star})=0
,
\nabla U(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\star})=0
, we have

\displaystyle\pi(\beta^{2})

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\bigg({\sum_{i=1}^{b}\rho_{i}\|\nabla U_{i}(\mathbf{A}_{i}\boldsymbol{\theta})\|^{2}}\bigg)\pi(\boldsymbol{\theta})\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{\theta}

\displaystyle\leq 2\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\bigg({\sum_{i=1}^{b}\rho_{i}M_{i}^{2}\|\mathbf{A}_{i}(\boldsymbol{\theta}-\boldsymbol{\theta^{\star}})\|^{2}}\bigg)\pi(\boldsymbol{\theta})\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{\theta}+2\sum_{i=1}^{b}\rho_{i}M_{i}^{2}\|\mathbf{A}_{i}(\boldsymbol{\theta^{\star}}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\star}_{i})\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 2\lambda_{\max}\bigg({\sum_{i=1}^{b}\rho_{i}M_{i}^{2}\mathbf{A}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{A}_{i}}\bigg)\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\|\boldsymbol{\theta}-\boldsymbol{\theta^{\star}}\|^{2}\pi(\boldsymbol{\theta})\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{\theta}+2\sum_{i=1}^{b}\rho_{i}M_{i}^{2}\|\mathbf{A}_{i}(\boldsymbol{\theta^{\star}}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\star}_{i})\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 2dL_{\beta}^{2}/m_{U}+2\sum_{i=1}^{b}\rho_{i}M_{i}^{2}\|\mathbf{A}_{i}(\boldsymbol{\theta^{\star}}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\star}_{i})\|^{2}\;,

where we have used
\pi[\|\boldsymbol{\theta}-\boldsymbol{\theta^{\star}}\|^{2}]\leq d/m_{U}
by
Durmus and
Moulines 2019
and
Lemma

S16
.
∎

Proposition

1
shows that
\pi_{\boldsymbol{\rho}}(\cdot)=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{p}}\Pi_{\boldsymbol{\rho}}(\cdot,\mathbf{z})\mathrm{d}\mathbf{z}
is well-defined and as such admits a finite normalising constant.
These two quantities are defined by

\displaystyle\mathrm{Z}_{\pi_{\boldsymbol{\rho}}}=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\exp\bigg\{{-\sum_{i\in[b]}U_{i}^{\rho_{i}}(\mathbf{A}_{i}\boldsymbol{\theta})}\bigg\}\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{\theta}\;,

\displaystyle\pi_{\boldsymbol{\rho}}(\cdot)=\exp\bigg\{{-\sum_{i\in[b]}U_{i}^{\rho_{i}}(\mathbf{A}_{i}\cdot)}\bigg\}/\mathrm{Z}_{\pi_{\boldsymbol{\rho}}}\;.

(S64)

Finally, note that the following quantity
\mathrm{Z}_{\pi}
is a normalising constant of
\pi
associated with the potential
U
,
i.e.

\pi=\mathrm{e}^{-U}/\mathrm{Z}_{\pi}
,

\mathrm{Z}_{\pi}=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\exp\bigg\{{-\sum_{i\in[b]}U_{i}(\mathbf{A}_{i}\boldsymbol{\theta})}\bigg\}\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{\theta}\;.

(S65)

Lemma S19
.

Assume
1
-
2
and let
\boldsymbol{\rho}\in(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{*})^{b}
. Suppose in addition that
6L_{\beta}^{2}\leq m_{U}
where
L_{\beta}
is given in (
S59
).
Then, we have

\log\left({\mathrm{Z}_{\pi_{\boldsymbol{\rho}}}/\mathrm{Z}_{\pi}}\right)\leq\bigg\{{dL_{\beta}^{2}/m_{U}+\sum_{i=1}^{b}\rho_{i}M_{i}^{2}\|\mathbf{A}_{i}(\boldsymbol{\theta^{\star}}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\star}_{i})\|^{2}}\bigg\}(1+2L_{\beta}^{2}/m_{U})+2L_{\beta}^{4}/m_{U}^{2}\;.

Proof.

From the definitions (
S64
) and (
S65
), we have

\mathrm{Z}_{\pi_{\boldsymbol{\rho}}}/\mathrm{Z}_{\pi}=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\pi(\boldsymbol{\theta})\exp\{\sum_{i=1}^{b}U_{i}(\mathbf{A}_{i}\boldsymbol{\theta})-U_{i}^{\rho_{i}}(\mathbf{A}_{i}\boldsymbol{\theta})\}\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{\theta}.

By
Lemma

S15
, we obtain

\mathrm{Z}_{\pi_{\boldsymbol{\rho}}}/\mathrm{Z}_{\pi}\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\pi(\boldsymbol{\theta})\exp(\overline{B}(\boldsymbol{\theta}))\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{\theta}\;.

Note that
\overline{B}=\beta^{2}/2
by (
S55
)-(
S58
),
hence using that
6L_{\beta}^{2}\leq m_{U}
, Lemma
S18
applied with
s=1/2
shows that

\log\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\pi(\boldsymbol{\theta})\exp(\overline{B}(\boldsymbol{\theta}))\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)\leq\pi[\beta^{2}]/2+2L_{\beta}^{4}/m_{U}^{2}+\{\pi[\beta]\}^{2}L_{\beta}^{2}/m_{U}\;.

Using
Lemma

S18
-(
S61
) and
\pi[\beta]\leq\pi[\beta^{2}]
concludes the proof.
∎

S3.2
Proof of
Proposition

3

Based on the technical lemmas derived in
Section

S3.1
, we are now ready to bound the Wasserstein distance of order 2 between
\pi
and
\pi_{\boldsymbol{\rho}}
.

Proof of Proposition
Proposition

3
.

Let
\boldsymbol{\rho}\in(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{*})^{b}
such that
\max_{i\in[b]}\rho_{i}=\bar{\rho}\leq\sigma_{U}^{2}/12
, where
\sigma_{U}^{2}=\|\mathbf{A}^{\top}\mathbf{A}\|\max_{i\in[b]}\{M_{i}^{2}\}/m_{U}
. Then, by definition of
L_{\beta}
(
S59
), we get

12L_{\beta}^{2}\leq m_{U}\;.

(S66)

and
Lemma

S18
can be applied for
s=1
and
Lemma

S19
too.
By
Lemma

S16
,
U=-\log\pi
is
m_{U}
-strongly convex therefore
\pi
satisfies a log-Sobolev inequality with constant
m_{U}

(
Ledoux 2001
, Theorem 5.2)
.
Finally,
Otto and
Villani 2000
shows that
\pi
satisfies for any
\nu\in\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
:

W_{2}(\nu,\pi)\leq\sqrt{(2/m_{U})\mathrm{KL}(\nu|\pi)}\leq\sqrt{(2/m_{U})\chi^{2}(\pi_{\boldsymbol{\rho}}|\pi)}\;,

(S67)

where
\chi^{2}
is the chi-square divergence and where we have used for the last inequality that
\mathrm{KL}(\pi_{\boldsymbol{\rho}}|\pi)\leq\chi^{2}(\pi_{\boldsymbol{\rho}}|\pi)
since
for any
t>0
,
\log(t)\leq t-1
. We now bound
\chi^{2}(\pi_{\boldsymbol{\rho}}|\pi)
.
By (
S64
) and (
S65
), for any
\boldsymbol{\theta}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, consider the decomposition given by

\displaystyle\pi_{\boldsymbol{\rho}}(\boldsymbol{\theta})/\pi(\boldsymbol{\theta})-1

\displaystyle=(\mathrm{Z}_{\pi}/\mathrm{Z}_{\pi_{\boldsymbol{\rho}}})\exp\bigg({\sum_{i=1}^{b}\big({U_{i}(\mathbf{A}_{i}\boldsymbol{\theta})-U_{i}^{\rho_{i}}(\mathbf{A}_{i}\boldsymbol{\theta})}\big)}\bigg)-1\;.

(S68)

In the sequel, we will both lower and upper bound (
S68
) in order to upper bound
|1-\pi_{\boldsymbol{\rho}}(\boldsymbol{\theta})/\pi(\boldsymbol{\theta})|
.
Using the fact that for all
x\in\mathbb{R},\exp(x)-1\geq x
, Lemmas
S15
and
S19
yield

\displaystyle\pi_{\boldsymbol{\rho}}(\boldsymbol{\theta})/\pi(\boldsymbol{\theta})-1\geq\log\left({\mathrm{Z}_{\pi}/\mathrm{Z}_{\pi_{\boldsymbol{\rho}}}}\right)+\sum_{i=1}^{b}\left({U_{i}(\mathbf{A}_{i}\boldsymbol{\theta})-U_{i}^{\rho_{i}}(\mathbf{A}_{i}\boldsymbol{\theta})}\right)

(S69)

\displaystyle\geq-\bigg\{{dL_{\beta}^{2}/m_{U}+\sum_{i=1}^{b}\rho_{i}M_{i}^{2}\|\mathbf{A}_{i}(\boldsymbol{\theta^{\star}}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\star}_{i})\|^{2}}\bigg\}(1+2L_{\beta}^{2}/m_{U})-2L_{\beta}^{4}/m_{U}^{2}+\underline{B}(\boldsymbol{\theta})\geq-A_{1}\;,

where

A_{1}=\bigg\{{dL_{\beta}^{2}/m_{U}+\sum_{i=1}^{b}\rho_{i}M_{i}^{2}\|\mathbf{A}_{i}(\boldsymbol{\theta^{\star}}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\star}_{i})\|^{2}}\bigg\}(1+2L_{\beta}^{2}/m_{U})\\
+2L_{\beta}^{4}/m_{U}^{2}+\sum_{i=1}^{b}(d_{i}/2)\log(1+\rho_{i}M_{i})\;,

where we have used in the last inequality that
\underline{B}(\boldsymbol{\theta})\geq-\sum_{i=1}^{b}(d_{1}/2)\log(1+\rho_{i}M_{i})
by (
S55
).
In addition,
by (
S64
) and (
S65
)

\mathrm{Z}_{\pi_{\boldsymbol{\rho}}}/\mathrm{Z}_{\pi}=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\pi(\boldsymbol{\theta})\exp\{\sum_{i=1}^{b}U_{i}(\mathbf{A}_{i}\boldsymbol{\theta})-U_{i}^{\rho_{i}}(\mathbf{A}_{i}\boldsymbol{\theta})\}\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{\theta}.

, which implies by
Lemma

S15
and Jensen inequality

\mathrm{Z}_{\pi_{\boldsymbol{\rho}}}/\mathrm{Z}_{\pi}\geq\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\pi(\boldsymbol{\theta})\exp(\underline{B}(\boldsymbol{\theta}))\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{\theta}\geq\exp(\pi[\underline{B}])\;.

It follows by (
S68
) that
\pi_{\boldsymbol{\rho}}(\boldsymbol{\theta})/\pi(\boldsymbol{\theta})-1\leq\exp(\overline{B}(\boldsymbol{\theta})-\pi\left({\underline{B}}\right))-1
.
Combining this result and (
S69
), it follows that the Pearson
\chi^{2}
-divergence between
\pi
and
\pi_{\boldsymbol{\rho}}
can be upper bounded as where

\chi^{2}(\pi_{\boldsymbol{\rho}}|\pi)\leq\max(A_{1}^{2},A_{2})\;,\qquad A_{2}=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\big({\exp({\overline{B}(\boldsymbol{\theta})-\pi\left({\underline{B}}\right)})-1}\big)^{2}\pi(\boldsymbol{\theta})\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{\theta}\;.

We now provide an explicit bound for
A_{2}
. First by Jensen inequality, we have
\pi(\exp(\overline{B}))\geq\exp(\pi(\overline{B}))
which implies that
\exp\big({-\pi({\underline{B}})}\big)\pi\big[{\exp({\overline{B}})}\big]\geq\prod_{i=1}^{b}(1+\rho_{i}M_{i})^{d_{i}/2}
by (
S55
). Therefore, using that
\overline{B}=\beta^{2}/2
by (
S55
)-(
S58
) and
Lemma

S18
with
s=1
since (
S66
) holds, we get by (
S55
),

\displaystyle A_{2}=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\big({\exp\big({\overline{B}(\boldsymbol{\theta})-\pi\left({\underline{B}}\right)}\big)-1}\big)^{2}\pi(\boldsymbol{\theta})\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{\theta}

\displaystyle=\exp\left({-2\pi\left({\underline{B}}\right)}\right)\pi\big[{\exp\big({2\overline{B}}\big)}\big]-2\exp\big({-\pi\big({\underline{B}}\big)}\big)\pi\big[{\exp\big({\overline{B}}\big)}\big]+1

\displaystyle\leq\prod_{i=1}^{b}(1+\rho_{i}M_{i})^{d_{i}}\cdot\exp(-\pi\{\sum_{i=1}^{b}(\rho_{i}/(1+\rho_{i}M_{i}))\|\nabla U_{i}(\mathbf{A}_{i}\cdot)\|^{2}\})\pi\big[{\exp({\beta^{2}})}\big]

\displaystyle\qquad-2\prod_{i=1}^{b}(1+\rho_{i}M_{i})^{d_{i}/2}+1

\displaystyle\leq\prod_{i=1}^{b}(1+\rho_{i}M_{i})^{d_{i}}\cdot\exp(\pi\{\sum_{i=1}^{b}(\rho_{i}^{2}M_{i}/(1+\rho_{i}M_{i}))\|\nabla U_{i}(\mathbf{A}_{i}\cdot)\|^{2}\})

\displaystyle\times\exp\bigg({8L_{\beta}^{4}/m_{U}^{2}+4\{2dL_{\beta}^{2}/m_{U}+2\sum_{i=1}^{b}\rho_{i}M_{i}^{2}\|\mathbf{A}_{i}(\boldsymbol{\theta^{\star}}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\star}_{i})\|^{2}\}L_{\beta}^{2}/m_{U}}\bigg)

(S70)

\displaystyle\qquad-2\prod_{i=1}^{b}(1+\rho_{i}M_{i})^{d_{i}/2}+1\;,

(S71)

where we have used for the last inequality that for
\boldsymbol{\theta}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,
\beta(\boldsymbol{\theta})^{2}-\sum_{i=1}^{b}(\rho_{i}/(1+\rho_{i}M_{i}))\|\nabla U_{i}(\mathbf{A}_{i}\boldsymbol{\theta})\|^{2}=\sum_{i=1}^{b}(\rho_{i}^{2}M_{i}/(1+\rho_{i}M_{i}))\|\nabla U_{i}(\mathbf{A}_{i}\boldsymbol{\theta})\|^{2}
,
\pi[\beta]^{2}\leq\pi[\beta^{2}]
by the Cauchy-Schwartz inequality and
Lemma

S18
-(
S61
).
Similarly to the proof of
Lemma

S18
-(
S61
), by
2
-
(i)
,
\nabla U_{i}(\mathbf{A}_{i}\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}^{\star})=0
,
\nabla U(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\star})=0
,
Durmus and
Moulines 2019
and
Lemma

S16
, we have

\displaystyle\pi\left[{\sum_{i=1}^{b}(\rho_{i}^{2}M_{i}/(1+\rho_{i}M_{i}))\|\nabla U_{i}(\mathbf{A}_{i}\cdot)\|^{2}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\pi\left[{\sum_{i=1}^{b}\rho_{i}^{2}M_{i}\|\nabla U_{i}(\mathbf{A}_{i}\cdot)\|^{2}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq 2d\lambda_{\max}\Big({\sum_{i=1}^{b}\rho_{i}^{2}M_{i}^{3}\mathbf{A}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{A}_{i}}\Big)

\displaystyle/m_{U}+2\sum_{i=1}^{b}\rho_{i}^{2}M_{i}^{3}\|\mathbf{A}_{i}(\boldsymbol{\theta^{\star}}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\star}_{i})\|^{2}\;.

Therefore, we get by (
S71
)

\displaystyle A_{2}\leq A_{3}=\prod_{i=1}^{b}(1+\rho_{i}M_{i})^{d_{i}}\exp\bigg({2d\lambda_{\max}\Big({\sum_{i=1}^{b}\rho_{i}^{2}M_{i}^{3}\mathbf{A}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{A}_{i}}\Big)/m_{U}+2\sum_{i=1}^{b}\rho_{i}^{2}M_{i}^{3}\|\mathbf{A}_{i}(\boldsymbol{\theta^{\star}}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\star}_{i})\|^{2}}\bigg)

\displaystyle\exp\bigg({8L_{\beta}^{4}/m_{U}^{2}+8\Big[{dL_{\beta}^{2}/m_{U}+\sum_{i=1}^{b}\rho_{i}M_{i}^{2}\|\mathbf{A}_{i}(\boldsymbol{\theta^{\star}}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\star}_{i})\|^{2}}\Big]L_{\beta}^{2}/m_{U}}\bigg)-2\prod_{i=1}^{b}(1+\rho_{i}M_{i})^{d_{i}/2}+1\;.

(S72)

It follows by (
S3.2
) and (
S67
) that

\displaystyle W_{2}(\pi_{\boldsymbol{\rho}},\pi)

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{(2/m_{U})\max(A_{1}^{2},A_{3})}\;,

(S73)

where
A_{1}
and
A_{3}
are given by (
S69
) and (
S72
) respectively. Using that
L_{\beta}^{2}=\mathcal{O}(\bar{\rho})
and an expansion of the bound as
\bar{\rho}\to 0
completes the proof.
∎

Appendix S4
Proof of
Proposition

4
and
Proposition

5

As in
Appendix

S2
, we assume in all this section that
\boldsymbol{\rho}\in(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{*})^{b}
is fixed.
For any
\boldsymbol{\gamma}=(\gamma_{1},\ldots,\gamma_{b})\in(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{*})^{b}
, we establish in this section explicit bounds on
W_{2}(\pi_{\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{\gamma},\boldsymbol{N}},\pi_{\boldsymbol{\rho}})
where
\pi_{\boldsymbol{\rho}}
is given in (
1
) and
\pi_{\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{\gamma},\boldsymbol{N}}
is the marginal distribution defined by

\pi_{\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{\gamma},\boldsymbol{N}}(\mathsf{A})=\Pi_{\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{\gamma},\boldsymbol{N}}(\mathsf{A}\times\mathbb{R}^{p})\;,\qquad\mathsf{A}\in\mathcal{B}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\;,

of the stationary probability measure
\Pi_{\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{\gamma},\boldsymbol{N}}
associated with the Markov chain
(Z_{n},\theta_{n})_{n\geq 0}
defined in
Algorithm

1
. Note that in the case
\boldsymbol{N}=N(1,\ldots,1)
, this distribution is independent of
N
, see
Proposition

S13
.
To this purpose, we define an “ideal” dynamics from which we cannot sample but which converges geometrically towards
\Pi_{\boldsymbol{\rho}}
under appropriate conditions.
The corresponding ideal process will play the same role as the Langevin dynamics for the study of the unadjusted Langevin algorithm
(
Durmus and
Moulines 2019
)
.
This dynamics is defined as follows.
Consider first for any
\boldsymbol{\theta}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,
i\in[b]
, the stochastic differential equation (SDE) defined by

\mathrm{d}\tilde{Y}_{t}^{i,\boldsymbol{\theta}}=-\nabla V_{i}(\tilde{Y}^{i,\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{t})\,\mathrm{d}t-\rho_{i}^{-1}\mathbf{A}_{i}\boldsymbol{\theta}+\sqrt{2}\,\mathrm{d}B_{t}^{i}\;,

(S74)

where
(B_{t}^{i})_{t\geq 0}
is a
d_{i}
-dimensional Brownian motion and
V_{i}
is defined in (
S20
).
Note that under
2
-
(i)
, this SDE admits a unique strong solution
(
Revuz and
Yor 2013
, Theorem (2.1) in Chapter IX)
.
Denote for any
i\in[b]
, the Markov semi-group associated to (
S74
) by
(\tilde{R}_{\rho_{i},t}^{i})_{t\geq 0}
defined for any
\tilde{\mathbf{y}}_{0}^{i}\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{i}}
,
t\geq 0
and
\mathsf{B}_{i}\in\mathcal{B}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{i}})
by

\tilde{R}_{\rho_{i},t}^{i}(\tilde{\mathbf{y}}_{0}^{i},\mathsf{B}_{i}|\boldsymbol{\theta})=\mathbb{P}(\tilde{Y}^{i,\boldsymbol{\theta},\tilde{\mathbf{y}}_{0}^{i}}_{t}\in\mathsf{B}_{i})\;,

where
(\tilde{Y}_{t}^{i,\boldsymbol{\theta},\tilde{\mathbf{y}}_{0}^{i}})_{t\geq 0}
is a solution of (
S74
) with
\tilde{Y}_{0}^{i,\boldsymbol{\theta},\tilde{\mathbf{y}}_{0}^{i}}=\tilde{\mathbf{y}}_{0}^{i}
. For any bounded measurable function
f_{i}\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}\mathbb{R}^{d_{i}}\to\mathbb{R}_{+}
,
Lemma

S20
shows the measurability of the function
(\boldsymbol{\theta},\tilde{\mathbf{y}}_{0}^{i})\mapsto\mathbb{E}[f_{i}(\tilde{Y}_{t}^{i,\boldsymbol{\theta},\tilde{\mathbf{y}}_{0}^{i}})]
on
\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{i}}
and therefore
\tilde{R}_{\rho_{i},t}^{i}
is a conditional Markov kernel.

Lemma S20
.

For any bounded measurable function
f_{i}\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}\mathbb{R}^{d_{i}}\to\mathbb{R}_{+}
and function
f_{i}
satisfying
2
-
(i)
, the mapping
(\tilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0},\tilde{\mathbf{y}}_{0}^{i})\mapsto\mathbb{E}[f_{i}(\tilde{Y}_{t}^{i,\tilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0},\tilde{\mathbf{y}}_{0}^{i}})]
is Borel measurable.

Proof.

Consider the following stochastic differential equation

\begin{cases}\mathrm{d}\tilde{\theta}_{t}=\mathbf{0}_{d}\;,\\
\mathrm{d}\tilde{Y}_{t}^{i}=-\nabla V_{i}(\tilde{Y}^{i}_{t})\,\mathrm{d}t-\rho_{i}^{-1}\mathbf{A}_{i}\tilde{\theta}_{t}+\sqrt{2}\,\mathrm{d}B_{t}^{i}\;.\end{cases}

Using
Revuz and
Yor 2013
, since
U_{i}
satisfies
2
-
(i)
, there exists a unique solution
(\tilde{X}_{t}^{\tilde{\mathbf{x}}})_{t\geq 0}=(\tilde{\theta}_{t},\tilde{Y}_{t}^{i})_{t\geq 0}
with initial condition
\tilde{\mathbf{x}}=(\tilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0}^{\top},(\tilde{\mathbf{y}}_{0}^{i})^{\top})^{\top}\in\mathbb{R}^{p}
.
Then, the proof follows from
Revuz and
Yor 2013
and the fact that
\tilde{Y}_{t}^{i}
is the unique solution of (
S74
) with
\boldsymbol{\theta}=\boldsymbol{\theta}_{0}
.
∎

Define
for any
\boldsymbol{\theta}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,
\mathbf{z}=(\mathbf{z}_{1}^{\top},\cdots,\mathbf{z}_{b}^{\top})^{\top}\in\mathbb{R}^{p}
, and for
i\in[b]
,
\mathsf{B}_{i}\in\mathcal{B}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{i}})
,

\displaystyle\tilde{Q}_{\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{\gamma}}\left({\mathbf{z},\mathsf{B}_{1}\times\cdots\times\mathsf{B}_{b}|\boldsymbol{\theta}}\right)=\prod_{i=1}^{b}\tilde{R}_{\rho_{i},N_{i}\gamma_{i}}^{i}(\mathbf{z}_{i},\mathsf{B}_{i}|\boldsymbol{\theta})\;,

and consider the Markov kernel defined, for any
\mathbf{x}^{\top}=(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\top},\mathbf{z}^{\top})
and
\mathsf{A}\in\mathcal{B}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
,
\mathsf{B}\in\mathcal{B}(\mathbb{R}^{p})
, by

\tilde{P}_{\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{\gamma}}(\mathbf{x},\mathsf{A}\times\mathsf{B})=\int_{\mathsf{B}}\tilde{Q}_{\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{\gamma}}\left({\mathbf{z},\mathrm{d}{\tilde{\mathbf{z}}}|\boldsymbol{\theta}}\right)\int_{\mathsf{A}}\Pi_{\boldsymbol{\rho}}(\mathrm{d}\tilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}|\tilde{\mathbf{z}})\,\;,

(S75)

where
\Pi_{\boldsymbol{\rho}}(\cdot|\tilde{\mathbf{z}})
is defined in (
5
).
Note that
P_{\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{\gamma},\boldsymbol{N}}
can be interpreted as a discretised version of
\tilde{P}_{\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{\gamma}}
using the Euler-Maruyama scheme.

In the sequel, we first derive technical lemmata in
Section

S4.1
that are used to prove both
Proposition

4
and
Proposition

5
.
Based on these lemmata, we then prove each proposition in a dedicated section, namely
Section

S4.2
and
Section

S4.3
.

S4.1
Synchronous coupling and a first estimate

The main idea to prove
Proposition

4
and
Proposition

5
is to define
(X_{n},\tilde{X}_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
such that for any
n\in\mathbb{N}
,
(X_{n},\tilde{X}_{n})
is a coupling between
\updelta_{\mathbf{x}}P^{n}_{\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{\gamma},\boldsymbol{N}}
defined in (
S17
) and
\updelta_{\mathbf{\tilde{x}}}\tilde{P}^{n}_{\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{\gamma}}
, and satisfies

\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathinner{\!\left\lVert X_{n}-\tilde{X}_{n}\right\rVert}^{2}}\right]\leq c_{1}(\mathbf{x},\tilde{\mathbf{x}})\mathrm{e}^{-c_{2}\min_{i\in[b]}\{\gamma_{i}m_{i}\}}+c_{3}\gamma^{\alpha}\;,

where
c_{2},c_{3}>0
and
\alpha\in\{1,2\}
depending if
3
holds or not.
Conditioning with respect to
(X_{0},\tilde{X}_{0})
with distribution
\updelta_{\mathbf{x}}\otimes\Pi_{\boldsymbol{\rho}}
, using the definition of the Wasserstein distance of order 2 and taking
n\rightarrow\infty
, we obtain

W_{2}(\pi_{\boldsymbol{\rho}},\pi_{\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{\gamma},\boldsymbol{N}})\leq W_{2}(\Pi_{\boldsymbol{\rho}},\Pi_{\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{\gamma},\boldsymbol{N}})\leq\tilde{c}_{3}\gamma^{\alpha}\;,

where
\tilde{c}_{3}>0
.
We now provide the rigourous construction of
(X_{n},\tilde{X}_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
.

Let
\{(B^{(i,n)}_{t})_{t\geq 0}\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}i\in[b],n\in\mathbb{N}\}
be independent random variables such that for any
i\in[b]
, the sequences
\{(B^{(i,n)}_{t})_{t\geq 0}\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}n\in\mathbb{N}\}
are i.i.d.
d_{i}
-dimensional Brownian motions and let
(\xi_{n})_{n\geq 0}
be a sequence of i.i.d. standard
d
-dimensional Gaussian random variables independent of
\{(B_{t}^{(i,n)})_{t\geq 0}\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}i\in[b],n\in\mathbb{N}\}
.
Consider the stochastic process
(\tilde{X}_{n})_{n\geq 0}
on
\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{p}
starting from
\tilde{X}_{0}
distributed according to
\Pi_{\boldsymbol{\rho}}
and defined by the recursion: for
n\in\mathbb{N}
,
i\in[b]
,

\tilde{X}_{n+1}=(\tilde{\theta}_{n+1}^{\top},\tilde{Z}_{n+1}^{\top})^{\top}\;,\quad\tilde{Z}_{n+1}^{i}=\tilde{Y}^{(i,n)}_{N_{i}\gamma_{i}}\;,\quad\tilde{\theta}_{n+1}=\bar{\mathbf{B}}_{0}^{-1}\mathbf{B}_{0}^{\top}\mathbf{\tilde{D}}_{0}^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\tilde{Z}_{n+1}+\bar{\mathbf{B}}_{0}^{-{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\xi_{n+1}\;,

(S76)

where
(\tilde{Y}_{t}^{(i,n)})_{t\geq 0}
, is a solution of (
S74
) starting from
\tilde{Z}_{n}^{i}
with parameter
\boldsymbol{\theta}\leftarrow\boldsymbol{\theta}_{n}
.
Similarly to the process
(X_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
defined in
Algorithm

1
, the process
(\tilde{X}_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
defines a homogeneous Markov chain. Indeed, it is easy to show that for any
n\in\mathbb{N}
and measurable function
f\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}\mathbb{R}^{p}\to\mathbb{R}_{+}
,
\mathbb{E}[f(\tilde{Z}_{n+1})|\tilde{X}_{n}]=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{p}}f(\tilde{\mathbf{z}})\tilde{Q}_{\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{\gamma}}(\tilde{Z}_{n},\mathrm{d}\mathbf{z}|\tilde{\theta}_{n})
and therefore
(\tilde{X}_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
is associated with (
S75
).

Proposition S21
.

Assume
1
-
2
-
(i)
, and let
\boldsymbol{N}\in(\mathbb{N}^{*})^{b},\boldsymbol{\gamma}\in(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{*})^{b}
.
Then, the Markov kernel
\tilde{P}_{\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{\gamma}}
defined in (
S75
) admits
\Pi_{\boldsymbol{\rho}}
as an invariant probability measure.

Proof.

By property of the Langevin diffusion defined in (
S74
), for all
\boldsymbol{\theta}_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, the Markov kernel
\tilde{Q}_{\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{\gamma}}(\cdot|\boldsymbol{\theta}_{0})
admits
\Pi_{\boldsymbol{\rho}}(\cdot|\boldsymbol{\theta}_{0})
as invariant measure, see e.g.
Roberts and
Tweedie 1996
or
Kent 1978
. Thus, for any
\boldsymbol{\theta}_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
and
\mathsf{B}\in\mathcal{B}(\mathbb{R}^{p})
, we have

\int_{\mathsf{B}}\Pi_{\boldsymbol{\rho}}(\mathbf{z}_{1}|\boldsymbol{\theta}_{0})\,\mathrm{d}\mathbf{z}_{1}=\int_{\mathbf{z}_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{p}}\tilde{Q}_{\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{\gamma}}(\mathbf{z}_{0},\mathsf{B}|\boldsymbol{\theta}_{0})\Pi_{\boldsymbol{\rho}}(\mathbf{z}_{0}|\boldsymbol{\theta}_{0})\,\mathrm{d}\mathbf{z}_{0}\;.

(S77)

Denote by
\pi_{\boldsymbol{\rho}}^{\boldsymbol{\theta}},\pi_{\boldsymbol{\rho}}^{\mathbf{z}}
the marginals under
\Pi_{\boldsymbol{\rho}}
:
\pi_{\boldsymbol{\rho}}^{\boldsymbol{\theta}}(\mathsf{A})=\Pi_{\boldsymbol{\rho}}(\mathsf{A}\times\mathbb{R}^{p})
,
\pi_{\boldsymbol{\rho}}^{\mathbf{z}}(\mathsf{B})=\Pi_{\boldsymbol{\rho}}(\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathsf{B})
, for
\mathsf{A}\in\mathcal{B}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
and
\mathsf{B}\in\mathcal{B}(\mathbb{R}^{p})
, and
consider the Markov chain
(\tilde{X}_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
defined in (
S76
). For any measurable function
f\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}\mathbb{R}^{d+p}\to\mathbb{R}_{+}
, the Fubini-Tonelli theorem gives

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[f(\tilde{X}_{1})]

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d+p}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d+p}}f(\mathbf{x}_{1})\Pi_{\boldsymbol{\rho}}(\boldsymbol{\theta}_{1}|\mathbf{z}_{1})\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{\theta}_{1}\tilde{Q}_{\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{\gamma}}(\mathbf{z}_{0},\mathrm{d}\mathbf{z}_{1}|\boldsymbol{\theta}_{0})\Pi_{\boldsymbol{\rho}}(\boldsymbol{\theta}_{0},\mathbf{z}_{0})\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{\theta}_{0}\,\mathrm{d}\mathbf{z}_{0}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{p}}f(\mathbf{x}_{1})\Pi_{\boldsymbol{\rho}}(\boldsymbol{\theta}_{1}|\mathbf{z}_{1})\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\bigg[{\int_{\mathbb{R}^{p}}\tilde{Q}_{\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{\gamma}}(\mathbf{z}_{0},\mathrm{d}\mathbf{z}_{1}|\boldsymbol{\theta}_{0})\Pi_{\boldsymbol{\rho}}(\mathbf{z}_{0}|\boldsymbol{\theta}_{0})\,\mathrm{d}\mathbf{z}_{0}}\bigg]\pi_{\boldsymbol{\rho}}^{\boldsymbol{\theta}}(\boldsymbol{\theta}_{0})\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{\theta}_{0}\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{\theta}_{1}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{p}}f(\mathbf{x}_{1})\Pi_{\boldsymbol{\rho}}(\boldsymbol{\theta}_{1}|\mathbf{z}_{1})\bigg[{\int_{\boldsymbol{\theta}_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\Pi_{\boldsymbol{\rho}}(\mathbf{z}_{1}|\boldsymbol{\theta}_{0})\pi_{\boldsymbol{\rho}}^{\boldsymbol{\theta}}(\boldsymbol{\theta}_{0})\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{\theta}_{0}}\bigg]\,\mathrm{d}\mathbf{z}_{1}\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{\theta}_{1}

(S78)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{p}}f(\mathbf{x}_{1})\Pi_{\boldsymbol{\rho}}(\boldsymbol{\theta}_{1}|\mathbf{z}_{1})\pi_{\boldsymbol{\rho}}^{\mathbf{z}}(\mathbf{z}_{1})\,\mathrm{d}\mathbf{z}_{1}\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{\theta}_{1}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d+p}}f(\mathbf{x}_{1})\Pi_{\boldsymbol{\rho}}(\boldsymbol{\theta}_{1},\mathbf{z}_{1})\,\mathrm{d}\mathbf{z}_{1}\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{\theta}_{1}=\mathbb{E}[f(\tilde{X}_{0})]\;,

where we have used (
S77
) in (
S78
).
Therefore,
X_{1}
has distribution
\Pi_{\boldsymbol{\rho}}
and the Markov kernel
\tilde{P}_{\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{\gamma}}
admits
\Pi_{\boldsymbol{\rho}}
as a stationary distribution, which completes the proof.
∎

Define by induction the synchronous coupling
(X_{n}=(\theta_{n},Z_{n}))_{n\geq 0},(\tilde{X}_{n}=(\tilde{\theta}_{n},\tilde{Z}_{n}))_{n\geq 0}
, starting from
(\theta_{0},Z_{0})=(\boldsymbol{\theta},\mathbf{z})
,
(\tilde{\theta}_{0},\tilde{Z}_{0})
distributed according to
\Pi_{\boldsymbol{\rho}}
, for any
i\in[b]
and
n\geq 0
, as

\displaystyle\tilde{Z}_{n+1}^{i}=\tilde{Y}_{N_{i}\gamma_{i}}^{(i,n)}\;,

\displaystyle\tilde{\theta}_{n+1}=\bar{\mathbf{B}}_{0}^{-1}\mathbf{B}_{0}^{\top}\mathbf{\tilde{D}}_{0}^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\tilde{Z}_{n+1}+\bar{\mathbf{B}}_{0}^{-{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\xi_{n+1}\;,

(S79)

\displaystyle Z_{n+1}^{i}=Y_{N_{i}\gamma_{i}}^{(i,n)}\;,

\displaystyle\theta_{n+1}=\bar{\mathbf{B}}_{0}^{-1}\mathbf{B}_{0}^{\top}\mathbf{\tilde{D}}_{0}^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}Z_{n+1}+\bar{\mathbf{B}}_{0}^{-{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\xi_{n+1}\;,

where we consider for any
i\in[b],k\in\mathbb{N}
, for
t\in[k\gamma_{i},(k+1)\gamma_{i})

\displaystyle\tilde{Y}_{t}^{(i,n)}=\tilde{Y}_{k\gamma_{i}}^{(i,n)}-\int_{k\gamma_{i}}^{t}\nabla V_{i}(\tilde{Y}^{(i,n)}_{l})\,\mathrm{d}l+(t-k\gamma_{i})(\rho_{i})^{-1}\mathbf{A}_{i}\tilde{\theta}_{n}+2^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}(B_{t}^{(i,n)}-B_{k\gamma_{i}}^{(i,n)})\;,

(S80)

\displaystyle Y_{t}^{(i,n)}=Y_{k\gamma_{i}}^{(i,n)}-(t-k\gamma_{i})\nabla V_{i}(Y_{k\gamma_{i}}^{(i,n)})+(t-k\gamma_{i})(\rho_{i})^{-1}\mathbf{A}_{i}\theta_{n}+2^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}(B_{t}^{(i,n)}-B_{k\gamma_{i}}^{(i,n)})\;.

Let
\mathcal{G}_{0}=\sigma(Z_{0},\tilde{Z}_{0},\theta_{0},\tilde{\theta}_{0})
, for any
n\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
, let

\mathcal{G}_{n}=\sigma\{(Z_{0},\tilde{Z}_{0},\theta_{0},\tilde{\theta}_{0}),(B^{(i,k)}_{t})_{t\geq 0}\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}i\in[b],k\leq n\}\;,

(S81)

and for any
t\geq 0
, let
\mathcal{H}_{t}^{(n)}=\sigma(\{(B^{(i,n)}_{s})_{s\leq t}\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}i\in[b]\})
, and

\mathcal{F}_{t}^{(n)}\text{ the }\sigma\text{-field generated by }\mathcal{H}_{t}^{(n)}\text{ and }\mathcal{G}_{n-1}\;.

(S82)

Note that
X_{n}
and
\tilde{X}_{n}
are distributed according to
\Pi_{\boldsymbol{\rho}}\tilde{P}_{\boldsymbol{\rho}}^{n}
and
\updelta_{\tilde{\mathbf{x}}}P_{\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{\gamma},\boldsymbol{N}}^{n}
, respectively.
Hence, by definition of the Wasserstein distance of order 2, it follows since
\Pi_{\boldsymbol{\rho}}\tilde{P}_{\boldsymbol{\rho}}^{n}=\Pi_{\boldsymbol{\rho}}
by
Proposition

S21
that

W_{2}(\Pi_{\boldsymbol{\rho}},\updelta_{\mathbf{x}}P_{\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{\gamma},\boldsymbol{N}}^{n})\leq\mathbb{E}\left[{\|X_{n}-\tilde{X}_{n}\|^{2}}\right]^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\;.

(S83)

We start this section by a first estimate on
\mathbb{E}[\|X_{n}-\tilde{X}_{n}\|^{2}]^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}
and some technical results needed for the proof of
Proposition

4
and
Proposition

5
.
The following result holds regarding the process
(\tilde{Y}_{t}^{(i,n)})_{t\in\mathbb{R}_{+}}
defined, for any
i\in[b]
and
n\in\mathbb{N}
, in (
S80
).

Lemma S22
.

Assume
1
-
2
.
For
i\in[b],n\in\mathbb{N}
, denote by
\mathbf{z}^{i}_{n,\star}
the unique minimiser of
\mathbf{z}_{i}\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{i}}\mapsto U_{i}(\mathbf{z}_{i})+\|\mathbf{z}_{i}-\mathbf{A}_{i}\tilde{\theta}_{n}\|/(2\rho_{i})
.
Then, for any
i\in[b],k\in\mathbb{N}
and
n\in\mathbb{N}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}^{\mathcal{G}_{n}}\big[{\|\tilde{Y}_{k\gamma_{i}}^{(i,n)}-\mathbf{z}_{n,\star}^{i}\|^{2}}\big]

\displaystyle\leq{d_{i}}/{\tilde{m}_{i}}\;.

(S84)

where
\tilde{m}_{i}
is defined in (
S25
).

Proof.

Let
n\in\mathbb{N}
.
By
Durmus and
Moulines 2019
, for
i\in[b]
and
k\in\mathbb{N}
, we have

\mathbb{E}^{\mathcal{F}_{k\gamma_{i}}^{(n)}}\|\tilde{Y}_{k\gamma_{i}}^{(i,n)}-\mathbf{z}_{n,\star}^{i}\|^{2}\leq\|\tilde{Z}_{n}^{i}-\mathbf{z}_{n,\star}^{i}\|^{2}\mathrm{e}^{-2k\gamma_{i}\tilde{m}_{i}}+({d_{i}}/{\tilde{m}_{i}})(1-\mathrm{e}^{-2k\gamma_{i}\tilde{m}_{i}})\;.

(S85)

By (
S80
), using
Proposition

S21
we get that
\tilde{X}_{n}
has distribution
\Pi_{\boldsymbol{\rho}}
, therefore given
\tilde{\theta}_{n}
,
\tilde{Z}_{n}
has distribution
\Pi_{\boldsymbol{\rho}}(\cdot|\tilde{\theta}_{n})
. Then, using (
S85
),
Durmus and
Moulines 2019
combined with
2
, and since
(\tilde{Z}_{n}^{1},\ldots,\tilde{Z}_{n}^{b})
are independent given
\tilde{\theta}_{n}
, we get the stated result.
∎

Lemma S23
.

Assume
1
and let
\boldsymbol{N}\in(\mathbb{N}^{*})^{b},\boldsymbol{\gamma}\in(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{*})^{b}
.
Then, for any
n\in\mathbb{N}
, the random variables
X_{n}=(\theta_{n}^{\top},Z_{n}^{\top})^{\top},\tilde{X}_{n}=(\tilde{\theta}_{n}^{\top},\tilde{Z}_{n}^{\top})^{\top}
defined in (
S79
) sastify

\|\tilde{X}_{n+1}-X_{n+1}\|^{2}\leq(1+\|\bar{\mathbf{B}}_{0}^{-1}\mathbf{B}_{0}^{\top}\mathbf{\tilde{D}}_{0}^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\|^{2})\|\tilde{Z}_{n+1}-Z_{n+1}\|^{2}\;,

where
\bar{\mathbf{B}}_{0},\mathbf{B}_{0},\mathbf{\tilde{D}}_{0}
are defined in (
S2
)-(
S3
).

Proof.

The proof is similar to the proof of
Lemma

S3
and is omitted.
∎

For any
k,n\in\mathbb{N},s\in\mathbb{R}_{+}
consider the
p\times p
matrices defined by

\displaystyle\mathbf{J}(k,s)=\mathrm{diag}\left({\mathds{1}_{[N_{1}]}(k+1)\mathds{1}_{[0,\gamma_{1}]}(s)\cdot\mathbf{I}_{d_{1}},\cdots,\mathds{1}_{[N_{b}]}(k+1)\mathds{1}_{[0,\gamma_{b}]}(s)\cdot\mathbf{I}_{d_{b}}}\right)\;,

(S86)

\displaystyle\mathbf{H}_{U,k}^{(n)}=\mathrm{diag}\Big(\gamma_{1}\int_{0}^{1}\nabla^{2}U_{1}((1-s)Y_{k\gamma_{1}}^{(1,n)}+s\tilde{Y}_{k\gamma_{1}}^{(1,n)})\,\mathrm{d}s,

(S87)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\ldots,\gamma_{b}\int_{0}^{1}\nabla^{2}U_{b}((1-s)Y_{k\gamma_{b}}^{(b,n)}+s\tilde{Y}_{k\gamma_{b}}^{(b,n)})\,\mathrm{d}s\Big)\;,

\displaystyle\mathbf{C}_{k}^{(n)}=\mathbf{J}(k,0)(\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}/\boldsymbol{\rho}}+\mathbf{H}_{U,k}^{(n)})\;,

(S88)

\displaystyle\mathbf{M}_{k+1}^{(n)}=(\mathbf{I}_{p}-\mathbf{C}_{0}^{(n)})^{-1}\ldots(\mathbf{I}_{p}-\mathbf{C}_{k}^{(n)})^{-1}\;,\qquad\text{ with }\mathbf{M}_{0}^{(n)}=\mathbf{I}_{p}\;.

(S89)

Similarly to (
S19
), for
n,k\in\mathbb{N}
and
i\in[b]
, consider
\mathbf{C}_{k}^{(i,n)}
corresponding to the
i
-th diagonal block of
\mathbf{C}_{k}^{(n)}
defined in (
S88
),
i.e.

\mathbf{C}_{k}^{(i,n)}=\mathds{1}_{[N_{i}]}(k+1)\gamma_{i}\left\{{\rho_{i}^{-1}\mathbf{I}_{d_{i}}+\int_{0}^{1}\nabla^{2}U_{i}((1-s)Y_{k\gamma_{i}}^{(i,n)}+s\tilde{Y}_{k\gamma_{i}}^{(i,n)})\,\mathrm{d}s}\right\}\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{i}\times d_{i}}\;,

(S90)

where, for any
n\in\mathbb{N}
and
i\in[b]
,
(Y_{k\gamma_{i}}^{(i,n)},\tilde{Y}_{k\gamma_{i}}^{(i,n)})_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
is defined in (
S80
).

Lemma S24
.

Assume
1
-
2
and let
\boldsymbol{\gamma}\in(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{*})^{b}
such that, for any
i\in[b],\gamma_{i}<1/\tilde{M}_{i}
.
Then, for any
n,k\in\mathbb{N}
, the matrix
(\mathbf{I}_{p}-\mathbf{C}_{k}^{(n)})
is invertible and in addition, for any
i\in[b]
, we have

\|\mathbf{I}_{d_{i}}-\mathbf{C}^{(i,n)}_{k}\|\leq 1-\gamma_{i}\tilde{m_{i}}\;,

where
\mathbf{C}^{(i,n)}_{k}
is defined in (
S90
).

Proof.

Let
i\in[b],n,k\in\mathbb{N}
.
By
2
, we have
\|\nabla^{2}U_{i}\|\leq M_{i}
which implies by (
S90
) that
\|\mathbf{C}^{(i,n)}_{k}\|\leq\gamma_{i}\tilde{M}_{i}
.
Since
\gamma_{i}<1/\tilde{M}_{i}
, the matrix
\mathbf{I}_{p}-\mathbf{C}_{k}^{(i,n)}
is invertible and so is
\mathbf{I}_{p}-\mathbf{C}_{k}^{(n)}
.
In addition, following the same lines as the proof of
Lemma

S9
implies
\|\mathbf{I}_{d_{i}}-\mathbf{C}^{(i,n)}_{k}\|\leq\max\{\textstyle|{1-\gamma_{i}\tilde{m_{i}}}|,\textstyle|{1-\gamma_{i}\tilde{M}_{i}}|\}=1-\gamma_{i}\tilde{m_{i}}
.
∎

For any
n,k\in\mathbb{N},i\in[b]
, if
\gamma_{i}\in(0,1/\tilde{M}_{i})
,
Lemma

S24
shows the invertibility of the matrices
\mathbf{I}_{p}-\mathbf{C}_{k}^{(n)}
.
Therefore,
\mathbf{M}_{\infty}^{(n)}
is invertible and we can define

\displaystyle\mathbf{\mathrm{T}}_{1}^{(n)}=[\mathbf{M}_{\infty}^{(n)}]^{-1}+\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}[\mathbf{M}_{\infty}^{(n)}]^{-1}\mathbf{M}_{k+1}^{(n)}\mathbf{J}(k,0)\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{N}}^{-{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}/\boldsymbol{\rho}}^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\mathbf{P}_{0}\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}/\boldsymbol{\rho}}^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{N}}^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\;,

(S91)

\displaystyle\mathbf{\mathrm{T}}_{2}^{(n)}=\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\Big\{{[\mathbf{M}_{\infty}^{(n)}]^{-1}\mathbf{M}_{k+1}^{(n)}\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{N}\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{-{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\int_{0}^{+\infty}\mathbf{J}(k,l)[\nabla V(\tilde{Y}_{k\boldsymbol{\gamma}+l}^{(n)})-\nabla V(\tilde{Y}_{k\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{(n)})]\,\mathrm{d}l}\Big\}\;.

(S92)

Using these matrices, we have the following result.

Lemma S25
.

Assume
1
-
2
and let
\boldsymbol{N}\in(\mathbb{N}^{*})^{b},\boldsymbol{\gamma}\in(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{*})^{b}
such that, for any
i\in[b],\gamma_{i}<1/\tilde{M}_{i}
.
Then, for any
n\geq 1
,

\displaystyle\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{N}\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{-{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}(\tilde{Z}_{n+1}-Z_{n+1})=\mathbf{\mathrm{T}}_{1}^{(n)}(\tilde{Z}_{n}-Z_{n})-\mathbf{\mathrm{T}}_{2}^{(n)}\;,

(S93)

where
(Z_{n},\tilde{Z}_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
is defined in (
S79
) and
\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{N}\boldsymbol{\gamma}}=\mathrm{diag}(N_{1}\gamma_{1}\mathbf{I}_{d_{1}},\ldots,N_{b}\gamma_{b}\mathbf{I}_{d_{b}})\in\mathbb{R}^{p\times p}
.

Proof.

Let
i\in[b]
and
n\geq 1
.
Recall that
V_{i}
is defined in (
S20
) and for
\mathbf{z}\in\mathbb{R}^{p}
, denote
V(\mathbf{z})=\sum_{i=1}^{b}V_{i}(\mathbf{z}_{i})
. For any
k\in\mathbb{N}
, we have

\nabla V_{i}(\tilde{Y}_{k\gamma_{i}}^{(i,n)})-\nabla V_{i}(Y_{k\gamma_{i}}^{(i,n)})=\bigg[{\int_{0}^{1}\nabla^{2}V_{i}((1-s)Y_{k\gamma_{i}}^{(i,n)}+s\tilde{Y}_{k\gamma_{i}}^{(i,n)})\,\mathrm{d}s}\bigg](\tilde{Y}_{k\gamma_{i}}^{(i,n)}-Y_{k\gamma_{i}}^{(i,n)})\;.

For
k\geq 0
, it follows from (
S80
) that

\displaystyle\tilde{Y}_{(k+1)\gamma_{i}}^{(i,n)}-Y_{(k+1)\gamma_{i}}^{(i,n)}

\displaystyle=\bigg({\mathbf{I}_{d_{i}}-\gamma_{i}\int_{0}^{1}\nabla^{2}V_{i}((1-s)Y_{k\gamma_{i}}^{(i,n)}+s\tilde{Y}_{k\gamma_{i}}^{(i,n)})\,\mathrm{d}s}\bigg)(\tilde{Y}_{k\gamma_{i}}^{(i,n)}-Y_{k\gamma_{i}}^{(i,n)})

(S94)

\displaystyle-\int_{0}^{\gamma_{i}}\big[\nabla V_{i}(\tilde{Y}_{k\gamma_{i}+l}^{(i,n)})-\nabla V_{i}(\tilde{Y}_{k\gamma_{i}}^{(i,n)})\big]\,\mathrm{d}l+(\gamma_{i}/\rho_{i})\mathbf{A}_{i}(\tilde{\theta}_{n}-\theta_{n})\;.

Consider the process
(\tilde{\mathrm{Y}}_{t}^{(n)},\mathrm{Y}_{t}^{(n)})_{t\in\mathbb{R}_{+}}
valued in
\mathbb{R}^{p}\times\mathbb{R}^{p}
and defined for any
t\geq 0
by

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathrm{Y}}_{t}^{(n)}=\tilde{Y}_{\min(t,N_{i}\gamma_{i})}^{(n)}\;,

\displaystyle\mathrm{Y}_{t}^{(n)}=Y_{\min(t,N_{i}\gamma_{i})}^{(n)}\;.

(S95)

The process (
S95
) is continuous with respect to
t
and defined so that its component
(\tilde{\mathrm{Y}}_{t}^{(i,n)},\mathrm{Y}_{t}^{(i,n)})
equals
(\tilde{Y}_{t}^{i},Y_{t}^{i})
for
t\leq N_{i}\gamma_{i}
and is constant for
t>N_{i}\gamma_{i}
.
For
l\geq 0
, we write
(\tilde{\mathrm{Y}}_{k\boldsymbol{\gamma}+l}^{(n)},\mathrm{Y}_{k\boldsymbol{\gamma}+l}^{(n)})=(\tilde{\mathrm{Y}}_{k\gamma_{i}+l}^{(i,n)},\mathrm{Y}_{k\gamma_{i}+l}^{(i,n)})_{i\in[b]}\in\mathbb{R}^{p}\times\mathbb{R}^{p}
.
Using the matrices defined in (
S89
), for
k\in\mathbb{N}
, we obtain

\tilde{\mathrm{Y}}_{(k+1)\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{(n)}-\mathrm{Y}_{(k+1)\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{(n)}=(\mathbf{I}_{p}-\mathbf{C}_{k}^{(n)})(\tilde{\mathrm{Y}}_{k\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{(n)}-\mathrm{Y}_{k\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{(n)})-\textstyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathbf{J}(k,l)\big[\nabla V(\tilde{\mathrm{Y}}_{k\boldsymbol{\gamma}+l}^{(n)})-\nabla V(\tilde{\mathrm{Y}}_{k\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{(n)})\big]\,\mathrm{d}l\\
+\mathbf{J}(k,0)\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}/\sqrt{\boldsymbol{\rho}}}\mathbf{P}_{0}\mathbf{\tilde{D}}_{0}^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}(\tilde{\mathrm{Y}}_{0}^{(n)}-\mathrm{Y}_{0}^{(n)})\;,

(S96)

where
\mathbf{P}_{0}
is defined in (
S3
).
Recall the matrix
\mathbf{M}_{k}^{(n)}
defined in (
S89
) with
\mathbf{M}_{0}^{(n)}=\mathbf{I}_{p}
and for
k\geq 1
,

\mathbf{M}_{k}^{(n)}=(\mathbf{I}_{p}-\mathbf{C}_{0}^{(n)})^{-1}\ldots(\mathbf{I}_{p}-\mathbf{C}_{k-1}^{(n)})^{-1}
.
By multiplying (
S96
) by
\mathbf{M}_{k+1}^{(n)}\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{N}\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{-{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbf{M}_{k+1}^{(n)}\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{N}\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{-{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}(\tilde{\mathrm{Y}}_{(k+1)\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{(n)}-\mathrm{Y}_{(k+1)\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{(n)})

\displaystyle=\mathbf{M}_{k}^{(n)}\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{N}\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{-{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}(\tilde{\mathrm{Y}}_{k\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{(n)}-\mathrm{Y}_{k\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{(n)})

\displaystyle-\mathbf{M}_{k+1}^{(n)}\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{N}\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{-{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathbf{J}(k,l)\big[\nabla V(\tilde{Y}_{k\boldsymbol{\gamma}+l}^{(n)})-\nabla V(\tilde{Y}_{k\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{(n)})\big]\,\mathrm{d}l

\displaystyle+\mathbf{M}_{k+1}^{(n)}\mathbf{J}(k,0)\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{N}}^{-{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}/\boldsymbol{\rho}}^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\mathbf{P}_{0}\mathbf{\tilde{D}}_{0}^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}(\tilde{\mathrm{Y}}_{0}^{(n)}-\mathrm{Y}_{0}^{(n)})\;.

By (
S95
) and (
S79
), we have for
t\geq\max_{i\in[b]}\{\gamma_{i}N_{i}\}
,
(\tilde{Z}_{n+1},Z_{n+1})=(\tilde{\mathrm{Y}}_{t},\mathrm{Y}_{t})
. Therefore, summing the previous expression over
k
, we get

\displaystyle\mathbf{M}_{\infty}^{(n)}

\displaystyle\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{N}\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{-{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}(\tilde{Z}_{n+1}-Z_{n+1})=-\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\mathbf{M}_{k+1}^{(n)}\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{N}\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{-{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathbf{J}(k,l)[\nabla V(\tilde{Y}_{k\boldsymbol{\gamma}+l}^{(n)})-\nabla V(\tilde{Y}_{k\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{(n)})]\,\mathrm{d}l

\displaystyle+\bigg[{\mathbf{M}_{0}^{(n)}+\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\mathbf{M}_{k+1}^{(n)}\mathbf{J}(k,0)\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{N}}^{-{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}/\boldsymbol{\rho}}^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\mathbf{P}_{0}\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}/\boldsymbol{\rho}}^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{N}}^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}}\bigg]\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{N}\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{-{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\cdot(\tilde{Z}_{n}-Z_{n})\;.

By
Lemma

S24
,
\mathbf{M}_{\infty}^{(n)}
is invertible and the proof is concluded by multiplying the previous equality by
[\mathbf{M}_{\infty}^{(n)}]^{-1}
.
∎

Based on
Lemma

S25
, we have the following relation between
\|\tilde{Z}_{n+1}-Z_{n+1}\|^{2}
and
\|\tilde{Z}_{n}-Z_{n}\|^{2}
.

Lemma S26
.

Assume
1
-
2
and let
\boldsymbol{N}\in(\mathbb{N}^{*})^{b},\boldsymbol{\gamma}\in(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{*})^{b}
such that, for any
i\in[b],\gamma_{i}<1/\tilde{M}_{i}
.
Then, for any
\epsilon>0
and
n\geq 1
,

\|\tilde{Z}_{n+1}-Z_{n+1}\|_{\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{N}\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{-1}}^{2}\leq(1+2\epsilon)\|{\mathbf{\mathrm{T}}_{1}^{(n)}}\|^{2}\|\tilde{Z}_{n}-Z_{n}\|_{\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{N}\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{-1}}^{2}+(1+1/\{2\epsilon\})\|{\mathbf{\mathrm{T}}_{2}^{(n)}}\|^{2}\;.

where
(Z_{n},\tilde{Z}_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
is defined in (
S79
) and
\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{N}\boldsymbol{\gamma}}=\mathrm{diag}(N_{1}\gamma_{1}\mathbf{I}_{d_{1}},\ldots,N_{b}\gamma_{b}\mathbf{I}_{d_{b}})\in\mathbb{R}^{p\times p}
.

Proof.

The proof follows from
Lemma

S25
and by using the fact that for
\mathbf{a},\mathbf{b}\in\mathbb{R}^{p},\epsilon>0
we have
2\langle\mathbf{a},\mathbf{b}\rangle\leq 2\epsilon\|\mathbf{a}\|^{2}+(1/\{{2\epsilon}\})\|\mathbf{b}\|^{2}
.
∎

Similarly to
Lemma

S10
, we have the following result regarding the contracting term.

Lemma S27
.

Assume
1
-
2
and let
\boldsymbol{N}\in(\mathbb{N}^{*})^{b},\boldsymbol{\gamma}\in(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{*})^{b}
such that, for any
i\in[b]
,
\gamma_{i}<1/\tilde{M}_{i}
and
N_{i}\gamma_{i}\leq 2/(m_{i}+\tilde{M}_{i})
. Then, for any
n\geq 0
, we have

\displaystyle\|{\mathbf{\mathrm{T}}_{1}^{(n)}}\|

\displaystyle\leq 1-\min_{i\in[b]}\{N_{i}\gamma_{i}m_{i}\}+r_{\boldsymbol{\gamma},\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{N}}\;,

where
\mathbf{\mathrm{T}}_{1}^{(n)}
and
r_{\boldsymbol{\gamma},\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{N}}
are defined in (
S91
) and (
S33
), respectively.

Proof.

The proof is similar to the proof of
Lemma

S10
and therefore is omitted.
∎

In the next lemma, we upper bound the coefficient
r_{\boldsymbol{\gamma},\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{N}}
defined in (
S33
). For this, we explicit a choice of
\boldsymbol{N}
that we denote
\boldsymbol{N}^{\star}=(N_{1}^{\star}(\gamma_{1}),\ldots,N_{b}^{\star}(\gamma_{b}))\in(\mathbb{N}^{*})^{b}
defined for any
i\in[b]
, any
\gamma_{i}>0
, by

N_{i}^{\star}(\gamma_{i})=\big\lfloor m_{i}\min_{i\in[b]}\{{m_{i}/\tilde{M}_{i}}\}^{2}/\big({20\gamma_{i}\tilde{M}_{i}^{2}\max_{i\in[b]}\{{m_{i}/\tilde{M}_{i}}\}^{2}}\big)\big\rfloor\;,

(S97)

where
\tilde{M}_{i}=M_{i}+1/\rho_{i}
.

Lemma S28
.

Assume
1
-
2
and let
\boldsymbol{\gamma}\in(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{*})^{b}
such that, for any
i\in[b]
,

\gamma_{i}\leq\frac{m_{i}}{40\tilde{M}_{i}^{2}}\left({\frac{\min_{i\in[b]}\{{{m_{i}}/{\tilde{M}_{i}}}\}}{\max_{i\in[b]}\{{{m_{i}}/{\tilde{M}_{i}}}\}}}\right)^{2}\;.

Then, for any
i\in[b]
, we have
N_{i}^{\star}(\gamma_{i})\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
and

r_{\boldsymbol{\gamma},\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{N}^{\star}}<\min_{i\in[b]}\{N_{i}^{\star}(\gamma_{i})\gamma_{i}m_{i}\}/2\;,

where
r_{\boldsymbol{\gamma},\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{N}^{\star}}
is defined in (
S33
).

Proof.

The assumption on
\gamma_{i}
combined with the definition (
S97
) of
N_{i}^{\star}(\gamma_{i})
imply
N_{i}^{\star}(\gamma_{i})\geq 2
, using in addition
m_{i}\leq M_{i}
,
\max_{i\in[b]}\{N_{i}^{\star}(\gamma_{i})\gamma_{i}\tilde{M}_{i}\mathds{1}_{N_{i}^{\star}(\gamma_{i})>1}\}\leq 1/20
and

\displaystyle\frac{1}{20}\bigg({\frac{\min_{i\in[b]}\{{m_{i}/\tilde{M}_{i}}\}}{\max_{i\in[b]}\{{m_{i}/\tilde{M}_{i}}\}}}\bigg)^{2}\geq\frac{N_{i}^{\star}(\gamma_{i})\gamma_{i}\tilde{M}_{i}^{2}}{m_{i}}

\displaystyle>\frac{1}{20}\bigg({\frac{\min_{i\in[b]}\{{m_{i}/\tilde{M}_{i}}\}}{\max_{i\in[b]}\{{m_{i}/\tilde{M}_{i}}\}}}\bigg)^{2}-\frac{\gamma_{i}\tilde{M}_{i}^{2}}{m_{i}}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{40}\bigg({\frac{\min_{i\in[b]}\{{m_{i}/\tilde{M}_{i}}\}}{\max_{i\in[b]}\{{m_{i}/\tilde{M}_{i}}\}}}\bigg)^{2}\;.

(S98)

Using the definition (
S33
) of
r_{\boldsymbol{\gamma},\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{N}}
, we have

r_{\boldsymbol{\gamma},\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{N}}<5\max_{i\in[b]}\{N_{i}^{\star}(\gamma_{i})\gamma_{i}\tilde{M}_{i}\mathds{1}_{N_{i}^{\star}(\gamma_{i})>1}\}^{2}.

Thus, plugging (
S98
) in the previous inequality gives

r_{\boldsymbol{\gamma},\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{N}}\leq\max_{i\in[b]}\{m_{i}/\tilde{M}_{i}\}^{2}\max_{i\in[b]}\left\{\frac{N_{i}^{\star}(\gamma_{i})\gamma_{i}\tilde{M}_{i}^{2}}{m_{i}}\right\}<\frac{\min_{i\in[b]}\{{m_{i}/\tilde{M}_{i}}\}^{4}}{80\max_{i\in[b]}\{{m_{i}/\tilde{M}_{i}}\}^{2}}\;.

(S99)

In addition, (
S98
) also shows that

\frac{1}{40}\bigg({\frac{\min_{i\in[b]}\{{m_{i}/\tilde{M}_{i}}\}}{\max_{i\in[b]}\{{m_{i}/\tilde{M}_{i}}\}}}\bigg)^{2}\bigg({\frac{m_{i}}{\tilde{M}_{i}}}\bigg)^{2}\leq N_{i}^{\star}(\gamma_{i})\gamma_{i}m_{i}\;.

(S100)

Therefore, combining (
S99
) and (
S100
) completes the proof.
∎

S4.2
Proof of
Proposition

4

We first give the formal statement of
Proposition

4
.

Proposition S29
.

Assume
1
-
2
and let
\boldsymbol{\gamma}\in(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{*})^{b}
,
\boldsymbol{N}\in(\mathbb{N}^{*})^{b}
such that for any
i\in[b]
,

\textstyle\gamma_{i}\leq{m_{i}}/{40\tilde{M}_{i}^{2}}(\min_{i\in[b]}\{m_{i}/\tilde{M}_{i}\}/\max_{i\in[b]}\{m_{i}/\tilde{M}_{i}\})^{2}
and
N_{i}=\lfloor m_{i}\min_{i\in[b]}\{{m_{i}/\tilde{M}_{i}}\}^{2}/(20\gamma_{i}\tilde{M}_{i}^{2}\max_{i\in[b]}\{{m_{i}/\tilde{M}_{i}^{2}}\})\rfloor
.
Then, we have

W_{2}^{2}({\Pi_{\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{\gamma},\boldsymbol{N}},\Pi_{\boldsymbol{\rho}}})\leq\frac{4(1+\|\bar{\mathbf{B}}_{0}^{-1}\mathbf{B}_{0}^{\top}\mathbf{\tilde{D}}_{0}^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\|^{2})\max_{i\in[b]}\{{{m_{i}}/{\tilde{M}_{i}^{2}}}\}}{5\min_{i\in[b]}\{{{m_{i}}/{\tilde{M}_{i}}}\}^{2}\max_{i\in[b]}\{{{m_{i}}/{\tilde{M}_{i}}}\}^{2}}\\
\times\sum_{i=1}^{b}d_{i}\gamma_{i}m_{i}(1+{\gamma_{i}^{2}\tilde{M}_{i}^{2}}/{12}+\gamma_{i}\tilde{M}_{i}^{2}/(2\tilde{m}_{i}))\;,

where
\bar{\mathbf{B}}_{0},\mathbf{B}_{0},\mathbf{\tilde{D}}_{0}
are defined in (
S2
)-(
S3
), and for any
i\in[b]
,
\tilde{m}_{i}
,
\tilde{M}_{i}
are defined in (
S25
).

By
Lemma

S23
and
Lemma

S26
, we can note that the proof of
Proposition

S29
boils down to derive an upper bound on
\|{\mathbf{\mathrm{T}}_{2}^{(n)}}\|^{2}
defined in (
S92
) for
n\in\mathbb{N}
.
The following lemma provides such a bound.

Lemma S30
.

Assume
1
-
2
and let
\boldsymbol{N}\in(\mathbb{N}^{*})^{b},\boldsymbol{\gamma}\in(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{*})^{b}
such that, for any
i\in[b]
,
\gamma_{i}<1/\tilde{M}_{i}
. Then, for any
n\in\mathbb{N}
, we have

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}\left[{\|{\mathbf{\mathrm{T}}_{2}^{(n)}}\|^{2}}\right]}

\displaystyle\leq{\sum_{i=1}^{b}}d_{i}N_{i}\gamma_{i}^{2}\tilde{M}_{i}^{2}\left[{1+{\gamma_{i}^{2}\tilde{M}_{i}^{2}}/{12}+\gamma_{i}\tilde{M}_{i}^{2}/(2\tilde{m}_{i})}\right]\;,

where
\tilde{m}_{i},\tilde{M}_{i},\mathrm{T}_{2}^{(n)}
are defined in (
S25
) and (
S92
), respectively.

Proof.

Let
n\in\mathbb{N}
.
Using (
S86
), we can write, for any
l\in\mathbb{R}_{+}
and
k\in\mathbb{N}
,
\mathbf{J}(k,l)
as a block-diagonal matrix
\mathrm{diag}(\mathbf{J}^{1}(k,l),\ldots,\mathbf{J}^{b}(k,l))
with
\mathbf{J}^{i}(k,l)=\mathds{1}_{[N_{i}]}(k+1)\mathds{1}_{[0,\gamma_{i}]}(s)\cdot\mathbf{I}_{d_{i}}
for any
i\in[b]
.
By (
S89
) and using for any
k\in\mathbb{N}
, that
[\mathbf{M}_{\infty}^{(n)}]^{-1}\mathbf{M}_{k+1}^{(n)}=\prod_{l=k+1}^{\infty}(\mathbf{I}_{d_{i}}-\mathbf{C}^{(i,n)}_{l})
is finite by (
S88
), we have

\displaystyle\|{\mathbf{\mathrm{T}}_{2}^{(n)}}\|^{2}

\displaystyle=\Big\|{\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}[\mathbf{M}_{\infty}^{(n)}]^{-1}\mathbf{M}_{k+1}^{(n)}\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{N}\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{-{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathbf{J}(k,l)\big[\nabla V(\tilde{Y}_{k\boldsymbol{\gamma}+l}^{(n)})-\nabla V(\tilde{Y}_{k\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{(n)})\big]\,\mathrm{d}l}\Big\|^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{b}\frac{1}{N_{i}\gamma_{i}}\Big\|{\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\prod_{l=k+1}^{\infty}(\mathbf{I}_{d_{i}}-\mathbf{C}^{(i,n)}_{l})\int_{0}^{\gamma_{i}}\mathbf{J}^{i}(k,0)\big[\nabla V_{i}(\tilde{Y}_{k\gamma_{i}+l}^{(i,n)})-\nabla V_{i}(\tilde{Y}_{k\gamma_{i}}^{(i,n)})\big]\,\mathrm{d}l}\Big\|^{2}\;.

(S101)

Since for any
i\in[b]
,
k\geq N_{i}
we have
\mathbf{J}^{i}(k,0)=\mathbf{C}^{(i,n)}_{l}=\mathbf{0}_{d_{i}\times d_{i}}
, (
S101
) can be rewritten as

\|{\mathbf{\mathrm{T}}_{2}^{(n)}}\|^{2}=\sum_{i=1}^{b}\frac{1}{N_{i}\gamma_{i}}\Big\|{\sum_{k=0}^{N_{i}-1}\prod_{l=k+1}^{N_{i}-1}(\mathbf{I}_{d_{i}}-\mathbf{C}^{(i,n)}_{l})\int_{0}^{\gamma_{i}}\mathbf{J}^{i}(k,0)\big[\nabla V_{i}(\tilde{Y}_{k\gamma_{i}+l}^{(i,n)})-\nabla V_{i}(\tilde{Y}_{k\gamma_{i}}^{(i,n)})\big]\,\mathrm{d}l}\Big\|^{2}\;,

and the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality gives

\|{\mathbf{\mathrm{T}}_{2}^{(n)}}\|^{2}\leq\sum_{i=1}^{b}\frac{1}{\gamma_{i}}\left({\sum_{k=0}^{N_{i}-1}\Big\|{\prod_{l=k+1}^{N_{i}-1}(\mathbf{I}_{d_{i}}-\mathbf{C}^{(i,n)}_{l})}\Big\|^{2}\Big\|{\int_{0}^{\gamma_{i}}\left[{\nabla V_{i}(\tilde{Y}_{k\gamma_{i}+l}^{(i,n)})-\nabla V_{i}(\tilde{Y}_{k\gamma_{i}}^{(i,n)})}\right]\,\mathrm{d}l}\Big\|^{2}}\right)\;.

(S102)

Since, for any
i\in[b]
,
\gamma_{i}\tilde{M}_{i}<1
, we get using
Lemma

S24
,

\displaystyle\mathinner{\!\left\lVert\prod_{l=k+1}^{N_{i}-1}(\mathbf{I}_{d_{i}}-\mathbf{C}^{(i,n)}_{l})\right\rVert}^{2}\leq\{1-\gamma_{i}\tilde{m}_{i}\}^{2(N_{i}-k-1)}\;.

By combining (
S102
) with the previous result and the Jensen inequality, we have

\|{\mathbf{\mathrm{T}}_{2}^{(n)}}\|^{2}\leq\sum_{i=1}^{b}\sum_{k=0}^{N_{i}-1}\{1-\gamma_{i}\tilde{m}_{i}\}^{2(N_{i}-k-1)}\int_{0}^{\gamma_{i}}\mathinner{\!\left\lVert\nabla V_{i}(\tilde{Y}_{k\gamma_{i}+l}^{(i,n)})-\nabla V_{i}(\tilde{Y}_{k\gamma_{i}}^{(i,n)})\right\rVert}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}l\;.

(S103)

For
i\in[b]
, using
Durmus and
Moulines 2019
applied to the potential
V_{i}^{\theta}\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}\mathbf{y}^{i}\mapsto U_{i}(\mathbf{y}^{i})+\|\mathbf{y}^{i}-\mathbf{A}_{i}\theta\|^{2}/(2\rho_{i})
yields

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\gamma_{i}}\mathbb{E}^{\mathcal{F}_{k\gamma_{i}}^{(n)}}\|{\nabla V_{i}(\tilde{Y}_{k\gamma_{i}+l}^{(i,n)})-\nabla V_{i}(\tilde{Y}_{k\gamma_{i}}^{(i,n)})}\|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}l=\int_{0}^{\gamma_{i}}\mathbb{E}^{\mathcal{F}_{k\gamma_{i}}^{(n)}}\|{\nabla V_{i}^{\tilde{\theta}_{n}}(\tilde{Y}_{k\gamma_{i}+l}^{(i,n)})-\nabla V_{i}^{\tilde{\theta}_{n}}(\tilde{Y}_{k\gamma_{i}}^{(i,n)})}\|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}l

\displaystyle\leq\gamma_{i}^{2}\tilde{M}_{i}^{2}\left[{d_{i}+{d_{i}\gamma_{i}^{2}\tilde{M}_{i}^{2}}/{12}+({\gamma_{i}\tilde{M}_{i}^{2}}/{2})\|\tilde{Y}_{k\gamma_{i}}^{(i,n)}-\mathbf{z}_{n,\star}^{i}\|^{2}}\right]\;,

(S104)

where
\mathbf{z}_{n,\star}^{i}=\arg\min_{\mathbf{z}_{i}\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{i}}}V_{i}^{\tilde{\theta}_{n}}(\mathbf{z}_{i})
.

By (
S104
), (
S84
),
Lemma

S22
and since
\max_{i\in[b]}\gamma_{i}\tilde{m}_{i}<1
, we get

\sum_{i=1}^{b}\sum_{k=0}^{N_{i}-1}\{1-\gamma_{i}\tilde{m}_{i}\}^{2(N_{i}-k-1)}\int_{0}^{\gamma_{i}}\mathbb{E}\|\nabla V_{i}(\tilde{Y}_{k\gamma_{i}+l}^{(i,n)})-\nabla V_{i}(\tilde{Y}_{k\gamma_{i}}^{(i,n)})\|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}l\\
\leq\sum_{i=1}^{b}d_{i}N_{i}\gamma_{i}^{2}\tilde{M}_{i}^{2}[1+{\gamma_{i}^{2}\tilde{M}_{i}^{2}}/{12}+{\gamma_{i}\tilde{M}_{i}^{2}}/{(2\tilde{m}_{i})}]\;.

Combining this result with (
S103
) completes the proof.
∎

We can now combine
Lemma

S30
and
Lemma

S27
with
Lemma

S26
to get the following bound.

Lemma S31
.

Assume
1
-
2
and let
\boldsymbol{N}\in(\mathbb{N}^{*})^{b},\boldsymbol{\gamma}\in(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{*})^{b}
such that, for any
i\in[b]
,
\gamma_{i}<1/\tilde{M}_{i}
,
N_{i}\gamma_{i}\leq 2/(m_{i}+\tilde{M}_{i})
.
Suppose in addition
\upkappa_{\boldsymbol{\gamma},\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{N}}=\min_{i\in[b]}\{N_{i}\gamma_{i}m_{i}\}-r_{\boldsymbol{\gamma},\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{N}}\in\left(0,1\right)
, where
r_{\boldsymbol{\gamma},\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{N}}
is defined in (
S33
).
Then, for
n\geq 1
, we have

\mathbb{E}\Big[{\|{\tilde{Z}_{n}-Z_{n}}\|_{\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{N}\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{-1}}^{2}}\Big]\leq(1-\upkappa_{\boldsymbol{\gamma},\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{N}}+\upkappa_{\boldsymbol{\gamma},\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{N}}^{2}/2)^{2(n-1)}\mathbb{E}\Big[{\|{\tilde{Z}_{1}-Z_{1}}\|_{\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{N}\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{-1}}^{2}}\Big]\\
+2\upkappa_{\boldsymbol{\gamma},\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{N}}^{-2}\sum_{i=1}^{b}d_{i}N_{i}\gamma_{i}^{2}\tilde{M}_{i}^{2}\bigg({1+\frac{\gamma_{i}^{2}\tilde{M}_{i}^{2}}{12}+\frac{\gamma_{i}\tilde{M}_{i}^{2}}{2\tilde{m}_{i}}}\bigg)\;,

where, for any
i\in[b]
,
\tilde{M}_{i}
and
\tilde{m}_{i}
are defined in (
S25
).

Proof.

Taking expectation in
Lemma

S26
, we get for any
n\in\mathbb{N},\epsilon>0
that

\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathinner{\!\left\lVert\tilde{Z}_{n+1}-Z_{n+1}\right\rVert}_{\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{N}\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{-1}}^{2}}\right]\leq(1+2\epsilon)\mathbb{E}\left[{\|{\mathbf{\mathrm{T}}_{1}^{(n)}}\|^{2}\|{\tilde{Z}_{n}-Z_{n}}\|_{\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{N}\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{-1}}^{2}}\right]+(1+1/\{2\epsilon\})\mathbb{E}\left[{\|{\mathbf{\mathrm{T}}_{2}^{(n)}}\|^{2}}\right]\;,

where
\mathbf{\mathrm{T}}_{1}^{(n)}
and
\mathbf{\mathrm{T}}_{2}^{(n)}
are defined in (
S91
) and (
S92
), respectively.
To ease notation, denote
\mathrm{B}=\sum_{i=1}^{b}d_{i}N_{i}\gamma_{i}^{2}\tilde{M}_{i}^{2}(1+{\gamma_{i}^{2}\tilde{M}_{i}^{2}}/{12}+\gamma_{i}\tilde{M}_{i}^{2}/(2\tilde{m}_{i}))
.
Using
Lemma

S30
, we obtain for any
n\in\mathbb{N},\epsilon>0

\mathbb{E}\left[{\|{\tilde{Z}_{n+1}-Z_{n+1}}\|_{\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{N}\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{-1}}^{2}}\right]\leq(1+2\epsilon)\mathbb{E}\left[{\|{\mathrm{T}_{1}^{(n)}}\|^{2}\|{\tilde{Z}_{n}-Z_{n}}\|_{\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{N}\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{-1}}^{2}}\right]+(1+1/\{2\epsilon\})\mathrm{B}\;.

(S105)

In addition,
Lemma

S27
implies that

\|{\mathbf{\mathrm{T}}_{1}^{(n)}}\|^{2}\leq(1-\upkappa_{\boldsymbol{\gamma},\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{N}})^{2}
almost surely.
Therefore, taking
\epsilon=(1-[1-\upkappa_{\boldsymbol{\gamma},\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{N}}]^{2})/(4[1-\upkappa_{\boldsymbol{\gamma},\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{N}}]^{2})
, (
S105
) yields for any
n\geq 0
,

\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathinner{\!\left\lVert\tilde{Z}_{n+1}-Z_{n+1}\right\rVert}_{\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{N}\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{-1}}^{2}}\right]\leq\frac{1+(1-\upkappa_{\boldsymbol{\gamma},\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{N}})^{2}}{2}\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathinner{\!\left\lVert\tilde{Z}_{n}-Z_{n}\right\rVert}_{\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{N}\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{-1}}^{2}}\right]+\frac{1+(1-\upkappa_{\boldsymbol{\gamma},\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{N}})^{2}}{1-(1-\upkappa_{\boldsymbol{\gamma},\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{N}})^{2}}\mathrm{B}\;.

An easy induction implies for any
n\geq 1
,

\mathbb{E}\big[{\|\tilde{Z}_{n}-Z_{n}\|_{\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{N}\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{-1}}^{2}}\big]\leq\bigg({\frac{1+(1-\upkappa_{\boldsymbol{\gamma},\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{N}})^{2}}{2}}\bigg)^{n-1}\mathbb{E}\big[{\|\tilde{Z}_{1}-Z_{1}\|_{\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{N}\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{-1}}^{2}}\big]+2\frac{1+(1-\upkappa_{\boldsymbol{\gamma},\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{N}})^{2}}{(1-(1-\upkappa_{\boldsymbol{\gamma},\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{N}})^{2})^{2}}\mathrm{B}\;.

(S106)

Since
\upkappa_{\boldsymbol{\gamma},\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{N}}^{2}=(\min_{i\in[b]}\{N_{i}\gamma_{i}m_{i}\}+r_{\boldsymbol{\gamma},\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{N}})^{2}
and using
\upkappa_{\boldsymbol{\gamma},\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{N}}^{2}\leq 1
, we obtain

\displaystyle(1+(1-\upkappa_{\boldsymbol{\gamma},\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{N}})^{2})/2=1-\upkappa_{\boldsymbol{\gamma},\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{N}}+\upkappa_{\boldsymbol{\gamma},\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{N}}^{2}/2\;,

\displaystyle(1+(1-\upkappa_{\boldsymbol{\gamma},\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{N}})^{2})/(1-(1-\upkappa_{\boldsymbol{\gamma},\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{N}})^{2})^{2}\leq\upkappa_{\boldsymbol{\gamma},\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{N}}^{-2}\;.

Combining these inequalities with (
S106
) and (
S105
) completes the proof.
∎

Lemma S32
.

Assume
1
-
2
and let
\boldsymbol{N}\in(\mathbb{N}^{*})^{b},\boldsymbol{\gamma}\in(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{*})^{b}
such that, for any
i\in[b]
,
\gamma_{i}<1/\tilde{M}_{i},N_{i}\gamma_{i}\leq 2/(m_{i}+\tilde{M}_{i})
and
\upkappa_{\boldsymbol{\gamma},\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{N}}=\min_{i\in[b]}\{N_{i}\gamma_{i}m_{i}\}-r_{\boldsymbol{\gamma},\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{N}}\in\left(0,1\right)
, where
r_{\boldsymbol{\gamma},\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{N}}
is defined in (
S33
). Then, for any
\mathbf{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{d+p}
and
n\geq 1
, we have

\displaystyle W_{2}^{2}(\updelta_{\mathbf{x}}P_{\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{\gamma},\boldsymbol{N}}^{n},\Pi_{\boldsymbol{\rho}})

\displaystyle\leq(1-\upkappa_{\boldsymbol{\gamma},\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{N}}+\upkappa_{\boldsymbol{\gamma},\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{N}}^{2}/2)^{2(n-1)}(1+\|\bar{\mathbf{B}}_{0}^{-1}\mathbf{B}_{0}^{\top}\mathbf{\tilde{D}}_{0}^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\|^{2})\max_{i\in[b]}\{N_{i}\gamma_{i}\}\mathbb{E}\big[{\|\tilde{Z}_{1}-Z_{1}\|_{\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{N}\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{-1}}^{2}}\big]

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{2(1+\|\bar{\mathbf{B}}_{0}^{-1}\mathbf{B}_{0}^{\top}\mathbf{\tilde{D}}_{0}^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\|^{2})\max_{i\in[b]}\{N_{i}\gamma_{i}\}}{\upkappa_{\boldsymbol{\gamma},\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{N}}^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{b}d_{i}N_{i}\gamma_{i}^{2}\tilde{M}_{i}^{2}[1+{\gamma_{i}^{2}\tilde{M}_{i}^{2}}/{12}+\gamma_{i}\tilde{M}_{i}^{2}/(2\tilde{m}_{i})]\;,

where
\bar{\mathbf{B}}_{0},\mathbf{B}_{0},\mathbf{\tilde{D}}_{0}
are defined in (
S2
)-(
S3
),
P_{\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{\gamma},\boldsymbol{N}}
is defined in (
S17
),
(\tilde{Z}_{n},Z_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
is defined in (
S79
) and for any
i\in[b]
,
\tilde{M}_{i}
,
\tilde{m}_{i}
are defined in (
S25
).

Proof.

By
Lemma

S31
, we have the following upper bound for
n\geq 1
,

\mathbb{E}\Big[{\|{\tilde{Z}_{n}-Z_{n}}\|_{\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{N}\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{-1}}^{2}}\Big]\leq(1-\upkappa_{\boldsymbol{\gamma},\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{N}}+\upkappa_{\boldsymbol{\gamma},\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{N}}^{2}/2)^{2(n-1)}\mathbb{E}\Big[{\|{\tilde{Z}_{1}-Z_{1}}\|_{\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{N}\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{-1}}^{2}}\Big]\\
+2\upkappa_{\boldsymbol{\gamma},\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{N}}^{-2}\sum_{i=1}^{b}d_{i}N_{i}\gamma_{i}^{2}\tilde{M}_{i}^{2}\bigg({1+\frac{\gamma_{i}^{2}\tilde{M}_{i}^{2}}{12}+\frac{\gamma_{i}\tilde{M}_{i}^{2}}{2\tilde{m}_{i}}}\bigg)\;.

Using (
S79
),
Lemma

S23
, combined with the previous inequality, we get for any
n\geq 1,\mathbf{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{d+p}
,

\displaystyle W_{2}^{2}(\Pi_{\boldsymbol{\rho}},\updelta_{\mathbf{x}}P_{\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{\gamma},\boldsymbol{N}}^{n})

\displaystyle\leq(1+\|\bar{\mathbf{B}}_{0}^{-1}\mathbf{B}_{0}^{\top}\mathbf{\tilde{D}}_{0}^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\|^{2})\mathbb{E}\big[{\|{\tilde{Z}_{n}-Z_{n}}\|^{2}}\big]

\displaystyle\leq(1+\|\bar{\mathbf{B}}_{0}^{-1}\mathbf{B}_{0}^{\top}\mathbf{\tilde{D}}_{0}^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\|^{2})\max_{i\in[b]}\{N_{i}\gamma_{i}\}\mathbb{E}\Big[{\|{\tilde{Z}_{n}-Z_{n}}\|_{\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{N}\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{-1}}^{2}}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq(1-\upkappa_{\boldsymbol{\gamma},\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{N}}+\upkappa_{\boldsymbol{\gamma},\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{N}}^{2}/2)^{2(n-1)}(1+\|\bar{\mathbf{B}}_{0}^{-1}\mathbf{B}_{0}^{\top}\mathbf{\tilde{D}}_{0}^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\|^{2})\max_{i\in[b]}\{N_{i}\gamma_{i}\}\mathbb{E}\Big[{\|{\tilde{Z}_{1}-Z_{1}}\|_{\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{N}\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{-1}}^{2}}\Big]

\displaystyle+\frac{2(1+\|\bar{\mathbf{B}}_{0}^{-1}\mathbf{B}_{0}^{\top}\mathbf{\tilde{D}}_{0}^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\|^{2})\max_{i\in[b]}\{N_{i}\gamma_{i}\}}{\upkappa_{\boldsymbol{\gamma},\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{N}}^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{b}d_{i}N_{i}\gamma_{i}^{2}\tilde{M}_{i}^{2}\Big({1+\frac{\gamma_{i}^{2}\tilde{M}_{i}^{2}}{12}+\frac{\gamma_{i}\tilde{M}_{i}^{2}}{2\tilde{m}_{i}}}\Big)\;.

Hence the stated result.
∎

Proof of
Proposition

4
/
Proposition

S29
.

Proof.

Since for any
i\in[b]
,
\textstyle\gamma_{i}\leq{m_{i}}/{40\tilde{M}_{i}^{2}}(\min_{i\in[b]}\{m_{i}/\tilde{M}_{i}\}/\max_{i\in[b]}\{m_{i}/\tilde{M}_{i}\})^{2}
, setting

N_{i}^{\star}(\gamma_{i})=\big\lfloor m_{i}\min_{i\in[b]}\{{m_{i}/\tilde{M}_{i}}\}^{2}/\big({20\gamma_{i}\tilde{M}_{i}^{2}\max_{i\in[b]}\{{m_{i}/\tilde{M}_{i}}\}^{2}}\big)\big\rfloor

implies
\upkappa_{\boldsymbol{\gamma},\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{N}^{\star}}\in(0,1)
by

S28
.

n
tend towards infinity in

S32
and using

S13
conclude the proof.
