S4.3
Proof of
Proposition

5

We first give the formal statement of
Proposition

5
.

Proposition S33
.

Assume
1
-
2
-
3
and let
\boldsymbol{\gamma}\in(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{*})^{b}
,
\boldsymbol{N}\in(\mathbb{N}^{*})^{b}
such that for any
i\in[b]
,

\textstyle\gamma_{i}\leq{m_{i}}/{40\tilde{M}_{i}^{2}}(\min_{i\in[b]}\{m_{i}/\tilde{M}_{i}\}/\max_{i\in[b]}\{m_{i}/\tilde{M}_{i}\})^{2}
and
N_{i}=\lfloor m_{i}\min_{i\in[b]}\{{m_{i}/\tilde{M}_{i}}\}^{2}/(20\gamma_{i}\tilde{M}_{i}^{2}\max_{i\in[b]}\{{m_{i}/\tilde{M}_{i}^{2}}\})\rfloor
.
Then, we have

W_{2}^{2}({\Pi_{\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{\gamma},\boldsymbol{N}},\Pi_{\boldsymbol{\rho}}})\leq 4({1+\|{\bar{\mathbf{B}}_{0}^{-1}\mathbf{B}_{0}^{\top}\mathbf{\tilde{D}}_{0}^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}}\|^{2}})\frac{\max_{i\in[b]}\{{m_{i}/\tilde{M}_{i}^{2}}\}}{\min_{i\in[b]}\{{m_{i}/\tilde{M}_{i}}\}^{2}}\mathscr{R}^{\star}(\boldsymbol{\gamma})\;,

where setting
\mathfrak{f}_{i}=m_{i}/(20\tilde{M}_{i})
,

\displaystyle\mathscr{R}^{\star}(\boldsymbol{\gamma})

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{b}\left\{d_{i}\gamma_{i}^{2}\tilde{M}_{i}^{2}+\frac{d_{i}\gamma_{i}^{2}\mathfrak{f}_{i}}{\tilde{M}_{i}}\left(d_{i}L^{2}_{i}+\frac{\tilde{M}_{i}^{4}}{\tilde{m}_{i}}\right)+d_{i}\gamma_{i}\tilde{M}_{i}\mathfrak{f}_{i}^{3}(1+\mathfrak{f}_{i}+\mathfrak{f}_{i}^{2})\right\}\;,

(S107)

\bar{\mathbf{B}}_{0},\mathbf{B}_{0},\mathbf{\tilde{D}}_{0}
are defined in (
S2
)-(
S3
), and for any
i\in[b]
,
\tilde{m}_{i}
,
\tilde{M}_{i}
are defined in (
S25
).

We provide the proof of
Proposition

5
in what follows.
Similarly to
Lemma

S26
for the proof of
Proposition

4
, we derive an explicit relation between
\|{\tilde{Z}_{n+1}-Z_{n+1}}\|
and
\|{\tilde{Z}_{n}-Z_{n}}\|
.

Lemma S34
.

Assume
1
-
2
-
3
and let
\boldsymbol{N}\in(\mathbb{N}^{*})^{b},\boldsymbol{\gamma}\in(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{*})^{b}
such that for any
i\in[b]
,
N_{i}\gamma_{i}\leq 2/(m_{i}+\tilde{M}_{i})
and
\gamma_{i}<1/\tilde{M}_{i}
.
Then, for
n\geq 1
, we have

{\mathbb{E}\big[{\|{\tilde{Z}_{n+1}-Z_{n+1}}\|_{\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{N}\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{-1}}^{2}}\big]}^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\leq\big({1-\min_{i\in[b]}\{N_{i}\gamma_{i}m_{i}\}+r_{\boldsymbol{\gamma},\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{N}}}\big)\mathbb{E}\big[{\|{\tilde{Z}_{n}-Z_{n}}\|_{\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{N}\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{-1}}^{2}}\big]^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}+\mathscr{R}(\boldsymbol{\gamma},\boldsymbol{N})^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\;,

where

\displaystyle\mathscr{R}(\boldsymbol{\gamma},\boldsymbol{N})

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{b}d_{i}N_{i}\gamma_{i}^{3}(d_{i}L_{i}^{2}+\tilde{M}_{i}^{4}/\tilde{m}_{i})+\sum_{i=1}^{b}\left({d_{i}\gamma_{i}^{2}\tilde{M}_{i}^{2}+d_{i}N_{i}^{3}\gamma_{i}^{4}\tilde{M}_{i}^{4}}\right)

(S108)

\displaystyle+\sum_{i=1}^{b}d_{i}N_{i}^{4}\gamma_{i}^{5}\tilde{M}^{5}_{i}(1+N_{i}\gamma_{i}\tilde{M}_{i})\;,

(\tilde{Z}_{n},Z_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
is defined in (
S79
),
r_{\boldsymbol{\gamma},\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{N}}
in (
S33
) and for any
i\in[b]
,
\tilde{m}_{i}
,
\tilde{M}_{i}
are defined in (
S25
).

Proof.

Let
n\in\mathbb{N}
.
For any
k\in\mathbb{N}
, recall that
\mathbf{M}_{k}^{(n)}
is defined in (
S89
) and invertible by
Lemma

S24
.
Define

\displaystyle w_{n}=\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{N}\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{-{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}({\tilde{Z}_{n}-Z_{n}})\;.

Under this notation, the result given in
Lemma

S25
can be rewritten as

w_{n+1}=\mathbf{\mathrm{T}}_{1}^{(n)}w_{n}-\mathbf{\mathrm{T}}_{2}^{(n)}\;,

where
\mathbf{\mathrm{T}}_{1}^{(n)}
and
\mathbf{\mathrm{T}}_{2}^{(n)}
are defined in (
S91
) and (
S92
), respectively.
By the Minkowsky inequality and using (
S81
), we have

{\mathbb{E}^{\mathcal{G}_{n}}\big[{\|{w_{n+1}}\|^{2}}\big]}^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\leq{\mathbb{E}^{\mathcal{G}_{n}}\big[{\|{\mathbf{\mathrm{T}}_{1}^{(n)}w_{n}}\|^{2}}\big]}^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}+{\mathbb{E}^{\mathcal{G}_{n}}\big[{\|{\mathbf{\mathrm{T}}_{2}^{(n)}}\|^{2}}\big]}^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\;.

(S109)

Since by
Lemma

S27
,

\|{\mathbf{\mathrm{T}}_{1}^{(n)}}\|\leq 1-\min_{i\in[b]}\{N_{i}\gamma_{i}m_{i}\}+r_{\boldsymbol{\gamma},\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{N}}\;,

(S110)

it remains to bound
\mathbb{E}^{\mathcal{G}_{n}}[\|\mathbf{\mathrm{T}}_{2}^{(n)}\|^{2}]
to complete the proof.

For any
i\in[b]
, recall the function
V_{i}^{\theta_{n}}\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}\mathbb{R}^{d_{i}}\to\mathbb{R}
defined for any
\mathbf{y}^{i}\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{i}}
by
V_{i}^{\theta_{n}}(\mathbf{y}^{i})=U_{i}(\mathbf{y}^{i})+\|{\mathbf{y}^{i}-\mathbf{A}_{i}\theta_{n}}\|^{2}/(2\rho_{i})
. For any
i\in[b],k\in\mathbb{N}
, using the Itô formula, we have for
l\in[k\gamma_{i},(k+1)\gamma_{i})
,

\nabla V_{i}(\tilde{Y}_{k\gamma_{i}+l}^{(i,n)})-\nabla V_{i}(\tilde{Y}_{k\gamma_{i}}^{(i,n)})=\int_{k\gamma_{i}}^{k\gamma_{i}+l}\big\{{\nabla^{2}V_{i}^{\theta_{n}}(\tilde{Y}_{u}^{(i,n)})\nabla V_{i}^{\theta_{n}}(\tilde{Y}_{u})+\vec{\Delta}(\nabla V_{i}^{\theta_{n}})(\tilde{Y}_{u}^{(i,n)})}\big\}\,\mathrm{d}u\\
+\sqrt{2}\int_{k\gamma_{i}}^{k\gamma_{i}+l}\nabla^{2}V_{i}^{\theta_{n}}(\tilde{Y}_{u}^{(i,n)})\,\mathrm{d}B_{u}^{i}\;.

(S111)

For any
i\in[b],k\in\mathbb{N}
, define

\displaystyle a_{1,k}^{(i,n)}=\mathds{1}_{[N_{i}]}(k+1)[{\mathbf{M}_{\infty}^{(i,n)}}]^{-1}\mathbf{M}_{k+1}^{(i,n)}\int_{0}^{\gamma_{i}}\int_{k\gamma_{i}}^{k\gamma_{i}+l}\nabla^{2}V_{i}^{\theta_{n}}(\tilde{Y}_{u}^{(i,n)})\nabla V_{i}^{\theta_{n}}(\tilde{Y}_{u}^{(i,n)})\,\mathrm{d}u\,\mathrm{d}l\;,

\displaystyle a_{2,k}^{(i,n)}=\mathds{1}_{[N_{i}]}(k+1)[{\mathbf{M}_{\infty}^{(i,n)}}]^{-1}\mathbf{M}_{k+1}^{(i,n)}\int_{0}^{\gamma_{i}}\int_{k\gamma_{i}}^{k\gamma_{i}+l}\vec{\Delta}(\nabla V_{i}^{\theta_{n}})(\tilde{Y}_{u}^{(i,n)})\,\mathrm{d}u\,\mathrm{d}l\;,

\displaystyle a_{3,k}^{(i,n)}=\sqrt{2}\mathds{1}_{[N_{i}]}(k+1)[{\mathbf{M}_{\infty}^{(i,n)}}]^{-1}\mathbf{M}_{k+1}^{(i,n)}\int_{0}^{\gamma_{i}}\int_{k\gamma_{i}}^{k\gamma_{i}+l}\nabla^{2}V_{i}^{\theta_{n}}(\tilde{Y}_{u}^{(i,n)})\,\mathrm{d}B_{u}^{i}\,\mathrm{d}l\;.

With these notation and by (
S111
), we have

\displaystyle\|{T_{2}^{(n)}}\|^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i\in[b]}\frac{1}{N_{i}\gamma_{i}}\Big\|{\sum_{k\in\mathbb{N}}\{{a_{1,k}^{(i,n)}+a_{2,k}^{(i,n)}+a_{3,k}^{(i,n)}}\}}\Big\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq E_{1}+E_{2}+E_{3}\;,

(S112)

where for any
j\in[3]
,
E_{j}=3\sum_{i\in[b]}\|\sum_{k=0}^{N_{i}-1}a_{j,k}^{(i,n)}\|^{2}/(N_{i}\gamma_{i})
.
We now bound
\{E_{j}\}_{j\in[3]}
.

Upper bound on
E_{1}
.

For any
i\in[b],k\in\mathbb{N}
, recall that we have
\big[{\mathbf{M}_{\infty}^{(i,n)}}\big]^{-1}\mathbf{M}_{k+1}^{(i,n)}=\prod_{l=k+1}^{\infty}({\mathbf{I}_{d_{i}}+\mathbf{C}_{l}^{(i,n)}})
where
\mathbf{C}_{l}^{(i,n)}
is defined in (
S88
). In addition, since we suppose for any
i\in[b]
, that
\gamma_{i}\tilde{M}_{i}<1
,
Lemma

S24
implies

\displaystyle\Big\|{\prod_{l=k+1}^{N_{i}-1}(\mathbf{I}_{d_{i}}-\mathbf{C}^{(i,n)}_{l})}\Big\|^{2}\leq\left\{{1-\gamma_{i}\tilde{m}_{i}}\right\}^{2(N_{i}-k-1)}\;.

Combining this result with the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, we obtain

\frac{1}{N_{i}}\bigg\|{\sum_{k=0}^{N_{i}-1}a_{1,k}^{(i,n)}}\bigg\|^{2}\leq\sum_{k=0}^{N_{i}-1}\Big\|{\int_{0}^{\gamma_{i}}\int_{k\gamma_{i}}^{k\gamma_{i}+l}\nabla^{2}V_{i}^{\theta_{n}}(\tilde{Y}_{u}^{(i,n)})\nabla V_{i}^{\theta_{n}}(\tilde{Y}_{u}^{(i,n)})\,\mathrm{d}u\,\mathrm{d}l}\Big\|^{2}\;.

(S113)

For
i\in[b]
, using the definition of
\mathbf{z}_{n,\star}^{i}=\arg\min_{\mathbf{y}^{i}\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{i}}}V_{i}^{\theta_{n}}(\mathbf{y}^{i})\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{i}}
, we have
\nabla V_{i}^{\theta_{n}}(\mathbf{z}_{n,\star}^{i})=\mathbf{0}_{d_{i}}
. Therefore, for
i\in[b],k\in\mathbb{N}
, conditioning with respect to
\mathcal{F}_{k\gamma_{i}}^{(n)}
defined in (
S82
) and using the
\tilde{M}_{i}
-Lipschitz property of
V_{i}^{\theta_{n}}
by
2
gives

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}^{\mathcal{F}_{k\gamma_{i}}^{(n)}}\big[{\|\nabla^{2}V_{i}^{\theta_{n}}(\tilde{Y}_{u}^{(i,n)})\nabla V_{i}^{\theta_{n}}(\tilde{Y}_{u}^{(i,n)})\|^{2}}\big]

\displaystyle\leq\tilde{M}_{i}^{2}\mathbb{E}^{\mathcal{F}_{k\gamma_{i}}^{(n)}}\big[{\|\nabla V_{i}^{\theta_{n}}(\tilde{Y}_{u}^{(i,n)})-\nabla V_{i}^{\theta_{n}}(\mathbf{z}_{n,\star}^{i})\|^{2}}\big]

\displaystyle\leq\tilde{M}_{i}^{4}\mathbb{E}^{\mathcal{F}_{k\gamma_{i}}^{(n)}}\big[{\|\tilde{Y}_{u}^{(i,n)}-\mathbf{z}_{n,\star}^{i}\|^{2}}\big]\;.

For any
i\in[b],k\in\mathbb{N}
, combining this result with the Jensen inequality yields

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}^{\mathcal{F}_{k\gamma_{i}}^{(n)}}\left[{\bigg\|{\int_{0}^{\gamma_{i}}\int_{k\gamma_{i}}^{k\gamma_{i}+l}\nabla^{2}V_{i}^{\theta_{n}}(\tilde{Y}_{u}^{(i,n)})\nabla V_{i}^{\theta_{n}}(\tilde{Y}_{u}^{(i,n)})\,\mathrm{d}u\,\mathrm{d}l}\bigg\|}\right]^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\gamma_{i}\int_{0}^{\gamma_{i}}l\int_{k\gamma_{i}}^{k\gamma_{i}+l}\mathbb{E}^{\mathcal{F}_{k\gamma_{i}}^{(n)}}\big[{\|\nabla^{2}V_{i}^{\theta_{n}}(\tilde{Y}_{u}^{(i,n)})\nabla V_{i}^{\theta_{n}}(\tilde{Y}_{u}^{(i,n)})\|^{2}}\big]\,\mathrm{d}u\,\mathrm{d}l

\displaystyle\leq\gamma_{i}\tilde{M}_{i}^{4}\int_{0}^{\gamma_{i}}l\int_{k\gamma_{i}}^{k\gamma_{i}+l}\mathbb{E}^{\mathcal{F}_{k\gamma_{i}}^{(n)}}\big[{\|\tilde{Y}_{u}^{(i,n)}-\mathbf{z}_{n,\star}^{i}\|^{2}}\big]\,\mathrm{d}u\,\mathrm{d}l\;.

(S114)

By
Lemma

S22
, we have for any
i\in[b]
,
u\in\mathbb{R}_{+}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}^{\mathcal{G}_{n}}\Big[{\|\tilde{Y}_{u}^{(i,n)}-\mathbf{z}_{n,\star}^{i}\|^{2}}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq{d_{i}}/{\tilde{m}_{i}}\;.

(S115)

Injecting this result in (
S4.3
) yields

\mathbb{E}\left[{\int_{0}^{\gamma_{i}}l\int_{k\gamma_{i}}^{k\gamma_{i}+l}\mathbb{E}^{\mathcal{F}_{k\gamma_{i}}^{(n)}}\Big[{\|\tilde{Y}_{u}^{(i,n)}-\mathbf{z}_{n,\star}^{i}\|^{2}}\Big]\,\mathrm{d}u\,\mathrm{d}l}\right]\leq d_{i}\gamma_{i}^{3}/(3\tilde{m}_{i})\;.

Finally, this inequality, (
S4.3
) and (
S113
), we get

\mathbb{E}\left[{E_{1}}\right]\leq\sum_{i=1}^{b}d_{i}N_{i}\gamma_{i}^{3}\tilde{M}_{i}^{4}/\tilde{m}_{i}\;.

(S116)

Upper bound on
E_{2}
.

Using the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, we have

\frac{1}{N_{i}}\bigg\|{\sum_{k=0}^{N_{i}-1}a_{2,k}^{(i,n)}}\bigg\|^{2}\leq\sum_{k=0}^{N_{i}-1}\Big\|{\int_{0}^{\gamma_{i}}\int_{k\gamma_{i}}^{k\gamma_{i}+l}\vec{\Delta}(\nabla V^{\theta_{n}}_{i})(\tilde{Y}_{u}^{(i,n)})\,\mathrm{d}u\,\mathrm{d}l}\Big\|^{2}\;.

By
3
, we have for any
\mathbf{z}_{i}\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{i}}
,
\|\vec{\Delta}(\nabla V^{\theta_{n}}_{i})(\mathbf{z}_{i})\|^{2}\leq d_{i}^{2}L_{i}^{2}
.
Therefore, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathinner{\!\left\lVert\int_{0}^{\gamma_{i}}\int_{k\gamma_{i}}^{k\gamma_{i}+l}\vec{\Delta}(\nabla V^{\theta_{n}}_{i})(\tilde{Y}_{u}^{(i,n)})\,\mathrm{d}u\,\mathrm{d}l\right\rVert}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\gamma_{i}\int_{0}^{\gamma_{i}}l\int_{k\gamma_{i}}^{k\gamma_{i}+l}\|\vec{\Delta}(\nabla V^{\theta_{n}}_{i})(\tilde{Y}_{u}^{(i,n)})\|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}u\,\mathrm{d}l

\displaystyle\leq d_{i}^{2}\gamma_{i}^{4}L_{i}^{2}/3\;.

Thus, we get

\mathbb{E}\left[E_{2}\right]\leq\sum_{i=1}^{b}d_{i}^{2}N_{i}\gamma_{i}^{3}L_{i}^{2}\;.

(S117)

Upper bound on
E_{3}
.

For any
i\in[b],k\in\mathbb{N}
, define

\displaystyle\Delta_{3,k}^{(i,n)}=\int_{0}^{\gamma_{i}}\int_{k\gamma_{i}}^{k\gamma_{i}+l}\nabla^{2}V^{\theta_{n}}_{i}({\tilde{Y}_{u}^{(i,n)}})\,\mathrm{d}B_{u}^{i}\,\mathrm{d}l\;.

Using for any
i\in[b],k\in\mathbb{N}
,
[\mathbf{M}_{\infty}^{(i,n)}]^{-1}\mathbf{M}_{k+1}^{(i,n)}=\mathbf{I}_{d_{i}}-\sum_{l=k+1}^{\infty}\mathbf{C}_{l}^{(i,n)}+\mathbf{R}_{k}^{(i,n)}
where
\mathbf{R}_{k}^{(i,n)}
is defined in (
S34
), we have, for any
i\in[b],k\in\mathbb{N}
,

\displaystyle\bigg\|{\sum_{k=0}^{N_{i}-1}a_{3,k}^{(i,n)}}\bigg\|^{2}

\displaystyle=\mathinner{\!\left\lVert\sqrt{2}\sum_{k=0}^{N_{i}-1}\prod_{l=k+1}^{N_{i}}\left[{\mathbf{I}_{d_{i}}-\mathbf{C}_{l}^{(i,n)}}\right]\Delta_{3,k}^{(i,n)}\right\rVert}^{2}

\displaystyle=2\sum_{k_{1},k_{2}=0}^{N_{i}-1}\langle\mathbf{R}_{k_{1}}^{(i,n)}\Delta_{3,k_{1}}^{(i,n)},\mathbf{R}_{k_{2}}^{(i,n)}\Delta_{3,k_{2}}^{(i,n)}\rangle+2\sum_{k_{1},k_{2}=0}^{N_{i}-1}\langle\Delta_{3,k_{1}}^{(i,n)},\Delta_{3,k_{2}}^{(i,n)}\rangle

\displaystyle+2\sum_{k_{1},k_{2}=0}^{N_{i}-1}\langle\sum_{l=k_{1}+1}^{N_{i}}\mathbf{C}_{l}^{(i,n)}\Delta_{3,k_{1}}^{(i,n)},\sum_{l=k_{2}+1}^{N_{i}}\mathbf{C}_{l}^{(i,n)}\Delta_{3,k_{2}}^{(i,n)}\rangle

\displaystyle-4\sum_{k_{1},k_{2}=0}^{N_{i}-1}\langle\sum_{l=k_{1}+1}^{N_{i}}\mathbf{C}_{l}^{(i,n)}\Delta_{3,k_{1}}^{(i,n)},\Delta_{3,k_{2}}^{(i,n)}\rangle+4\sum_{k_{1},k_{2}=0}^{N_{i}-1}\langle\mathbf{R}_{k_{1}}^{(i,n)}\Delta_{3,k_{1}}^{(i,n)},\Delta_{3,k_{2}}^{(i,n)}\rangle

\displaystyle-4\sum_{k_{1},k_{2}=0}^{N_{i}-1}\langle\mathbf{R}_{k_{1}}^{(i,n)}\Delta_{3,k_{1}}^{(i,n)},\sum_{l=k_{2}+1}^{N_{i}}\mathbf{C}_{l}^{(i,n)}\Delta_{3,k_{2}}^{(i,n)}\rangle\;.

(S118)

We now control the quantities which appear in (
S118
).
First, by
2
, for any
i\in[b],\mathbf{x}^{i},\mathbf{y}^{i}\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{i}}
, note that we have

\|\nabla^{2}V_{i}^{\theta_{n}}(\mathbf{x}^{i})\mathbf{y}^{i}\|\leq\tilde{M}_{i}\|\mathbf{y}^{i}\|\;.

By the Jensen inequality and the Itô isometry, for any
k\in\mathbb{N}
, we get

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}^{\mathcal{F}_{k\gamma_{i}}^{(n)}}\left[{\|{\Delta_{3,k}^{(i,n)}}\|^{2}}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}^{\mathcal{F}_{k\gamma_{i}}^{(n)}}\bigg[{\Big\|{\int_{0}^{\gamma_{i}}\int_{k\gamma_{i}}^{k\gamma_{i}+l}\nabla^{2}V^{\theta_{n}}_{i}({\tilde{Y}_{u}^{(i,n)}})\,\mathrm{d}B_{u}^{i}\,\mathrm{d}l}\Big\|^{2}}\bigg]

\displaystyle\leq\gamma_{i}\tilde{M}_{i}^{2}\int_{0}^{\gamma_{i}}\mathbb{E}^{\mathcal{F}_{k\gamma_{i}}^{(n)}}\bigg[{\Big\|{\int_{k\gamma_{i}}^{k\gamma_{i}+l}\,\mathrm{d}B_{u}^{i}}\Big\|^{2}}\bigg]\,\mathrm{d}l=d_{i}\gamma_{i}^{3}\tilde{M}_{i}^{2}/2\;.

(S119)

In addition, since for
i\in[b]
,
(\int_{0}^{t}\nabla^{2}V_{i}^{\theta_{n}}(\tilde{Y}_{u}^{(i,n)})\,\mathrm{d}B_{u}^{i})_{t\geq 0}
is a
(\mathcal{F}_{t}^{(n)})_{t\geq 0}
-martingale, for
(k_{1},k_{2})\in\{{0,\ldots,N_{i}-1}\}^{2}
such that
k_{1}<k_{2}
, we obtain

\mathbb{E}^{\mathcal{G}_{n}}\Big[{\big[{\Delta_{3,k_{1}}^{(i,n)}}\big]^{\top}\Delta_{3,k_{2}}^{(i,n)}}\Big]=\mathbb{E}^{\mathcal{G}_{n}}\Big[{\mathbb{E}^{\mathcal{F}_{k_{2}\gamma_{i}}^{(n)}}\big[{\Delta_{3,k_{1}}^{(i,n)\top}\Delta_{3,k_{2}}^{(i,n)}}\big]}\Big]=0\;.

Therefore,

\sum_{k_{1},k_{2}=0}^{N_{i}-1}\mathbb{E}^{\mathcal{G}_{n}}\left[{\langle\Delta_{3,k_{1}}^{(i,n)},\Delta_{3,k_{2}}^{(i,n)}\rangle}\right]=d_{i}N_{i}\gamma_{i}^{3}\tilde{M}_{i}^{2}/2\;.

Second, since for any
i\in[b],l\in\mathbb{N},\mathbf{C}_{l}^{(i,n)}\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{i}\times d_{i}}
is symmetric positive semi-definite, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{k_{1},k_{2}=0}^{N_{i}-1}\langle\sum_{l=k_{1}+1}^{N_{i}}\mathbf{C}_{l}^{(i,n)}\Delta_{3,k_{1}}^{(i,n)},\Delta_{3,k_{2}}^{(i,n)}\rangle

\displaystyle=\left\langle\left\{\sum_{l=1}^{N}C_{l}\right\}\sum_{k_{1}=0}^{l-1}\Delta_{3,k_{1}},\sum_{k_{1}=0}^{l-1}\Delta_{3,k_{2}}\right\rangle\geq 0\;.

Third, using for any
i\in[b],l\in\mathbb{N}
, using
\|{\mathbf{C}_{l}^{(i,n)}}\|\leq\gamma_{i}\tilde{M}_{i}
by definition (
S88
) and
2
and combining the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality with (
S119
), for any
i\in[b],(k_{1},k_{2})\in\{{0,\ldots,N_{i}-1}\}^{2}
, we get

\sum_{k_{1},k_{2}=0}^{N_{i}-1}\mathbb{E}^{\mathcal{G}_{n}}\Big[{\langle\sum_{l=k_{1}+1}^{N_{i}}\mathbf{C}_{l}^{(i,n)}\Delta_{3,k_{1}}^{(i,n)},\sum_{l=k_{2}+1}^{N_{i}}\mathbf{C}_{l}^{(i,n)}\Delta_{3,k_{2}}^{(i,n)}\rangle}\Big]\leq d_{i}N_{i}^{4}\gamma_{i}^{5}\tilde{M}_{i}^{4}/8\;.

Using (
S119
) again and
Lemma

S8
, for
i\in[b]
, we obtain

\displaystyle\sum_{k_{1},k_{2}=0}^{N_{i}-1}\mathbb{E}^{\mathcal{G}_{n}}\Big[{\langle\mathbf{R}_{k_{1}}^{(i,n)}\Delta_{3,k_{1}}^{(i,n)},\mathbf{R}_{k_{2}}^{(i,n)}\Delta_{3,k_{2}}^{(i,n)}\rangle}\Big]\leq(d_{i}\gamma_{i}^{3}\tilde{M}_{i}^{2}/2)\sum_{k_{1},k_{2}=0}^{N_{i}-1}\mathbb{E}\left[\|{\mathbf{R}_{k_{1}}^{(i,n)}}\|\|{\mathbf{R}_{k_{2}}^{(i,n)}}\|\right]

\displaystyle\leq(d_{i}\gamma_{i}^{3}\tilde{M}_{i}^{2}/2)\left\{\sum_{k=0}^{N_{i}-1}(\exp[(N_{i}-k)\gamma_{i}\tilde{M}_{i}]-1-[(N_{i}-k)\gamma_{i}\tilde{M}_{i}])\right\}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq(d_{i}\gamma_{i}^{3}\tilde{M}_{i}^{2}/2)\left\{(\tilde{M}_{i}\gamma_{i})^{-1}\int_{0}^{N_{i}\gamma_{i}\tilde{M}_{i}}\{\mathrm{e}^{t}-1-t\}\mathrm{d}t\right\}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{(\mathrm{e}^{N_{i}\gamma_{i}\tilde{M}_{i}}+1)^{2}}{288}d_{i}N_{i}^{6}\gamma_{i}^{7}\tilde{M}^{6}_{i}\;.

Similarly, we get
Moreover, using the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, for any
i\in[b]
we get

\displaystyle\sum_{k_{1},k_{2}=0}^{N_{i}-1}\mathbb{E}[\langle\Delta_{k_{1}}^{(i,n)},\mathbf{R}_{k_{2}}^{(i,n)}\Delta_{k_{2}}^{(i,n)}\rangle]

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k_{1},k_{2}=0}^{N_{i}-1}\mathbb{E}\left[\|{\Delta_{k_{1}}^{(i,n)}}\|\|{\Delta_{k_{2}}^{(i,n)}}\|\|{\mathbf{R}_{k_{2}}^{(i,n)}}\|\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{d_{i}N_{i}\gamma_{i}^{3}\tilde{M}_{i}^{2}}{24}(\mathrm{e}^{N_{i}\gamma_{i}\tilde{M}_{i}}+1)N_{i}^{3}\gamma_{i}^{2}\tilde{M}_{i}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq d_{i}N_{i}^{4}\gamma_{i}^{5}\tilde{M}_{i}^{4}\frac{\mathrm{e}^{N_{i}\gamma_{i}\tilde{M}_{i}}+1}{24}\;.

In addition, for any
i\in[b]
, we have also

\sum_{k_{1},k_{2}=0}^{N_{i}-1}\mathbb{E}\left[\langle\mathbf{R}_{k_{1}}^{(i,n)}\Delta_{3,k_{1}}^{(i,n)},\sum_{l=k_{2}+1}^{N_{i}}\mathbf{C}_{l}^{(i,n)}\Delta_{3,k_{2}}^{(i,n)}\rangle\right]\leq d_{i}N_{i}^{5}\gamma_{i}^{6}\tilde{M}_{i}^{5}\frac{\mathrm{e}^{N_{i}\gamma_{i}\tilde{M}_{i}}+1}{24}\;.

(S120)

For any
i\in[b],k\in\mathbb{N}
, regrouping the previous results and using that
N_{i}\gamma_{i}\tilde{M}_{i}\leq 2
give

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[E_{3}]

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i=1}^{b}\{d_{i}N_{i}\gamma_{i}^{2}\tilde{M}_{i}^{2}+d_{i}N_{i}^{3}\gamma_{i}^{4}\tilde{M}_{i}^{4}\}+\sum_{i=1}^{b}d_{i}N_{i}^{4}\gamma_{i}^{5}\tilde{M}^{5}_{i}(1+N_{i}\gamma_{i}\tilde{M}_{i})\;.

(S121)

Combination of our previous results.

Injecting the three upper bounds (

), (
S117
), (
S121
) in (

), we get

\mathbb{E}\left[{\|{T_{2}^{(n)}}\|^{2}}\right]\leq\sum_{i=1}^{b}d_{i}N_{i}\gamma_{i}^{3}(d_{i}L_{i}^{2}+\tilde{M}_{i}^{4}/\tilde{m}_{i})+\sum_{i=1}^{b}\{d_{i}\gamma_{i}^{2}\tilde{M}_{i}^{2}+d_{i}N_{i}^{3}\gamma_{i}^{4}\tilde{M}_{i}^{4}\}\\
+\sum_{i=1}^{b}d_{i}N_{i}^{4}\gamma_{i}^{5}\tilde{M}^{5}_{i}(1+N_{i}\gamma_{i}\tilde{M}_{i})\;.

(S122)

Using the recursion defined in (
S109
), and combining the upper bounds derived in (

) completes the proof.

Lemma S35
.

Assume

-

-

and let

such that for any

,

and
\upkappa_{\boldsymbol{\gamma},\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{N}}=\min_{i\in[b]}\{N_{i}\gamma_{i}m_{i}\}-r_{\boldsymbol{\gamma},\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{N}}\in\left(0,1\right)
, where

{\mathbb{E}\big[{\|{\tilde{Z}_{n+1}-Z_{n+1}}\|_{\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{N}\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{-1}}^{2}}\big]}^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\leq(1-\upkappa_{\boldsymbol{\gamma},\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{N}})^{n-1}{\mathbb{E}\big[{\|{\tilde{Z}_{1}-Z_{1}}\|_{\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{N}\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{-1}}^{2}}\big]}^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}+\{{\upkappa_{\boldsymbol{\gamma},\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{N}}}\}^{-1}\mathscr{R}(\boldsymbol{\gamma},\boldsymbol{N})\;,

Proof of

/

.

For any
i\in[b]
, consider

N_{i}^{\star}(\gamma_{i})=\big\lfloor m_{i}\min_{i\in[b]}\{{m_{i}/\tilde{M}_{i}}\}^{2}/\big({20\gamma_{i}\tilde{M}_{i}^{2}\max_{i\in[b]}\{{m_{i}/\tilde{M}_{i}}\}^{2}}\big)\big\rfloor\;.

By

S13
and

,
P_{\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{\gamma},\boldsymbol{N}}

Appendix S5
Explicit mixing times

This section aims at providing mixing times for DG-LMC with explicit dependencies w.r.t. the dimension
d
and the prescribed precision
\varepsilon
.
We specify our result to the case where for any
i\in[b]
,
m_{i}=m
,
M_{i}=M
,
L_{i}=L
,
\rho_{i}=\rho
,
\gamma_{i}=\gamma
,
N_{i}=N
and for the specific initial distribution

\mu_{\boldsymbol{\rho}}^{\star}=\updelta_{\mathbf{z}^{\star}}\otimes\Pi_{\boldsymbol{\rho}}(\cdot|\mathbf{z}^{\star})\;,

(S124)

where

\text{
$\mathbf{x}^{\star}=([\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\star}]^{\top},[\mathbf{z}^{\star}]^{\top})^{\top}$, where $\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\star}=\operatorname*{arg\,min}\{-\log\pi\}$ and $\mathbf{z}^{\star}=([\mathbf{A}_{1}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\star}]^{\top},\cdots,[\mathbf{A}_{b}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\star}]^{\top})^{\top}$}\;.

(S125)

Note that sampling from
\mu_{\boldsymbol{\rho}}^{\star}
is straightforward and simply consists in setting
\mathbf{z}_{0}=\mathbf{z}^{\star}
and
\boldsymbol{\theta}_{0}=\bar{\mathbf{B}}_{0}^{-1}\mathbf{B}_{0}^{\top}\mathbf{\tilde{D}}_{0}^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\mathbf{z}_{0}+\bar{\mathbf{B}}_{0}^{-{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\xi
, where
\xi
is a
d
-dimensional standard Gaussian random variable.
Starting from this initialisation, we consider the marginal law of
\theta_{n}
for
n\geq 1
and denote it
\Gamma_{\mathbf{x}^{\star}}^{n}
.
By
Proposition

S13
, since for any
i\in[b]
,
N_{i}=N
, the stationary distribution associated to
P_{\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{\gamma},\boldsymbol{N}}
is
\Pi_{\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{\gamma}}=\Pi_{\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{\gamma},\mathbf{1}_{b}}
.
We build upon the natural decomposition of the bias:

\displaystyle W_{2}(\Gamma_{\mathbf{x}^{\star}}^{n},\pi)\leq W_{2}(\mu^{\star}_{\boldsymbol{\rho}}P_{\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{\gamma},\boldsymbol{N}}^{n},\Pi_{\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{\gamma}})+W_{2}(\Pi_{\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{\gamma}},\Pi_{\boldsymbol{\rho}})+W_{2}(\pi_{\boldsymbol{\rho}},\pi)\;,

where
\Pi_{\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{\gamma}}
,
\Pi_{\boldsymbol{\rho}}
and
\pi_{\boldsymbol{\rho}}
are defined in
Proposition

2
, (
2
) and (
3
), respectively.
The following subsections focus on deriving conditions on
n_{\varepsilon}
,
\gamma_{\varepsilon}
,
N_{\varepsilon}
and
\rho_{\varepsilon}
to satisfy
W_{2}(\Gamma_{\mathbf{x}^{\star}}^{n_{\varepsilon}},\pi)\leq\varepsilon
, where
\varepsilon>0
.

S5.1
Lower bound on the number of iterations
n_{\varepsilon}

In this section, we derive a lower bound on
n_{\varepsilon}
such that
W_{2}(\mu^{\star}_{\boldsymbol{\rho}}P_{\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{\gamma},\boldsymbol{N}}^{n_{\varepsilon}},\Pi_{\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{\gamma}})\leq\varepsilon/3
following the result provided in

Proposition

S14
.
Recall that we define the
\mathbf{z}
-marginal under
\Pi_{\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{\gamma}}
by

\pi^{\mathbf{z}}_{\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{\gamma}}=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\Pi_{\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{\gamma}}(\boldsymbol{\theta},\mathbf{z})\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{\theta}\;,

(S126)

and the transition kernel of the Markov chain
\{Z_{n}\}_{n\geq 0}
, for all
\mathbf{z}\in\mathbb{R}^{p}
and
\mathsf{B}\in\mathcal{B}(\mathbb{R}^{p})
, by

P_{\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{\gamma},\boldsymbol{N}}^{\mathbf{z}}(\mathbf{z},\mathsf{B})=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}Q_{\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{\gamma},\boldsymbol{N}}(\mathbf{z},\mathsf{B}|\boldsymbol{\theta})\Pi_{\rho}(\boldsymbol{\theta}|\mathbf{z})\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{\theta}\;,

(S127)

where
\Pi_{\rho}(\cdot|\mathbf{z})
and
Q_{\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{\gamma},\boldsymbol{N}}
are defined in (
5
) and (
S16
), respectively.
In the case
\boldsymbol{N}=\mathbf{1}_{b}
, we simply denote
P_{\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{\gamma},\boldsymbol{N}}^{\mathbf{z}}
by
P_{\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{\mathbf{z}}
.
We need to bound in
Proposition

S14
the factor

\left\{\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\|\mathbf{z}_{1}-\mathbf{z}^{\star}\|_{\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{N}\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{-1}}^{2}\pi^{\mathbf{z}}_{\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{\gamma}}(\mathrm{d}\mathbf{z}_{1})+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\|\mathbf{z}_{1}-\mathbf{z}^{\star}\|_{\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{N}\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{-1}}^{2}P_{\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{\gamma},\boldsymbol{N}}^{\mathbf{z}}(\mathbf{z}^{\star},\mathrm{d}\mathbf{z}_{1})\right\}^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\;.

(S128)

Our next results provide such bounds.

Lemma S36
.

Assume
1
. Then, the transition kernel
P_{\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{\mathbf{z}}
leaves
\pi_{\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{\mathbf{z}}
invariant, that is
\pi_{\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{\mathbf{z}}P_{\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{\mathbf{z}}=\pi_{\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{\mathbf{z}}
, where
\pi_{\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{\mathbf{z}}
is defined by (
S50
).

Proof.

We have for any
\mathsf{B}\in\mathcal{B}(\mathbb{R}^{p})

\int_{\mathsf{B}}\pi_{\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{\mathbf{z}}(\mathrm{d}\mathbf{z})=\int_{\mathsf{B}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\Pi_{\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{\gamma}}(\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{\theta},\mathrm{d}\mathbf{z})=\int_{\mathsf{B}}\pi_{\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{\mathbf{z}}(\mathrm{d}\mathbf{z})\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\Pi_{\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{\gamma}}(\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{\theta}|\mathbf{z})\;.

Therefore, using the fact that
P_{\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{\gamma}}
leaves
\Pi_{\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{\gamma}}
invariant from
Proposition

S5
and Fubini’s theorem, we get

\displaystyle\int_{\mathsf{B}}\pi_{\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{\mathbf{z}}(\mathrm{d}\mathbf{z})

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathsf{B}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\Pi_{\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{\gamma}}(\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{\theta},\mathrm{d}\mathbf{z})=\int_{\mathsf{B}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{p}}\Pi_{\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{\gamma}}(\mathrm{d}\tilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}},\mathrm{d}\tilde{\mathbf{z}})P_{\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{\gamma}}((\tilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}},\tilde{\mathbf{z}}),(\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{\theta},\mathrm{d}\mathbf{z}))

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathsf{B}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{p}}\Pi_{\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{\gamma}}(\mathrm{d}\tilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}},\mathrm{d}\tilde{\mathbf{z}})Q_{\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{\gamma}}(\tilde{\mathbf{z}},\mathrm{d}\mathbf{z}|\tilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}})\Pi_{\boldsymbol{\rho}}(\boldsymbol{\theta}|\mathbf{z})\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{\theta}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathsf{B}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{p}}\Pi_{\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{\gamma}}(\mathrm{d}\tilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}},\mathrm{d}\tilde{\mathbf{z}})Q_{\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{\gamma}}(\tilde{\mathbf{z}},\mathrm{d}\mathbf{z}|\tilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}})\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\Pi_{\boldsymbol{\rho}}(\boldsymbol{\theta}|\mathbf{z})\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{\theta}

(S129)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\pi_{\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{\mathbf{z}}(\mathrm{d}\tilde{\mathbf{z}})P_{\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{\mathbf{z}}(\tilde{\mathbf{z}},\mathsf{B})\;.

∎

For any
i\in[b]
, let
\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}^{\star}
a minimiser of
\boldsymbol{\theta}\mapsto U_{i}(\mathbf{A}_{i}\boldsymbol{\theta})
, and define

\mathbf{u}^{\star}=([\mathbf{A}_{1}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\star}-\boldsymbol{\theta}_{1}^{\star})]^{\top},\cdots,[\mathbf{A}_{b}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\star}-\boldsymbol{\theta}_{b}^{\star})]^{\top})^{\top}

(S130)

Lemma S37
.

Assume
1
-
2
and let
\boldsymbol{N}\in(\mathbb{N}^{*})^{b},\boldsymbol{\gamma},\boldsymbol{\rho}\in(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{*})^{b}
such that, for any
i\in[b]
,
\gamma_{i}\leq 2/(m_{i}+M_{i}+1/\rho_{i})
and denote
\mathbf{z}^{\star}=([\mathbf{A}_{1}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\star}]^{\top},\cdots,[\mathbf{A}_{b}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\star}]^{\top})^{\top}
.
Then, for any
\mathbf{z}\in\mathbb{R}^{p}
and
\varepsilon>0
,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{p}}\mathinner{\!\left\lVert\tilde{\mathbf{z}}-\mathbf{z}^{\star}\right\rVert}_{\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{N}\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{-1}}^{2}P_{\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{\mathbf{z}}(\mathbf{z},\mathrm{d}\tilde{\mathbf{z}})

\displaystyle\leq\min_{i\in[b]}\{N_{i}\}^{-1}\Big[\kappa_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{2}(1+2\varepsilon)\mathinner{\!\left\lVert\mathbf{z}-\mathbf{z}^{\star}\right\rVert}_{\mathbf{D}_{\gamma}^{-1}}^{2}

\displaystyle+(1+1/(2\varepsilon))\max_{i\in[b]}\{\gamma_{i}M_{i}^{2}\}\mathinner{\!\left\lVert\mathbf{u}^{\star}\right\rVert}^{2}+\mathrm{Tr}(\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}/\boldsymbol{\rho}}\mathbf{P}_{0})+2\sum_{i=1}^{b}d_{i}\Big]\;,

where the transition kernel
P_{\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{\mathbf{z}}
is defined in (
S51
) with
\boldsymbol{N}=\mathbf{1}_{b}
.

Proof.

Let
\gamma_{i}\leq 2/(m_{i}+M_{i}+1/\rho_{i})
for any
i\in[b]
.
Let
\xi
be a
d
-dimensional Gaussian random variable independent of
\{\eta^{i}\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}i\in[b]\}
where for any
i\in[b]
,
\eta^{i}
is a
d_{i}
-dimensional Gaussian random variable.
Let
\mathbf{z}\in\mathbb{R}^{p}
and
Z
be the random variable distributed according to
\updelta_{\mathbf{z}}P_{\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{\mathbf{z}}
, and defined by

\displaystyle\theta

\displaystyle=\bar{\mathbf{B}}_{0}^{-1}\mathbf{B}_{0}^{\top}\mathbf{\tilde{D}}_{0}^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\mathbf{z}+\bar{\mathbf{B}}_{0}^{-{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\xi\;,

and for any
i\in[b]
,

\displaystyle Z^{i}

\displaystyle=\left(1-\gamma_{i}/\rho_{i}\right)\mathbf{z}_{i}-\gamma_{i}\nabla U_{i}(\mathbf{z}_{i})+\frac{\gamma_{i}}{\rho_{i}}\mathbf{A}_{i}\theta+\sqrt{2\gamma_{i}}\eta^{i}

\displaystyle=\left(1-\gamma_{i}/\rho_{i}\right)\mathbf{z}_{i}-\gamma_{i}\nabla U_{i}(\mathbf{z}_{i})+\frac{\gamma_{i}}{\rho_{i}}\mathbf{A}_{i}\bar{\mathbf{B}}_{0}^{-1}\mathbf{B}_{0}^{\top}\mathbf{\tilde{D}}_{0}^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\mathbf{z}+\frac{\gamma_{i}}{\rho_{i}}\mathbf{A}_{i}\bar{\mathbf{B}}_{0}^{-{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\xi+\sqrt{2\gamma_{i}}\eta^{i}

\displaystyle=\left(1-\gamma_{i}/\rho_{i}\right)\mathbf{z}_{i}-\gamma_{i}[\nabla U_{i}(\mathbf{z}_{i})-\nabla U_{i}(\mathbf{A}_{i}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\star})]-\gamma_{i}[\nabla U_{i}(\mathbf{A}_{i}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\star})-\nabla U_{i}(\mathbf{A}_{i}\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}^{\star})]

\displaystyle+\frac{\gamma_{i}}{\rho_{i}}\mathbf{A}_{i}\bar{\mathbf{B}}_{0}^{-1}\mathbf{B}_{0}^{\top}\mathbf{\tilde{D}}_{0}^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\mathbf{z}+\frac{\gamma_{i}}{\rho_{i}}\mathbf{A}_{i}\bar{\mathbf{B}}_{0}^{-{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\xi+\sqrt{2\gamma_{i}}\eta^{i}\;.

Let

\displaystyle\mathbf{D}_{U}^{\star}=\mathrm{diag}\Big({\gamma_{1}\int_{0}^{1}\nabla^{2}U_{1}(\mathbf{z}_{1}+t(\mathbf{A}_{1}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\star}-\mathbf{z}_{1}))\,\mathrm{d}t,\cdots,\gamma_{b}\int_{0}^{1}\nabla^{2}U_{b}(\mathbf{z}_{b}+t(\mathbf{A}_{b}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\star}-\mathbf{z}_{b}))\,\mathrm{d}t}\Big)\;,

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathbf{D}}_{U}^{\star}=\mathrm{diag}\Big({\gamma_{1}\int_{0}^{1}\nabla^{2}U_{1}(\mathbf{A}_{1}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\star}+t(\mathbf{A}_{1}\boldsymbol{\theta}_{1}^{\star}-\mathbf{A}_{1}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\star}))\,\mathrm{d}t,\cdots,\gamma_{b}\int_{0}^{1}\nabla^{2}U_{b}(\mathbf{A}_{b}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\star}+t(\mathbf{A}_{b}\boldsymbol{\theta}_{b}^{\star}-\mathbf{A}_{b}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\star}))\,\mathrm{d}t}\Big)\;.

(S131)

Since
\mathbf{P}_{0}\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{\rho}}^{-{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\mathbf{z}^{\star}=\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{\rho}}^{-{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\mathbf{z}^{\star}
, it follows that

Z-\mathbf{z}^{\star}=\left[{\mathbf{I}_{p}-\mathbf{D}_{U}^{\star}-\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}/\boldsymbol{\rho}}^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}(\mathbf{I}_{p}-\mathbf{P}_{0})\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{\rho}}^{-{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}}\right](\mathbf{z}-\mathbf{z}^{\star})-\tilde{\mathbf{D}}_{U}^{\star}\mathbf{u}^{\star}+\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}/\boldsymbol{\rho}}^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\mathbf{B}_{0}\bar{\mathbf{B}}_{0}^{-{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\xi+\mathbf{D}_{2\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\eta\;.

With the notation
\mathbf{H}=\mathbf{I}_{p}-\mathbf{D}_{U}^{\star}-\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}/\boldsymbol{\rho}}^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}(\mathbf{I}_{p}-\mathbf{P}_{0})\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{\rho}}^{-{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}
, (
S14
), and using the fact that for any
\varepsilon>0
,
\mathbf{a},\mathbf{b}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,
|\langle\mathbf{a},\mathbf{b}\rangle|\leq\varepsilon\mathinner{\!\left\lVert\mathbf{a}\right\rVert}^{2}+(4\varepsilon)^{-1}\mathinner{\!\left\lVert\mathbf{b}\right\rVert}^{2}
, it follows, for any
\mathbf{z}\in\mathbb{R}^{p}
, that

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{p}}\big\|{\tilde{\mathbf{z}}-\mathbf{z}^{\star}}\big\|_{\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{-1}}^{2}P_{\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{\mathbf{z}}(\mathbf{z},\mathrm{d}\tilde{\mathbf{z}})

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{p}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\big\|{\mathbf{H}(\mathbf{z}-\mathbf{z}^{\star})-\tilde{\mathbf{D}}_{U}^{\star}\mathbf{u}^{\star}+\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}/\boldsymbol{\rho}}^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\mathbf{B}_{0}\bar{\mathbf{B}}_{0}^{-{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\boldsymbol{\xi}+\mathbf{D}_{2\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\boldsymbol{\eta}}\big\|_{\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{-1}}^{2}\upphi_{d}(\boldsymbol{\xi})\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{\xi}\upphi_{p}(\boldsymbol{\eta})\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{\eta}

\displaystyle=\big\|{\mathbf{H}(\mathbf{z}-\mathbf{z}^{\star})-\tilde{\mathbf{D}}_{U}^{\star}\mathbf{u}^{\star}}\big\|_{\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{-1}}^{2}+\mathrm{Tr}(\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}/\boldsymbol{\rho}}\mathbf{P}_{0})+2\sum_{i=1}^{b}d_{i}

\displaystyle\leq\kappa_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{2}\mathinner{\!\left\lVert\mathbf{z}-\mathbf{z}^{\star}\right\rVert}_{\mathbf{D}_{\gamma}^{-1}}^{2}-2\langle\mathbf{H}(\mathbf{z}-\mathbf{z}^{\star}),\tilde{\mathbf{D}}_{U}^{\star}\mathbf{u}^{\star}\rangle_{\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{-1}}+\big\|{\tilde{\mathbf{D}}_{U}^{\star}\mathbf{u}^{\star}}\big\|_{\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{-1}}^{2}+\mathrm{Tr}(\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}/\boldsymbol{\rho}}\mathbf{P}_{0})+2\sum_{i=1}^{b}d_{i}

\displaystyle\leq\kappa_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{2}(1+2\varepsilon)\mathinner{\!\left\lVert\mathbf{z}-\mathbf{z}^{\star}\right\rVert}_{\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{-1}}^{2}+\Big(1+\frac{1}{2\varepsilon}\Big)\max_{i\in[b]}\{\gamma_{i}M_{i}^{2}\}\mathinner{\!\left\lVert\mathbf{u}^{\star}\right\rVert}^{2}+\mathrm{Tr}(\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}/\boldsymbol{\rho}}\mathbf{P}_{0})+2\sum_{i=1}^{b}d_{i}\;.

∎

Proposition S38
.

Assume
1
-
2
and let
\boldsymbol{N}\in(\mathbb{N}^{*})^{b},\boldsymbol{\gamma},\boldsymbol{\rho}\in(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{*})^{b}
such that, for any
i\in[b]
,
\gamma_{i}\leq 2/(m_{i}+M_{i}+1/\rho_{i})
.
Then, we have

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\|\mathbf{z}_{1}-\mathbf{z}^{\star}\|_{\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{N}\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{-1}}^{2}\pi^{\mathbf{z}}_{\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{\gamma}}(\mathrm{d}\mathbf{z}_{1})\\
\leq\min_{i\in[b]}\{N_{i}\}^{-1}\frac{2}{1-\kappa_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{2}}\Bigg({\frac{1+\kappa_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{2}}{1-\kappa_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{2}}\max_{i\in[b]}\{\gamma_{i}M_{i}^{2}\}\mathinner{\!\left\lVert\mathbf{u}^{\star}\right\rVert}^{2}+\mathrm{Tr}(\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}/\boldsymbol{\rho}}\mathbf{P}_{0})+2\sum_{i=1}^{b}d_{i}}\Bigg)\;,

with
\kappa_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}
defined in (
S12
).

Proof.

With the choice
\varepsilon=(1-\kappa_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{2})/(4\kappa_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{2})
in
Lemma

S37
and using
Lemma

S36
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{p}}\mathinner{\!\left\lVert\tilde{\mathbf{z}}-\mathbf{z}^{\star}\right\rVert}^{2}_{\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{-1}}\pi^{\mathbf{z}}_{\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{\gamma}}(\mathrm{d}\tilde{\mathbf{z}})\leq

\displaystyle\frac{\kappa_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{2}+1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{p}}\mathinner{\!\left\lVert\mathbf{z}-\mathbf{z}^{\star}\right\rVert}_{\mathbf{D}_{\gamma}^{-1}}^{2}\pi^{\mathbf{z}}_{\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{\gamma}}(\mathrm{d}\mathbf{z})+\frac{1+\kappa_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{2}}{1-\kappa_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{2}}\max_{i\in[b]}\{\gamma_{i}M_{i}^{2}\}\mathinner{\!\left\lVert\mathbf{u}^{\star}\right\rVert}^{2}

\displaystyle+\mathrm{Tr}(\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}/\boldsymbol{\rho}}\mathbf{P}_{0})+2\sum_{i=1}^{b}d_{i}\;.

Rearranging terms concludes the proof.
∎

Lemma S39
.

Assume
1
-
2
and let
\boldsymbol{N}\in(\mathbb{N}^{*})^{b},\boldsymbol{\gamma},\boldsymbol{\rho}\in(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{*})^{b}
such that, for any
i\in[b]
,
N_{i}\gamma_{i}\leq 2/(m_{i}+M_{i}+1/\rho_{i}),\gamma_{i}\tilde{M}_{i}<1
and denote
\mathbf{z}^{\star}=([\mathbf{A}_{1}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\star}]^{\top},\cdots,[\mathbf{A}_{b}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\star}]^{\top})^{\top}
.
Then, we have

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{p}}\mathinner{\!\left\lVert\tilde{\mathbf{z}}-\mathbf{z}^{\star}\right\rVert}_{\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{N}\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{-1}}^{2}P_{\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{\gamma},\boldsymbol{N}}^{\mathbf{z}}(\mathbf{z}^{\star},\mathrm{d}\tilde{\mathbf{z}})\leq 2\sum_{i=1}^{b}\gamma_{i}N_{i}\left({1+\mathrm{Tr}(\mathbf{P}_{0})/\rho_{i}}\right)+4\sum_{i=1}^{b}d_{i}\;.

where the transition kernel
P_{\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{\gamma},\boldsymbol{N}}^{\mathbf{z}}
is defined in (
S51
).

Proof.

Let
\{(\eta^{i}_{k})_{k\geq 1}\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}i\in[b]\}
be independent random variables such that for any
i\in[b]
, the sequences
\{(\eta^{i}_{k})_{k\geq 1}\}
are i.i.d.
d_{i}
-dimensional Brownian motions and let
\xi
a
d
-dimensional standard Gaussian random variable independent of
\{(\eta^{i}_{k})_{k\geq 1}\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}i\in[b]\}
.
Consider the stochastic process
(Y_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
initialised for any
i\in[b]
at
Y_{0}^{i}=\mathbf{A}_{i}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\star}
and defined, for any
i\in[b],k\in\mathbb{N}
, by

\displaystyle Y_{k+1}^{i}=Y_{k}^{i}-\gamma_{i}\nabla V_{i}(Y_{k}^{i})+(\gamma_{i}/\rho_{i})\mathbf{A}_{i}\theta+\sqrt{2\gamma_{i}}\eta_{k+1}^{i}\;,

(S132)

where the potential
V_{i}=\mathbf{y}^{i}\mapsto U_{i}(\mathbf{y}^{i})+\|\mathbf{y}^{i}\|^{2}/(2\rho_{i})
and

\displaystyle\theta=\bar{\mathbf{B}}_{0}^{-1}\mathbf{B}_{0}^{\top}\mathbf{\tilde{D}}_{0}^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\mathbf{z}^{\star}+\bar{\mathbf{B}}_{0}^{-{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\xi\;.

(S133)

In addition, we define the random variable
Z=(Z^{1},\ldots,Z^{b})
, for any
i\in[b]
, as

Z^{i}=Y_{N_{i}}^{i}\;.

By definition, note that
Z
is distributed according to
P_{\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{\gamma},\boldsymbol{N}}^{\mathbf{z}}(\mathbf{z}^{\star},\cdot)
.
Define the process
(\mathrm{Y}_{k}=\{\mathrm{Y}_{k}^{i}\}_{i=1}^{b})_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
valued in
\mathbb{R}^{p}\times\mathbb{R}^{p}
defined for any
i\in[b]
,
k\geq 0
by

\displaystyle\mathrm{Y}_{k}^{i}=Y_{\min(k,N_{i})}^{i}\;.

and consider the following matrices defined, for any
k\in\mathbb{N}
, by

\displaystyle\mathbf{H}_{U,k}=\mathrm{diag}\bigg(\gamma_{1}\int_{0}^{1}\nabla^{2}U_{1}((1-s)Y_{k}^{1}+s\mathbf{z}^{\star})\,\mathrm{d}s,

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\ldots,\gamma_{b}\int_{0}^{1}\nabla^{2}U_{b}((1-s)Y_{k}^{b}+s\mathbf{z}^{\star})\,\mathrm{d}s\bigg)\;,

\displaystyle\mathbf{J}(k)=\mathrm{diag}\left({\mathds{1}_{[N_{1}]}(k+1)\cdot\mathbf{I}_{d_{1}},\cdots,\mathds{1}_{[N_{b}]}(k+1)\cdot\mathbf{I}_{d_{b}}}\right)\;,

(S134)

\displaystyle\mathbf{C}_{k}=\mathbf{J}(k)(\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}/\boldsymbol{\rho}}+\mathbf{H}_{U,k})\;,

(S135)

\displaystyle\mathbf{M}_{k+1}=(\mathbf{I}_{p}-\mathbf{C}_{0})^{-1}\ldots(\mathbf{I}_{p}-\mathbf{C}_{k})^{-1}\;,\qquad\text{ with }\mathbf{M}_{0}=\mathbf{I}_{p}\;.

(S136)

Using these notation and (
S132
), for any
k\in\mathbb{N}
, we get

\displaystyle\mathrm{Y}_{k+1}-\mathbf{z}^{\star}=

\displaystyle(\mathbf{I}_{p}-\mathbf{C}_{k})(\mathrm{Y}_{k}-\mathbf{z}^{\star})+\mathbf{J}(k)\left({\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}/\sqrt{\boldsymbol{\rho}}}\mathbf{B}_{0}\theta-\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}\nabla V(\mathbf{z}^{\star})+\mathbf{D}_{2\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\eta_{k+1}}\right)\;.

Multiplying the previous equality by
\mathbf{M}_{k+1}\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{N}\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{-{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}
, we obtain, for
k\geq 0
,

\mathbf{M}_{k+1}\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{N}\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{-{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}(\mathrm{Y}_{k+1}-\mathbf{z}^{\star})=\mathbf{M}_{k}\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{N}\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{-{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}(\mathrm{Y}_{k}-\mathbf{z}^{\star})\\
+\mathbf{M}_{k+1}\mathbf{J}(k)\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{N}\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{-{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\left({\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}/\sqrt{\boldsymbol{\rho}}}\mathbf{B}_{0}\theta-\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}\nabla V(\mathbf{z}^{\star})+\mathbf{D}_{2\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\eta_{k+1}}\right)\;.

Summing the previous equality over
k\in\mathbb{N}
gives

\mathbf{M}_{\infty}\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{N}\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{-{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}(\mathrm{Y}_{\boldsymbol{N}}-\mathbf{z}^{\star})=\mathbf{M}_{0}\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{N}\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{-{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}(\mathrm{Y}_{0}-\mathbf{z}^{\star})\\
+\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\mathbf{M}_{k+1}\mathbf{J}(k)\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{N}\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{-{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\left({\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}/\sqrt{\boldsymbol{\rho}}}\mathbf{B}_{0}\theta-\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}\nabla V(\mathbf{z}^{\star})+\mathbf{D}_{2\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\eta_{k+1}}\right)\;.

Multiplying the last equality by
[\mathbf{M}_{\infty}]^{-1}
and using the fact that
\mathrm{Y}_{0}=\mathbf{z}^{\star}
, we get

\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{N}\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{-{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}(Z-\mathbf{z}_{\star})=\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}[\mathbf{M}_{\infty}]^{-1}\mathbf{M}_{k+1}\mathbf{J}(k)\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{N}\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{-{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\left({\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}/\sqrt{\boldsymbol{\rho}}}\mathbf{B}_{0}\theta-\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}\nabla V(\mathbf{z}^{\star})+\mathbf{D}_{2\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\eta_{k+1}}\right)\;.

(S137)

Recall that
\mathbf{P}_{0}=\mathbf{B}_{0}\bar{\mathbf{B}}_{0}^{-1}\mathbf{B}_{0}^{\top}
.
Hence, by (
S133
) and using
\mathbf{P}_{0}\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{\rho}}^{-{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\mathbf{z}^{\star}=\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{\rho}}^{-{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\mathbf{z}^{\star}
, we get

\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}/\sqrt{\boldsymbol{\rho}}}\mathbf{B}_{0}\theta-\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}\nabla V(\mathbf{z}^{\star})=\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}/\sqrt{\boldsymbol{\rho}}}\mathbf{B}_{0}\bar{\mathbf{B}}_{0}^{-{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\xi-\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}\nabla U(\mathbf{z}^{\star})\;.

Plugging this equality into (
S137
) yields

\displaystyle\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{N}\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{-{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}(Z-\mathbf{z}^{\star})

\displaystyle=-\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}[\mathbf{M}_{\infty}]^{-1}\mathbf{M}_{k+1}\mathbf{J}(k)\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}/\boldsymbol{N}}^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\nabla U(\mathbf{z}^{\star})

\displaystyle+\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}[\mathbf{M}_{\infty}]^{-1}\mathbf{M}_{k+1}\mathbf{J}(k)\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}/(\boldsymbol{N}\boldsymbol{\rho})}^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\mathbf{B}_{0}\bar{\mathbf{B}}_{0}^{-{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\xi

\displaystyle+\sqrt{2}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}[\mathbf{M}_{\infty}]^{-1}\mathbf{M}_{k+1}\mathbf{J}(k)\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{N}}^{-{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\eta_{k+1}\;.

(S138)

Recall that
[\mathbf{M}_{\infty}]^{-1}\mathbf{M}_{k+1}=(([\mathbf{M}_{\infty}]^{-1}\mathbf{M}_{k+1})^{1},\ldots,([\mathbf{M}_{\infty}]^{-1}\mathbf{M}_{k+1})^{b})
is a block-diagonal matrix where, for any
i\in[b]
,
([\mathbf{M}_{\infty}]^{-1}\mathbf{M}_{k+1})^{i}=\prod_{l=k+1}^{\infty}({\mathbf{I}_{d_{i}}-\mathbf{C}_{l}^{i}})
where
\mathbf{C}_{l}^{i}
is defined in (
S135
).
In addition, since we suppose for any
i\in[b]
, that
\gamma_{i}\tilde{M}_{i}<1
,
Lemma

S24
implies

\displaystyle\bigg\|{\prod_{l=k+1}^{N_{i}-1}(\mathbf{I}_{d_{i}}-\mathbf{C}^{i}_{l})}\bigg\|^{2}\leq\left({1-\gamma_{i}\tilde{m}_{i}}\right)^{2(N_{i}-k-1)}\;.

We now upper bound separately each term on the right-hand side of (
S138
).
First, using the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, we have

\displaystyle\mathinner{\!\left\lVert\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}[\mathbf{M}_{\infty}]^{-1}\mathbf{M}_{k+1}\mathbf{J}(k)\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}/\boldsymbol{N}}^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\right\rVert}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i=1}^{b}(\gamma_{i}/N_{i})\mathinner{\!\left\lVert\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}([\mathbf{M}_{\infty}]^{-1}\mathbf{M}_{k+1})^{i}\mathbf{J}^{i}(k)\right\rVert}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i=1}^{b}(\gamma_{i}/N_{i})\mathinner{\!\left\lVert\sum_{k=0}^{N_{i}-1}\prod_{l=k+1}^{N_{i}-1}\left({\mathbf{I}_{d_{i}}-\mathbf{C}^{i}_{l}}\right)\right\rVert}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i=1}^{b}\gamma_{i}\sum_{k=0}^{N_{i}-1}\mathinner{\!\left\lVert\prod_{l=k+1}^{N_{i}-1}\left({\mathbf{I}_{d_{i}}-\mathbf{C}^{i}_{l}}\right)\right\rVert}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i=1}^{b}\gamma_{i}\sum_{k=0}^{N_{i}-1}\left({1-\gamma_{i}\tilde{m}_{i}}\right)^{2(N_{i}-k-1)}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i=1}^{b}N_{i}\gamma_{i}\;.

(S139)

Second, using the same techniques as for the above inequality, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathinner{\!\left\lVert\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}[\mathbf{M}_{\infty}]^{-1}\mathbf{M}_{k+1}\mathbf{J}(k)\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}/(\boldsymbol{N}\boldsymbol{\rho})}^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\mathbf{B}_{0}\bar{\mathbf{B}}_{0}^{-{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\xi\right\rVert}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i=1}^{b}\frac{N_{i}\gamma_{i}}{\rho_{i}}\mathinner{\!\left\lVert\mathbf{B}_{0}\bar{\mathbf{B}}_{0}^{-{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\xi\right\rVert}^{2}

(S140)

Finally, the third term can be upper-bounded as

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathinner{\!\left\lVert\sqrt{2}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}[\mathbf{M}_{\infty}]^{-1}\mathbf{M}_{k+1}\mathbf{J}(k)\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{N}}^{-{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\eta_{k+1}\right\rVert}^{2}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq 2\sum_{i=1}^{b}d_{i}\;.

(S141)

Combining (
S138
), (
S139
), (
S140
) and (
S141
), we get

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{p}}\mathinner{\!\left\lVert\tilde{\mathbf{z}}-\mathbf{z}^{\star}\right\rVert}_{\mathbf{D}_{\boldsymbol{N}\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{-1}}^{2}P_{\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{\gamma},\boldsymbol{N}}^{\mathbf{z}}(\mathbf{z}^{\star},\mathrm{d}\tilde{\mathbf{z}})\leq\sum_{i=1}^{b}\gamma_{i}N_{i}\left({1+\mathrm{Tr}(\mathbf{P}_{0})/\rho_{i}}\right)+2\sum_{i=1}^{b}d_{i}\;.

∎

Given
\varepsilon>0
, we are now ready to provide a condition on the number of iterations
n_{\varepsilon}
to achieve
W_{2}(\mu^{\star}_{\boldsymbol{\rho}}P_{\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{\gamma},\boldsymbol{N}}^{n_{\varepsilon}},\Pi_{\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{\gamma}})\leq\varepsilon/3
in the case where for any
i\in[b]
,
m_{i}=m
,
M_{i}=M
,
\rho_{i}=\rho
,
\gamma_{i}=\gamma
and
N_{i}=N
.
Define

\mathsf{E}_{0}^{2}=9(1+\|\bar{\mathbf{B}}_{0}^{-1}\mathbf{B}_{0}^{\top}\mathbf{\tilde{D}}_{0}^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\|)2N\gamma\Bigg[\frac{2}{N(1-\kappa_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{2})}\bigg(\frac{1+\kappa_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{2}}{1-\kappa_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{2}}\cdot\gamma M^{2}\mathinner{\!\left\lVert\mathbf{u}^{\star}\right\rVert}^{2}\\
+(\gamma/\rho)\mathrm{Tr}(\mathbf{P}_{0})+2\sum_{i=1}^{b}d_{i}\bigg)+2b\gamma N\left({1+\mathrm{Tr}(\mathbf{P}_{0})/\rho}\right)+4\sum_{i=1}^{b}d_{i}\Bigg]\;.

Theorem S40
.

Assume
1
-
2
and assume that for any
i\in[b]
,
m_{i}=m
and
M_{i}=M
.
In addition, let
\boldsymbol{N}=N\mathbf{1}_{b},\boldsymbol{\gamma}=\gamma\mathbf{1}_{b},\boldsymbol{\rho}=\rho\mathbf{1}_{b}
,
\rho>0,\gamma>0,N\geq 1
, such that
\gamma<1/\tilde{M}
,
N\gamma<2/(m+\tilde{M})
, and (
S46
) is satisfied.
Then, for any
\varepsilon>0
, any

n_{\varepsilon}\geq 2\log\left({\mathsf{E}_{0}/\varepsilon}\right)/(N\gamma m),

we have,
W_{2}(\mu^{\star}_{\boldsymbol{\rho}}P_{\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{\gamma},\boldsymbol{N}}^{n_{\varepsilon}},\Pi_{\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{\gamma}})\leq\varepsilon/3
.

Proof.

By some algebra and using
1/\log(1/(1-x))\leq 1/x
for
0<x<1
, the proof directly follows from
Proposition

S14
combined with
Proposition

S38
and
Lemma

S39
.
∎

S5.2
Upper bound on the tolerance parameter
\boldsymbol{\rho}_{\varepsilon}

Define

\displaystyle R_{0}

\displaystyle=2\sigma^{2}_{U}\Big(d\sigma_{U}^{2}+\sum_{i=1}^{b}M_{i}^{2}\|\mathbf{A}_{i}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\star}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\star}_{i})\|^{2}\Big)+2\sigma^{4}_{U}\;,

\displaystyle R_{1}

\displaystyle=d\sigma_{U}^{2}+\sum_{i=1}^{b}M_{i}^{2}\|\mathbf{A}_{i}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\star}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\star}_{i})\|^{2}+\sum_{i=1}^{b}d_{i}M_{i}/2

\displaystyle R_{2}

\displaystyle=2d\max_{i\in[b]}\{M_{i}\}\sigma_{U}^{2}+2\sum_{i=1}^{b}M_{i}^{3}\|\mathbf{A}_{i}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\star}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\star}_{i})\|^{2}+8\sigma_{U}^{4}+8\sigma_{U}^{2}\Big[2d\sigma_{U}^{2}

\displaystyle+2\sum_{i=1}^{b}M_{i}^{2}\|\mathbf{A}_{i}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\star}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\star}_{i})\|^{2}\Big]\;.

Recall that
\bar{\rho}=\max_{i\in[b]}\{\rho_{i}\}
.
Then, the following result holds.

Lemma S41
.

Assume
1
-
2
.
For any
\varepsilon>0
, let
\boldsymbol{\rho}_{\varepsilon}\in(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{*})^{b}
such that

\displaystyle\bar{\rho}_{\varepsilon}\leq

\displaystyle\frac{-R_{1}+\sqrt{R_{1}^{2}+4R_{0}\varepsilon m_{U}^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}/(3\sqrt{2})}}{2R_{0}}\wedge\frac{\varepsilon\sqrt{m_{U}}}{3\sqrt{2}\sqrt{R_{2}+[R_{2}/(12\sigma_{U}^{2})+\sum_{i=1}^{b}d_{i}M_{i}]^{2}}}

\displaystyle\wedge\frac{1}{12\sigma_{U}^{2}}\;\wedge\frac{-\sum_{i=1}^{b}d_{i}M_{i}+\sqrt{(\sum_{i=1}^{b}d_{i}M_{i})^{2}+6R_{2}}}{2R_{2}}\;.

Then,
W_{2}(\pi_{\boldsymbol{\rho}_{\varepsilon}},\pi)\leq\varepsilon/3
.

Proof.

Let
\varepsilon>0
.
From (
S73
), for any
\bar{\rho}\leq 1/(12\sigma_{U}^{2})
,
W_{2}(\pi_{\boldsymbol{\rho}},\pi)\leq\sqrt{\frac{2}{m_{U}}}\max(A_{1},A_{3}^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}})\;
, where
A_{1},A_{3}
are defined in (
S69
) and (
S72
) respectively.
This implies that
W_{2}(\pi_{\boldsymbol{\rho}},\pi)\leq\varepsilon/3
is verified if
\max(A_{1},A_{3}^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}})\leq\varepsilon\sqrt{m_{U}}/(3\sqrt{2})
.
First,
A_{1}\leq\varepsilon\sqrt{m_{U}}/(3\sqrt{2})
holds if

\bar{\rho}\leq\frac{-R_{1}+\sqrt{R_{1}^{2}+4R_{0}\varepsilon m_{U}^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}/(3\sqrt{2})}}{2R_{0}}\wedge\frac{1}{12\sigma_{U}^{2}}\;.

(S142)

We now focus on
A_{3}
.
Using the fact that for any
x\in\mathbb{R},\mathrm{e}^{x}\geq x+1
, we have
2\prod_{i=1}^{b}(1+\rho_{i}M_{i})^{d_{i}}\geq 2+\sum_{i=1}^{b}d_{i}\log(1+\rho_{i}M_{i})
and therefore

\displaystyle A_{3}

\displaystyle\leq\exp\Big(\bar{\rho}^{2}R_{2}+\sum_{i=1}^{b}d_{i}\log(1+\rho_{i}M_{i})\Big)-1-\sum_{i=1}^{b}d_{i}\log(1+\rho_{i}M_{i})\;.

Since
\sum_{i=1}^{b}d_{i}\log(1+\rho_{i}M_{i})\leq\bar{\rho}\sum_{i=1}^{b}d_{i}M_{i}
,
\bar{\rho}^{2}R_{2}+\sum_{i=1}^{b}d_{i}\log(1+\rho_{i}M_{i})\leq 3/2
holds for

\bar{\rho}\leq\frac{-\sum_{i=1}^{b}d_{i}M_{i}+\sqrt{(\sum_{i=1}^{b}d_{i}M_{i})^{2}+6R_{2}}}{2R_{2}}\;.

(S143)

Since for any
x\leq 3/2,\mathrm{e}^{x}\leq 1+x+x^{2}
and using the fact that
\bar{\rho}\leq 1/(12\sigma_{U}^{2})
, it follows that

\displaystyle A_{3}\leq\bar{\rho}^{2}R_{2}+\Big(\bar{\rho}^{2}R_{2}+\bar{\rho}\sum_{i=1}^{b}d_{i}M_{i})\Big)^{2}\leq\bar{\rho}^{2}\left[{B_{1}+\Big(\frac{R_{2}}{12\sigma_{U}^{2}}+\sum_{i=1}^{b}d_{i}M_{i}\Big)^{2}}\right]\;.

Hence
A_{3}^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\leq\varepsilon\sqrt{m_{U}}/(3\sqrt{2})
holds under (
S143
) and

\bar{\rho}\leq\frac{\varepsilon\sqrt{m_{U}}}{3\sqrt{2}\sqrt{R_{2}+\Big(\frac{R_{2}}{12\sigma_{U}^{2}}+\sum_{i=1}^{b}d_{i}M_{i}\Big)^{2}}}\;.

(S144)

The proof is concluded by combining (
S142
), (
S143
) and (
S144
).
∎

S5.3
Upper bound on the step-size
\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}
and number of local iteration
\boldsymbol{N}_{\varepsilon}

Based on
Proposition

S29
or
Proposition

S33
, we now determine an upper bound on
\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}
to ensure
W_{2}(\Pi_{\boldsymbol{\rho}},\Pi_{\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}})\leq\varepsilon/3
in the case
\boldsymbol{N}=N\mathbf{1}_{b},\boldsymbol{\gamma}=\gamma\mathbf{1}_{b},\boldsymbol{\rho}=\rho\mathbf{1}_{b}
where
\rho>0,\gamma>0,N\geq 1
.
The following results hold depending if
3
is considered.
Define

\displaystyle C_{\rho}=\frac{4\tilde{M}^{2}(1+\|\bar{\mathbf{B}}_{0}^{-1}\mathbf{B}_{0}^{\top}\mathbf{\tilde{D}}_{0}^{{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\|^{2})}{5m}\;,

(S145)

\displaystyle C_{0}=(\tilde{M}^{2}/2)\left[{\tilde{M}/\tilde{m}+1/6}\right]\sum_{i=1}^{b}d_{i}\;,\quad C_{1}=\sum_{i=1}^{b}d_{i}\;,\quad C_{2}=\varepsilon^{2}/(9C_{\rho})\;.

Lemma S42
.

Assume
1
-
2
and assume for any
i\in[b]
,
m_{i}=m
and
M_{i}=M
.
In addition, let
\rho,\gamma_{\varepsilon}>0
and
N_{\varepsilon}\geq 1
such that
\boldsymbol{\rho}=\rho\mathbf{1}_{b}
,
\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}=\gamma_{\varepsilon}\mathbf{1}_{b}
,
\boldsymbol{N}_{\varepsilon}=N_{\varepsilon}\mathbf{1}_{b}
and
\varepsilon>0
satisfying

\displaystyle\gamma_{\varepsilon}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{-C_{1}+\sqrt{C_{1}^{2}+4C_{0}C_{2}}}{2C_{0}}\wedge\frac{m}{40\tilde{M}^{2}}\;.

(S146)

Then
W_{2}(\Pi_{\boldsymbol{\rho}},\Pi_{\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}})\leq\varepsilon/3
.

Proof.

Let
\varepsilon>0
.
By
Proposition

S29
, note that
W_{2}^{2}(\Pi_{\boldsymbol{\rho}},\Pi_{\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}})\leq\varepsilon^{2}/9
is satisfied if

C_{0}\gamma_{\varepsilon}^{2}+C_{1}\gamma_{\varepsilon}\leq C_{2}\;.

This inequality is satisfied under the choice (
S147
).
∎

We now provide a condition on
\boldsymbol{N}
and
\boldsymbol{\gamma}
when
3
is considered.

Lemma S43
.

Assume
1
-
2
and assume for any
i\in[b]
,
m_{i}=m
,
M_{i}=M
and
L_{i}=L
.
In addition, let
\rho,\gamma_{\varepsilon}>0
and
N_{\varepsilon}\geq 1
such that
\boldsymbol{\rho}=\rho\mathbf{1}_{b}
,
\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}=\gamma_{\varepsilon}\mathbf{1}_{b}
,
\boldsymbol{N}_{\varepsilon}=N_{\varepsilon}\mathbf{1}_{b}
and
\varepsilon>0
satisfying

\displaystyle\gamma_{\varepsilon}\leq

\displaystyle\frac{\varepsilon}{6b\sqrt{5\max_{i\in[b]}\{d_{i}\}C_{\rho}\tilde{M}^{2}[4+(\max_{i\in[b]}\{d_{i}\}L^{2}m)/(20\tilde{M}^{4})]}}\wedge\frac{m}{40\tilde{M}^{2}}

(S147)

\displaystyle\quad\quad\wedge\frac{\varepsilon}{6b(5C_{\rho}\max_{i\in[b]}\{d_{i}\}m^{3}/\tilde{M}^{2})}\;,

(S148)

where
C_{\rho}
is defined in (
S145
).
Then
W_{2}(\Pi_{\boldsymbol{\rho}},\Pi_{\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}})\leq\varepsilon/3
.

Proof.

In
Proposition

S33
, we dissociate
R^{\star}(\boldsymbol{\gamma})
into two contributions and the conditions we impose on
\gamma_{\varepsilon}
ensure
W_{2}(\Pi_{\boldsymbol{\rho}},\Pi_{\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}})\leq\varepsilon/3
.
More precisely, we have
\sum_{i=1}^{b}d_{i}\gamma_{i}^{2}\tilde{M}_{i}^{2}+\frac{d_{i}\gamma_{i}^{2}\mathfrak{f}_{i}}{\tilde{M}_{i}}(d_{i}L^{2}_{i}+\frac{\tilde{M}_{i}^{4}}{\tilde{m}_{i}})\leq 2\varepsilon^{2}/9
and
\sum_{i=1}^{b}d_{i}\gamma_{i}\tilde{M}_{i}\mathfrak{f}_{i}^{3}(1+\mathfrak{f}_{i}+\mathfrak{f}_{i}^{2})\leq 2\varepsilon^{2}/9
where
\mathfrak{f}_{i}<1
for any
i\in[b]
.
∎

S5.4
Discussion

Let
\boldsymbol{\rho}_{\varepsilon}=\rho_{\varepsilon}\mathbf{1}_{b}
such that
W_{2}(\pi_{\boldsymbol{\rho}_{\varepsilon}},\pi)\leq\varepsilon/3
.
From
Lemma

S41
,
\rho_{\varepsilon}=\mathcal{O}(\varepsilon/d)
when
\varepsilon\rightarrow 0
and
d\rightarrow\infty
.
Similarly, let
\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}=\gamma_{\varepsilon}\mathbf{1}_{b}
such that
W_{2}(\Pi_{\boldsymbol{\rho}_{\varepsilon}},\Pi_{\boldsymbol{\rho}_{\varepsilon},\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}})<\varepsilon/3
.
Under
1
-
2
, we obtain by
Lemma

S42

\gamma_{\varepsilon}=\mathcal{O}(\varepsilon^{4}/d^{3})
.
On the other hand, when
3
is additionally assumed, we get by
Lemma

S43

\gamma_{\varepsilon}=\mathcal{O}(\varepsilon^{2}/d^{2})
.
Finally, to apply
Theorem

S40
for the previous choices
\gamma_{\varepsilon}
and
\rho_{\varepsilon}
, we obtain for
\boldsymbol{N}_{\varepsilon}=N_{\varepsilon}\mathbf{1}_{b}
the conditions
N_{\varepsilon}=\mathcal{O}(d/\varepsilon^{2})
and
N_{\varepsilon}=\mathcal{O}(1)
under
1
-
2
and
1
-
2
-
3
, respectively.
In both scenarios,
Theorem

S40
implies
n_{\varepsilon}=\mathcal{O}(d^{2}\log(d)/(\varepsilon^{2}|\log(\varepsilon)|)
.
This concludes the results depicted in Table 1 in the main paper.
